The final PostgreSQL gate also confirms that a role-required first-binding ticket cannot finish after rule withdrawal or revive after reenforcement. Both Compose variants validate offline with disposable values; JavaScript syntax and `git diff --check` pass. TypeScript has exactly the same 17 unrelated diagnostics with no additions. Owned listeners/clusters and isolated browser build/config files are cleaned up; no production migration, container build or deployment is performed.

An additional unchanged legacy credential-service gate initially passed 313 of 314 tests: its password-reset policy characterization returned an invalid-token error before the expected password-policy rejection. The isolated 29-test file and then the full 314-test gate pass without source or assertion changes. This transient result does not establish complete legacy credential/provisioning consolidation; no unrelated password-reset change is included.

## Twenty-Sixth Implemented Increment: Durable Microsoft License Fulfillment

Reviewed local entitlements can now drive an explicitly enabled Microsoft Graph worker. Local seat allocation, desired external state, a dispatched request and observed external assignment remain different states. No live Microsoft tenant, consent, credential, user, SKU purchase or production deployment is created by this work.

### Explicit authority and immutable mappings

- `license-provider-administrator` reads known provider records, configures bindings and explicitly adopts a reviewed direct assignment. It cannot allocate local seats or retry uncertain writes by that role alone. `license-fulfillment-operator` reads and reviews retries, but cannot configure or adopt. `license-fulfillment-reader` reads only known license/user status. Ordinary `license-manager` and `license-administrator` roles acquire none of these new duties implicitly; holding explicitly granted roles composes their capabilities. Global administration retains its broad authority, so this is duty separation, not a mandatory two-human approval workflow.
- The two write duties are protected canonical roles, initially requiring MFA with a five-minute original authentication age across clients. Every configuration/review write independently requires a canonical human, matching live license identity, bound issuer-verified password-plus-TOTP proof no older than five minutes and reviewed 10–500-character reason. Changes to existing records and target reviews additionally require the exact record version; creation uses server-generated identifiers and immutable uniqueness constraints instead. Live issuer/session/client/factor/canonical actor authority is checked again after lock waits and before committing configuration/review evidence; revocation during an admitted SQL wait cannot rely on its earlier positive proof. Provider flags, raw scopes, workload tokens, alias selectors and an ordinary license-administrator token cannot substitute for that authority. Existing first binding supports these initially MFA-required roles without a provisional application session.
- Connectors bind one immutable tenant UUID. A product binds one local user-license pool to one SKU and sorted unique disabled-plan UUIDs. A subject binds one canonical local user UUID to one immutable provider object UUID. No email/UPN guessing, automatic account creation, mutable tenant/object/SKU selectors, arbitrary Graph URL or secret in an API body is accepted. A tenant cannot be duplicated under another connector, a provider object cannot alias two local users in that connector, and one connector/SKU cannot be split into competing local pools.
- Every binding starts paused. Product and subject creation require explicit exclusive-direct-management acknowledgement; the immutable actor/reason/configuration evidence is committed transactionally. Only conditional enablement changes are allowed afterward. Pausing stops new work, not already accepted remote effects, and does **not** mean license withdrawal. Bindings and evidence are retained; mapped pools cannot be hard-deleted through the old catalog cascade. Rebinding/retirement needs a separately reviewed migration rather than silent cleanup of uncertain remote ownership.

### Transactional intent, current eligibility and observed ownership

Policy migration `009_license_fulfillment.sql` attaches durable desired-state changes to effective user-assignment and eligibility/configuration changes in the same transaction. A missing queue/audit source cannot acknowledge an allocation or configuration that lost its provisioning work. Overlapping direct/group sources still consume one local seat and produce one effective provider target; removing only one source does not remove a still-required external assignment. Device assignments and mere pending requests/eligibility do not become user-license jobs.

New local user assignments capture their authoritative eligibility incarnation as an immutable origin. Existing assignments are deliberately **not** retroactively stamped by the migration. The provider worker checkpoints current canonical revision/enabled/deleted state through the minimal directory source before inspection and again before dispatch; it cannot use a saved positive after an outage. A missed disable/reactivate cycle cannot authorize an old-incarnation external grant. Expiry is reconsidered without depending on the local lifecycle timer, including inside dispatch/review transactions. Legacy/null or changed eligibility origins require independently withdrawing and freshly authorizing the local entitlement, not modifying its stored revision. The worker changes only provider projections: local records/seats still need their separate authorized lifecycle/review workflow.

Each target is serialized with a real database-session advisory lock, not just a process-local flag. A durable immutable `write-intent` precedes the remote request. Crash/receipt/audit loss after that point is conservatively unknown; a fresh process does not assume it was never sent. Desired generations cannot regress. A receipt for a superseded version cannot confirm the newer desired state; a known owned add that raced with local withdrawal is reconciled toward withdrawal afterward. Accepted remote effects are not cross-database atomic and cannot be recalled by aborting the caller.

The Graph adapter uses only `GET /v1.0/users/{objectId}` with a bounded `$select` and `POST /v1.0/users/{objectId}/assignLicense` with exactly one configured SKU. No request can forward browser credentials or choose a third-party endpoint. A successful mutation receipt is merely `verifying`; a separate current observation must show the expected direct SKU/plan state before confirmation. Malformed/partial/oversized replies, HTTP 202/207, wrong object IDs and ambiguous transport/server failures do not become success. Graph assignment errors become explicit failed observations, not endless successful provisioning. Confirmation concerns the observed license-assignment state, **not** every downstream mailbox/service plan, federated session or Microsoft workload's eventual activation. See Microsoft's [assignment API](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/user-assignlicense?view=graph-rest-1.0) and [assignment-state model](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/resources/licenseassignmentstate?view=graph-rest-1.0).

Existing foreign direct assignments, group-only licenses and changed plan configuration are observed without automatic takeover/overwrite. A protected operator can explicitly adopt a recent matching direct assignment only against the current desired version and fresh canonical source. Unknown add outcomes retain their irreversible attempt origin: even after local withdrawal or canonical disable/delete, a separately reviewed cleanup adoption can authorize removing a matching direct assignment tied to that earlier attempt. This is an explicit human ownership declaration, not cryptographic proof of an external administrator's actions. Ordinary foreign licenses without that authorization/history cannot be adopted merely to remove them. Group-inherited access is never removed by editing group membership; remaining external group access is shown as a conflict rather than falsely called fully revoked.

Unknown results never trigger an automatic repeated write. Explicit retry requires its separate duty, fresh MFA, reason, exact generation and uncertainty acknowledgement. It creates queued work, not completion or rollback. Known request failures do not gain broader credentials. HTTP 429 uses a bounded, durable connector-wide `Retry-After` pause across worker instances; repeated mutation attempts are bounded and eventually require review. Stopping during inspection prevents follow-up writes. Already dispatched requests may still settle; retaining old uncertainty evidence makes late effects visible for renewed cleanup review rather than erasing their origin. Microsoft documents the [throttling/retry contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/throttling); these controls do not establish exactly-once external delivery.

### Separate machine identity and private execution

The issuer now maps only the explicitly registered application scope `licenses.users.read` to that single workload permission. It emits no human role or fabricated MFA. The direct directory endpoint still verifies issuer, audience, access purpose, persisted session and current client generation before returning only the selected canonical user's revision/enabled/deleted snapshot. The scope must be explicitly granted to a protected OAuth client with `client_credentials`; arbitrary scope text at a receiver or any other unknown/admin scope is not a permission translator. Workload disable/rotation/generation changes fence the old read bearer, and the Graph worker cannot use an old positive or ambient human/admin credential afterward.

`scripts/license-fulfillment-worker.js` is a separate process, not started by policy-service HTTP startup. It refuses an absent, symlinked, non-owner/group-readable or oversized configuration file, implicit database connection, administrative/assumed-role database identity, schema/database creation privileges, role memberships or local entitlement/configuration write privileges. It has no listener. Both Compose variants provide the **off-by-default** `license-fulfillment` profile, read-only root/configuration mounts, no published ports, dropped capabilities and an independently specified `LICENSE_FULFILLMENT_DATABASE_URL`. The web service never receives that private configuration mount.

Provision a dedicated nonadministrative login with no role memberships and no local license/catalog/subject/request or provider-binding writes. After the owner applies migrations, grant only the columns/tables/functions listed in `services/core/policy-service/scripts/license-fulfillment-grants.sql` using reviewed `worker_role` and `license_schema` psql identifiers. The helper neither creates credentials nor strips an existing role's broader rights; startup rejects that broader role instead. Native tests connect as an actual separately granted login and verify both successful fulfillment and denied local-right/configuration writes. This is not per-connector database RLS, automatic secret provisioning or protection against a database owner rewriting evidence.

Use a separate Microsoft application with reviewed application permissions: `LicenseAssignment.ReadWrite.All` for the assignment operation and `User.Read.All` for the selected user's assignment-state read. Do not replace them with directory/user write-all permissions for convenience. These Graph application grants are tenant-wide permissions, not an object-scoped Microsoft security boundary. The adapter's configured target allowlist constrains this implementation, not a stolen credential used elsewhere. See Microsoft's [operation permissions](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/user-assignlicense?view=graph-rest-1.0) and [user-read permissions](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/user-get?view=graph-rest-1.0). `/licenseDetails` is not used as an app-only shortcut: its current documentation does not support application permissions. [Microsoft reference](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/user-list-licensedetails?view=graph-rest-1.0).

The owner-only worker file contains exactly these fields; the disabled template intentionally fails startup until an operator independently provisions and enables it:

```json
{
  "enabled": false,
  "connectorId": "11111111-1111-4111-8111-111111111111",
  "tenantId": "22222222-2222-4222-8222-222222222222",
  "graphClientId": "33333333-3333-4333-8333-333333333333",
  "graphClientSecret": "REPLACE-WITH-PRIVATE-GRAPH-SECRET",
  "sourceClientId": "REPLACE-WITH-REVIEWED-LOCAL-CLIENT",
  "sourceClientSecret": "REPLACE-WITH-PRIVATE-LOCAL-SECRET",
  "sourceTokenUrl": "http://oauth-provider:3010/oauth/token",
  "identityUrl": "http://identity-service:3001/"
}
```

Graph credentials go only to the fixed tenant-specific Microsoft token endpoint, using the documented [client-credentials flow](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity-platform/v2-oauth2-client-creds-grant-flow) and `https://graph.microsoft.com/.default`. The local read client uses only `licenses.users.read`. Tokens are cached only in worker memory until their validated expiry minus 30 seconds, not database/browser state; accepted token lifetimes range from 60 to 86,400 seconds. Raw provider bodies, credential/token values and provider messages are not stored in audit. Independently provision matching file ownership for `LICENSE_FULFILLMENT_UID/GID` and mode 0400/0600; no environment fallback for Graph/local-OIDC client secrets or key-generation endpoint exists. The separate database connection is explicitly supplied through `DATABASE_URL` (Compose: `LICENSE_FULFILLMENT_DATABASE_URL`). Private local endpoints must use protected transport/network trust; global-cloud Graph URLs cannot be replaced with arbitrary or national-cloud URLs through this file. Certificate/federated/KMS credentials and automated rotation remain future work.

### API, console and rollout

All routes below are under `/api/licenses/fulfillment`, use canonical live bearer authorization and `no-store`, and reject unknown body/query fields:

| Operation | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `POST /connectors` | `tenantId`, `reason`; disabled initial connector |
| `POST /products` | `connectorId`, `licenseId`, `skuId`, sorted `disabledPlans`, `exclusiveDirectManagement:true`, `reason` |
| `POST /subjects` | `connectorId`, canonical `userId`, provider `objectId`, `exclusiveDirectManagement:true`, `reason`; active canonical target required |
| `GET /{connectors,products,subjects}/:id` | Known binding metadata only; no credentials or inventory search |
| `PUT /{connectors,products,subjects}/:id` | Only boolean `enabled` and `reason`; exact `If-Match: "od-fulfillment:<kind>:<id>:<version>"` |
| `GET /targets/:licenseId/:userId` | Explicit known UUID pair; local assignment, filtered external desired state, pause/ownership/error/observation/check time and actual retry/adoption capability |
| `POST /targets/:id/retry` | Reason, boolean `acknowledgeUncertainOutcome` (must be true for unknown outcomes), exact target ETag |
| `POST /targets/:id/adopt` | Reason, `acknowledgeExclusiveManagement:true`, exact target ETag and a current permitted fulfillment/cleanup ownership review |

The actual license console adds a separate known-ID status panel. It does not need directory profiles or local seat-management authority, does not load credentials, distinguishes paused/unknown/failed/verifying/last-observed confirmation, and requires explicit reason/acknowledgement for its capability-gated review dialog. A changed actor/target/capability closes stale confirmation. Lost/partial/unchanged/mismatched receipts cannot become success or automatic retry. Even a successful adoption/retry response explicitly says only that a review decision was queued, not that Microsoft provisioned or revoked anything.

Apply **policy migration 009** and **identity migration 011**, ship the coordinated issuer/directory/policy/web images, provision the separate role/DB/client/file authorities, and review canonical user/application assignments. No legacy assignment automatically acquires a fresh eligibility origin. Configure disabled mappings through the reviewed API, validate the connector in an isolated Microsoft test tenant, independently enable the reviewed mappings/configuration and only then start the explicit worker profile. This work performs none of that live acceptance/deployment. Do not infer provider purchase capacity from local `total_seats`, and do not use a local allocation, queued review or an HTTP-200 mutation alone as evidence of external service access.

Native PostgreSQL tests cover atomic intent, overlap, concurrency/real advisory locks, actual restricted database credentials, actor/retry/adoption separation, source outages/incarnations, expiry/review races, audit loss before/after real HTTP effects, worker restart, superseded receipts, provider failures, throttling, immutable evidence and reviewed uncertain cleanup. Actual issuer/directory/policy HTTP gates exercise the minimal machine identity, source-client nonresurrection and separate real-MFA human configuration through to observed external fixture assignment/removal. Actual Chrome/Next/issuer/directory/policy/PostgreSQL tests exercise protected own-factor bootstrap, different local/configuration/read duties, no profile requests for the provider reader, explicit adoption, queued-not-fulfilled UI, later observation and eventual observed withdrawal. The provider endpoints are owned HTTP protocol fixtures, **not Microsoft test-tenant certification**.

Current targeted gates pass **2,474 tests**: 397 issuer, 218 identity, 94 gateway, 390 policy, 32 license adapter, 393 quick-actions and 581 frontend unit/API tests; 57 identity, 104 license and 136 issuer/receiver PostgreSQL tests; 39 browser-mediation core, 28 actual Chrome/Next/issuer/directory/policy tests and five fixture-backed layout tests. The license gate starts and cleanly stops the actual worker CLI using an independently granted database login and private configuration, without vendor access. The HTTP gate independently revokes a real-MFA actor's canonical role while an admitted binding update is blocked inside its target-refresh trigger; final authorization rolls back both configuration and target state. The provider-status screen is visually checked. Both Compose variants validate offline, JavaScript syntax and `git diff --check` pass, and TypeScript retains exactly the same 17 unrelated diagnostics. Owned listeners/clusters and isolated browser/configuration files are cleaned up; no production migration, image build, deployment or live Microsoft operation is performed.

One concurrent full policy run reported two unrelated legacy API failures (a missing-type response and a blueprint validation timeout); the unchanged full 390-test gate subsequently passes. No legacy validation assertion or production behavior is weakened to obtain that result.

## Twenty-Seventh Implemented Increment: Mandatory Privileged Activation Policies

PIM can now be a binding canonical role rule, not merely an alternative to an unrestricted direct grant. The design follows the distinction between assignment and bounded, independently reviewed activation, not Microsoft's API or licensing implementation. See Microsoft's [PIM role settings](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/id-governance/privileged-identity-management/pim-how-to-change-default-settings). No production policy, administrator grant or user session is changed by this development run.

### Separate policy duties and explicit changes

- `privileged-access-policy-reader` reads a known role's policy. Protected `privileged-access-policy-administrator` reads and changes unprotected role policies, with immutable five-minute original MFA assurance. Neither role receives profile, active-role, eligibility, approval or license-management permissions. Existing PIM and role administrators do not acquire these policy duties implicitly. Protected role changes require global administration with the same fresh issuer-bound password/TOTP proof.
- A policy has an immutable catalog role, monotonic version, `required` flag, 60–28,800-second maximum PIM duration and an explicit bounded list of canonical `reviewerUserIds`. The baseline `user` policy is fixed. Policies initially preserve direct-grant compatibility; mandatory enforcement is reviewed opt-in, not a universal secure-default claim.
- Changes require an exact ETag, a 10–500-character reviewed reason and `acknowledgeInvalidation:true`. Unknown fields, workload/alias claims, weak/fabricated MFA, stale versions and unavailable authority cannot authorize them. The model checks current canonical actor/roles after its actual policy lock and checks the original bearer at the live issuer before DML and again after evidence creation, before commit. A role or session revoked during an admitted SQL wait cannot use the earlier positive admission.
- Policy changes and immutable previous/current/actor/reason evidence share the same transaction. Missing evidence or final live-authority failure rolls back the new version and its effective-right changes. The policy and audit cannot be deleted; record versions and captured assignment/request origins cannot be rewritten. Database-owner tampering and off-host evidence retention remain separate concerns.

### Effective rights, not an extra active label

Identity migration `012_privileged_access_policy.sql` adds immutable policy origins to canonical assignments and PIM requests. Initial compatibility policies and existing origins start at version one. Every subsequent policy change invalidates **all** earlier direct grants, activated PIM grants and pending/approved requests for that role, including when only the duration changes or direct grants are allowed again. No timestamp rewrite or silent restamping restores them. Historical records remain visible; the role console labels affected grants `policy_changed`, not active or falsely user-revoked.

The live role source returns only current-origin, permitted, unexpired grants. Missing or malformed policy metadata fails closed. Effective-source filtering precedes the 100-grant bound, so retained old evidence cannot exhaust the active bound or hide a newly reviewed grant. Policy origins/current versions participate in the held-role fingerprint. Issuer access, refresh and pending code/device issuance, core gateway, direct directory/policy and delegated quick-actions use that same live authority even when a token omits role claims. An old grant or credential cannot revive after a later relaxation. Already accepted effects are not rolled back; withdrawing an administrator role is not automatic withdrawal of a user's separately approved local/vendor license.

Direct role APIs, the regular role catalog and the database assignment trigger honor mandatory activation. Global administration and the offline bootstrap script are not exceptions. Current PIM requests and assignments must use the current target-role version and configured maximum as well as their existing eligibility lifetime; owner activation still requires independently verified approval and fresh actual MFA. Policy changes stale earlier approvals/leases rather than extending them. Request lists and activation controls expose that state instead of retrying an old approval as a new grant.

### An independent path for global administration

Requiring PIM for every existing global administrator must not remove the only authority able to approve its replacement. Mandatory `admin` therefore requires at least one explicitly named, different canonical operator with a current, unaffected `pim-approver` duty, plus a currently enabled eligible `admin` user outside the named reviewer list whose eligibility lasts at least another minute. These conditions are checked transactionally before the change. No eligibility, reviewer role, account, factor or recovery bypass is created automatically.

For a protected mandatory role, a named current PIM approver may review an independent owner's request; an unrelated PIM approver cannot. Global administrators retain their existing independent-review authority while they actually hold that role. Protected review/revocation requires original password/TOTP assurance no older than five minutes. The recorded decision includes only the verified review authentication time and factor revision, not an OTP, seed or bearer. Activation rechecks canonical reviewer identity/link/roles and designation and also bounds the original review age; refresh cannot rejuvenate either reviewer or owner proof. The approval's displayed deadline is no later than either its five-minute decision window or original review-age deadline.

Before enabling a global rule, independently provision the eligible account, reviewer duty, normal application assignments, usable credentials/factors and controlled database-owner recovery. The minimum path check is not a perpetual lockout guarantee: later disablement, expiry, deleted application access or unavailable authority must still deny activation. A valid recorded approval is not a continuing reviewer session; later individual-token/session/factor revocation alone is not separately rechecked as a reviewer session at activation. Canonical user/link/role changes are rechecked. Purpose-bound reviewer-session/factor recall, notifications and independently monitored emergency access remain further work, not a reason to accept a weaker active token.

An independently approved offline recovery must use the explicitly selected identity database/schema and its separately controlled owner, not a browser endpoint or fulfillment-worker credential. Preserve the current policy/evidence, lock the known `admin` policy, set reviewed transaction-local `opendirectory.actor` and `opendirectory.activation_reason`, and conditionally change only the reviewed version to `activation_required=false`, `reviewer_user_ids='{}'`, `version=version+1`. Require exactly one affected row and a committed immutable audit record. Earlier grants remain ineffective. Only then use `scripts/bootstrap-role.js USER_UUID admin SECONDS "reviewed reason"` from identity-service with an explicitly bounded lifetime, normal application assignment and real credentials/factor; a new normal login is still required. Prepare and verify the independent eligibility/review path before reenforcing the rule, which invalidates the recovery grant as well. None of these owner actions is performed automatically or exercised against production here.

### API, console and rollout

| Operation | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/identity/pim/policies/:role` | Known catalog role; nine-field policy DTO, no profiles; canonical read duty and `no-store` |
| `PUT /api/identity/pim/policies/:role` | `required`, `maxDurationSeconds`, `reason`, `acknowledgeInvalidation:true`; optional `reviewerUserIds` defaults to `[]`, but is required/nonempty for mandatory global administration |
| Conditional write | `If-Match: "od-role-activation:<role>:<version>"`; exact next-version receipt |

Reviewer IDs must be lowercase canonical UUIDs, sorted and unique, at most 20. A nonempty designation is accepted only for a protected mandatory role. The known-role console sorts entered IDs but does not guess usernames, fetch profiles or choose reviewers automatically. It exposes read-only/fresh-login/protected states and separately confirms the target, duration, reason, reviewer list, withdrawal of previous grants/requests and recovery acknowledgement. Actor/target/capability changes discard stale confirmation; lost, partial, unchanged or mismatched receipts cannot become success or blind retry. PIM self-service shows and honors the stricter current duration rather than advertising the eligibility's larger maximum alone.

Apply identity migration **012** and ship coordinated directory/web/shared-contract consumers. Existing canonical humans holding roles must reauthenticate because origin-aware role fingerprints change; old refresh/pending snapshots cannot silently migrate to new authority. The migration itself does not enable mandatory policies or delete historical grants. Review each role deliberately, prepare the independent activation path before changing global administration, and verify actual receiving services. No production migration, image build, deployment, vendor operation or automatic emergency grant is performed here. Tenant/resource scoping, mandatory policy defaults, periodic access reviews, recovery delivery and full legacy receiver consolidation remain open.

Current targeted gates pass **2,541 tests**: 397 issuer, 246 identity, 94 gateway, 390 policy, 32 license adapter, 393 quick-actions and 602 frontend unit/API tests; 71 identity, 104 license and 139 issuer/receiver PostgreSQL tests; 39 browser-mediation core, 29 actual Chrome/Next/issuer/directory/policy tests and five fixture-backed layout tests. Native tests cover immutable policy origins, atomic evidence/final-authority rollback, real competing policy/grant locks, retained stale evidence, global/offline non-bypass, named reviewer separation and a viable current global-admin lease. Actual issuer HTTP tests use real TOTP, separate duties, cross-client/omitted-scope revocation, pending code/device denial, no unauthorized seat mutation, current bounded PIM licensing and actual global-admin activation after every earlier admin grant is invalidated. A real issuer revocation while policy audit DML is blocked rolls the policy and audit back. The actual browser changes the rule with explicit acknowledgement, loses the old affected session and gives an independent policy reader neither profile requests nor write controls; its screen is visually checked. Both Compose variants validate offline; syntax, relative imports and `git diff --check` pass. TypeScript has the same 17 unrelated diagnostics. Owned listeners, native clusters, private fixture files and isolated browser configuration/build paths are cleaned up; no production or live Microsoft acceptance is claimed.

## Twenty-Eighth Implemented Increment: Recurring Independent Role Access Reviews

Canonical active role assignments and PIM eligibilities can now require periodic independent recertification. This addresses continued need for an already authorized entitlement, not creation of another active-label database or automatic renewal of a grant. Microsoft's [access-review guidance](https://learn.microsoft.com/entra/id-governance/manage-access-review) distinguishes role reviews from group/application reviews; this implementation uses its own deliberately narrower canonical-role contracts, not Microsoft API compatibility or a deployed Entra integration.

### Review duties, scoped records and real decisions

- Protected `access-review-administrator` configures known role policies and reads known review metadata. Protected `access-reviewer` reads only assigned reviews and makes decisions. `access-review-reader` reads known policy/review metadata without decisions or configuration. Neither delegated write duty receives role assignment, PIM approval, directory profile or license-allocation authority implicitly. Both write duties have immutable five-minute original MFA minima; weak/fabricated proof or a workload principal cannot substitute for an issuer-verified canonical human.
- A policy names one canonical catalog role, immutable interval/window and sorted unique reviewer UUIDs (1–20). The interval is 3,600–31,536,000 seconds; the window is 60–604,800 seconds and shorter than the interval. Protected roles require current global administration to configure. Creation explicitly acknowledges the immediate initial deadline and its withdrawal/recovery consequences. Each named reviewer must currently have a linked enabled canonical identity and a current voting duty. A sole reviewer cannot be the beneficiary of a covered grant; future grants with that impossible self-review path are rejected too.
- The policy captures both existing current grants and future grants through actual SQL triggers, serialized with a per-role transaction lock. Grant/eligibility UUIDs are immutable targets, not usernames, requested claims, client scopes or replaceable row positions. Original base expiry, role-activation policy origins and ordinary PIM approval/fresh-factor requirements remain authoritative. Creation/retirement/decision intent and actor/reason/proof evidence commit with the actual mutation; missing audit or final live-session authority rolls everything back.
- A reviewer must be named, currently authorized, distinct from the canonical beneficiary and freshly authenticated with verified password/TOTP. Global administration is not a self-review or unnamed-reviewer exception. `retain` advances only the review lease to the next scheduled window; it neither creates a role nor extends the underlying grant/eligibility lifetime. `revoke` updates the actual canonical assignment or eligibility, using the normal role/PIM audit and generation triggers. Eligibility withdrawal also revokes its derived activations and cancels pending requests. Other independent grants can still provide the same role; the console does not claim otherwise.

### Fail-closed deadlines without a privileged scheduler

Identity migration `013_role_access_reviews.sql` adds immutable policy history, target-bound review leases and append-only review evidence. A current lease supplies a due time and cutoff. Before the due time the next cycle is scheduled; during the window it is pending; after the cutoff it is ineffective without waiting for a background job, queue delivery or browser. The live canonical role query excludes deadline-expired assignments and also excludes PIM assignments whose reviewed eligibility expired. Active and eligible PIM projections and admission guards use the same cutoff. `review_expired` is visible in the role console; expiry does not pretend that an operator set `revoked_at`.

Review lease identity/version participates in the canonical held-role fingerprint, including a PIM assignment's reviewed-eligibility origin. Attaching, replacing or confirming a lease invalidates earlier affected access/refresh/pending snapshots, even if the visible role name is unchanged or omitted from token scope. A continuing grant needs a new normal login after that authority change. The issuer, core gateway, direct identity/policy APIs and delegated quick-actions consume this same live source. Existing user-license seats are not silently withdrawn just because their administrator lost management authority; actual recipient entitlement review is a separate next step.

No unanswered review is automatically approved. A cutoff cannot be extended by a late vote, policy retirement or later policy replacement. Retained expired history is not silently restamped. New independently authorized grants get new source IDs and fresh review bindings when the role still has an active policy. Effective-role bounds ignore expired review history, and the indexed assigned-review queue prioritizes actual actionable grants rather than allowing obsolete/deleted-user history to hide a later open review behind its 100-row page.

### Retirement, replacement and conditional scope

Policy settings are immutable. An operator can only retire a policy with an exact version and reviewed acknowledgement; changing reviewers or timing requires retirement and a new policy incarnation. New bindings stop, but accepted deadlines remain binding. A timely independent confirmation of an already retired policy can explicitly close recurrence while preserving only the original base grant lifetime. A missed cutoff still cannot be revived. A new policy can rebind only an effective, unexpired lease, without extending its earlier deadline; it cannot reuse a denied or expired grant.

Decisions bind **both** review-lease and policy versions. Retirement therefore invalidates a form captured while recurrence was active instead of silently turning its retain into an exemption. The operator must read the current retired state and explicitly acknowledge that different consequence. Lost/partial/malformed/mismatched receipts are not success; no mutation is automatically replayed. Actor/target/capability changes discard stale confirmations and ignore late success under another actor.

Policies persist as recorded governance intent after the configuring session ends. Reviewer identity/link/roles and original live bearer/factor authority are checked at decision time, including after actual lock waits and after audit DML before commit. No broadly privileged background JWT, database worker or automatic recovery grant is introduced. The trusted database clock is authoritative; there is no request/body/header/environment clock override. Later service outages fail closed at the existing live authority, not through a saved positive decision.

### API and console

All operations are under `/api/identity/reviews`, use canonical live bearer authorization and `no-store`, reject unknown selectors/body fields and have no demo-memory fallback:

| Operation | Contract |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /policies/:role` | Known catalog role; latest active/retired policy and actual create/fresh-MFA capabilities |
| `POST /policies` | `role`, `intervalSeconds`, `windowSeconds`, sorted canonical `reviewerUserIds`, `reason`, `acknowledgeDeadlines:true`; server-generated immutable policy ID |
| `POST /policies/:id/retire` | `reason`, `acknowledgeDeadlines:true`; `If-Match: "od-role-review-policy:<id>:<version>"` |
| `GET /self` | Current human's assigned reviews only; bounded to 100 with explicit truncation and actionable-first ordering |
| `GET /:id` | Known review; assigned reviewer or separate minimal metadata-reader duty, not arbitrary user/role inventory |
| `POST /:id/decisions` | `decision:"retain"\|"revoke"`, `reason`, `acknowledgeScope:true`; `If-Match: "od-role-review:<id>:<leaseVersion>:<policyVersion>"` |

The real Roles console presents policy configuration separately from the assigned-review queue and a known-ID reader. It needs no profile/role-list authority, has no prototype counters, and requires a fresh explicit confirmation of target, deadline consequences, reason and independent scope. Scheduled/expired/denied/obsolete/exempt states are not represented as a newly granted role. The base expiry remains visible, and a protected or read-only response cannot acquire write controls from stale client capability data.

### Rollout and remaining license work

Apply identity migration **013** and coordinated directory/shared-contract/web images. Policies start absent: deployment alone does not enable destructive deadlines or change existing unreviewed fingerprints. Provision named voting duties and real factors, normal application assignments and a separately controlled recovery path before enabling a policy, especially for global administration or review staff themselves. Creating the first lease changes affected source fingerprints and requires normal reauthentication. Notifications, off-host evidence retention and purpose-bound reviewer-session recall remain separate work, not implemented delivery guarantees.

For an independently approved database-owner recovery, explicitly select the identity database/schema, preserve policy/evidence, set reviewed transaction-local `opendirectory.actor` and `opendirectory.review_reason`, lock and conditionally retire the exact active policy/version. Do not rewrite/delete an expired lease. If mandatory PIM also prevents the required fresh grant, use the separate reviewed activation-policy recovery described above. Only then bootstrap a **new**, explicitly bounded canonical grant and complete normal real-factor/application login; prepare independent review before reenabling a new policy. Retirement alone never restores a timed-out grant. No owner recovery or production migration is performed here.

This first review plane governs who can administer licenses, not whether a recipient should retain a particular product seat. Existing direct/group license sources have composite reusable keys rather than immutable grant incarnations. The next license-review step must add source incarnations and semantic group-rule versions, capture reviewed sources exactly, preserve overlapping entitlements/capacity, stop denied group rules from silently readding rights, and enqueue provider withdrawals in the same transaction. A review label or deletion of one effective user row would not satisfy that requirement. Application/group/resource/tenant-scoped reviews, usage-driven license recommendations and purchased capacity/cost reconciliation remain open; the full Entra-like objective is not complete.

Current targeted gates pass **2,602 tests**: 397 issuer, 270 identity, 94 gateway, 390 policy, 32 license adapter, 393 quick-actions and 619 frontend unit/API tests; 87 identity, 104 license and 142 issuer/receiver PostgreSQL tests; 39 browser-mediation core, 30 actual Chrome/Next/issuer/directory/policy tests and five fixture-backed layout tests. Native tests cover recurring windows, no early/late votes, real canonical withdrawal, eligibility-derived PIM loss, retirement/replacement nonresurrection, policy-version fencing, bounded-queue starvation, immutable evidence, current actor revocation after a real mutex wait and rollback after final authority/audit failure. Actual issuer tests use real TOTP, preserve base expiry, deny old/pending/refresh credentials, enforce cutoffs across five receivers without changing separately authorized recipient seats, forbid global self-review/unnamed peers, and roll back a cycle when the real bearer is revoked inside blocked audit DML. The real browser configures and confirms a review independently, reauthenticates affected holders, loses actual license-management authority at the cutoff, and gives a metadata reader no list/individual-profile requests or mutation controls. The read-only expired screen is visually checked.

Review-time boundaries in database/HTTP/browser tests use an isolated test-database owner's substitution of `identity_role_review_now()`; production code defaults to `clock_timestamp()` and exposes no clock override. These are deterministic boundary tests, not wall-clock latency/SLA certification. Credential verification remains an owned upstream HTTP fixture, not a deployed authentication-service or Microsoft tenant. Both Compose variants validate offline, JavaScript syntax/relative imports/new-file whitespace and `git diff --check` pass, and TypeScript retains exactly the same 17 unrelated diagnostics. Owned listeners, native clusters, private fixture files and isolated browser configuration/build paths are cleaned up. No production migration, image build, external provisioning or recovery action is performed.

## Twenty-Ninth Implemented Increment: Versioned License-Source Lineage and Conditional Withdrawal

Recipient licensing now has a stable target for future access reviews. Policy migration `010_license_grant_lineage.sql` gives each direct user grant and whole group rule a server-generated UUID incarnation. Group-derived member sources refer to their parent grant rather than masquerading as independent direct grants. Deleting/regranting a direct source or explicitly reactivating a retired group creates a new UUID, even for the same pool and recipient. Automatic reconciliation cannot reactivate a disabled rule.

Source-derived, append-only `license_grant_events` record semantic expiry, local membership and retirement changes under serialized grant locks. No-op reconciliation, source hashes, synchronization status and error metadata do not churn versions. Targets, source provenance and caller-chosen IDs cannot be rewritten through ordinary SQL updates. Retired grant history cannot be reused or mutated into a new right. These are database application guards, not an off-host immutable archive or protection against a database owner disabling triggers or truncating storage.

Migration imports existing sources as `origin: observed`, with no invented actor, issuer or historical approval. Opaque legacy recipients remain opaque. Orphaned group sources, disabled parents with surviving sources and mismatched group expiries fail migration for explicit reconciliation; they are not silently made canonical or granted a new authority. Reapplying migration preserves existing IDs, versions and history. No production migration is run by this development increment.

### Protected Source API

- `GET /api/licenses/:licenseId/grants` requires assignment-read authority and returns at most 100 grants, an explicit `hasMore` and UUID `nextAfter` cursor. `?after=UUID` is the only supported query. It is a bounded keyset page, not a complete snapshot inventory.
- `GET /api/licenses/:licenseId/grants/:grantId` returns the minimal source view and exact `"od-license-grant:<grantId>:<version>"` ETag. Current state distinguishes active, expired, revoked and deleted sources; local source-user counts do not imply a fresh directory snapshot or purchased vendor inventory.
- `POST /api/licenses/:licenseId/grants/:grantId/revoke` requires assignment-read/manage authority, a linked canonical human, issuer-bound identity/MFA evidence no older than 300 seconds, the exact `If-Match`, and only `{ "confirm": true, "reason": "10–500 characters" }`. Missing versions require preconditions, stale versions return `412`, and inactive grants cannot be acted on. Ordinary users, license approvers, role-review voters and vendor-status readers gain no new source-management rights.

Withdrawal locks affected local subjects in canonical order and then the pool, rereads the source/version, and revalidates the original bearer against the live issuer both before DML and before commit. A local membership committed during the pool wait invalidates the old form. A direct withdrawal removes only that direct grant. A group withdrawal disables the whole rule and removes only its derived sources, so reconciliation does not recreate the denied source. Other direct/group sources remain effective, sharing one seat. Recalculation, source lineage, counters, reasoned canonical audit and provider desired-state/outbox work commit together. Authority/audit failures roll all of them back; a broken projection is not repaired by inventing a new assignment during withdrawal.

The receipt is deliberately `localOnly: true`, with removed-source and retained-user counts. A configured provider worker handles an effective withdrawal separately; a later known Graph observation, not local deletion or successful POST alone, confirms removal. Uncertain, inherited or foreign vendor assignments retain the existing reviewed-recovery restrictions. The worker's database admission also rejects mutation privileges on group rules and lineage tables.

### Actual Console and Remaining Review Work

The group section of the license kiosk reads the known grant before opening its confirmation form. It shows the exact source, local version/count, whole-rule effect, preserved overlaps and unconfirmed manufacturer state. A reason and explicit checkbox are mandatory. The write submits only the captured source version, never a recipient-wide delete or silently refreshed version. Changed contexts ignore late reads/receipts; stale, uncertain or fresh-MFA-required failures close the action to blind retry. Strict client parsing rejects partial, wrong-scope, wrong-status and inconsistent receipts.

This increment is a review prerequisite, not a scheduled license-review plane. No reviewer policies, independent named license-recipient votes, recurrence/deadline cutoffs, automatic review mail, device-owner reviews or tenant scopes are advertised. Historical recipient-delete APIs remain separately authorized unconditional compatibility operations; the kiosk's group action has moved to the fenced path, not every legacy client. A future review must target the immutable direct grant or whole group incarnation, preserve base expiry separately, and disable a denied group rather than merely deleting a child that reconciliation would recreate.

### Verification

Owned native PostgreSQL tests cover legacy observation/rejection, migration idempotence, immutable targets/history, bounded pagination, membership/version fencing, overlapping-source preservation, explicit regrant, competing withdrawals, pool-lock races, atomic audit failure and post-DML authority loss. Provider tests follow local withdrawal through queued desired state, a real owned HTTP removal and a separate confirming observation. Frontend tests cover conditional requests, explicit confirmation, stale contexts and invalid/ambiguous receipts. Fixtures use disposable local credentials, not production or Microsoft tenant credentials.

Final relevant gates pass **1,321 distinct cases**: policy Jest 390, frontend Jest 640, native license/group/provider PostgreSQL 117, actual issuer/receivers 142 regression cases plus the new focused issuer source-withdrawal case, and real Chrome 31. The focused issuer case rejects an actual password-only token, requires actual TOTP enrollment and a new normal login, commits only the exact source, then rejects the original source manager after canonical-role revocation. Chrome confirms the real kiosk form and captured ETag through the actual issuer, identity source and policy database; an overlapping direct entitlement remains and group reconciliation cannot resurrect the withdrawn parent. Its screenshot is inspected for readable source, version, counts, explicit confirmation and unconfirmed manufacturer status, without factor or token disclosure.

The final affected frontend slice passes 39 cases without double-counting them above. TypeScript retains exactly the same 17 unrelated diagnostics; no new type error is accepted. JavaScript syntax, new-file whitespace and `git diff --check` pass. Owned native clusters, listeners, temporary credentials and isolated browser/build configuration are cleaned up. This increment performs no deployment, production migration, external purchase or live Microsoft tenant operation. Recurring recipient reviews and the remaining production boundaries are still unfinished.

## Thirtieth Implemented Increment: Recurring Independent License-Source Reviews

Policy migration `011_license_reviews.sql` adds opt-in pool-specific recurring reviews for canonical human direct grants and whole group rules. Identity migration `014_license_review_roles.sql` extends the canonical catalog to 47 roles. Deploy coordinated shared-contract, issuer, directory, gateway, policy/worker and web images; no production migration or deployment is performed by this development run.

### Separate Duties and Minimal Identity Evidence

- `license-review-administrator` can read/configure/retire review policies and read known reviews, but cannot allocate licenses or vote merely by holding this role.
- `license-reviewer` can read its assigned queue and vote on explicitly assigned sources, but gains no catalog, allocation, policy-management or directory-profile powers.
- `license-review-reader` can read policies and known review IDs without mutation powers. Ordinary license administrators and role-review voters do not implicitly acquire license-review administration or voting duties.

The two writing roles are protected canonical grants with fixed real MFA and a maximum original authentication age of 300 seconds, including when another client or refresh flow is chosen. Global administrators still need explicit naming as a voter and cannot certify a source that benefits them. Named reviewers must currently be enabled, linked to the caller's issuer and able to hold the actual voting duty; unavailable or self-only reviewer sets fail configuration.

A purpose-limited `GET /api/license-reviewers/:id/snapshot` returns only schema version, known canonical ID, enabled/link status and current voting availability. It is gated to configuration authority and a canonical human, not a profile/role inventory or credential-bearing endpoint. The gateway preserves the original verified bearer and delegates final authorization to identity-service. Configurators/voters receive only existing narrow known-user/group eligibility reads needed for independence, never ordinary profile access. A group vote checks fresh authoritative active/inactive membership plus local derived beneficiaries both before and after DML; unresolved identities, outages and a reviewer joining during a vote fail closed.

### Real Entitlement Cutoffs

Current and future grant UUIDs attach to immutable-target review leases. The first review is due immediately with an explicit response window; a timely independent retention schedules the next due time from confirmation plus the configured interval and advances only the cycle. Source/rule base `expires_at` values do not change. The effective user projection aggregates each source's earlier base/review expiry, preserving overlaps and one seat per user. Millisecond projection precision rounds cutoffs down, never past a base expiry.

The existing allocator, SQL expiry-update guard, raw projection-insert guard, active catalog/assignment queries, capacity guards and provider desired-state checks use this effective bound. A deadline therefore removes local effective entitlement even before a worker sweeps physical rows. The existing expiry worker later releases sources/counters, disables an expired group parent and produces the existing transactional provider work. No new privileged scheduler or ambient reviewer credential is introduced. Provider removal remains separately queued and observed; neither the review receipt nor a successful Graph POST claims confirmed manufacturer removal.

New direct-source headers also capture their canonical beneficiary eligibility revision independently of a shared user projection. A missed disable/reactivate generation cannot be recertified as the old direct grant, and a retention never rewrites that origin. Pre-migration or otherwise unproven direct origins are not retroactively stamped: explicit canonical source reconciliation/regrant is required before enabling reviews over them. Group reviews certify the current whole rule, not every member's individual lifecycle or group membership. External account mapping and broader lifecycle-event ordering remain separate unfinished work.

Denial withdraws the exact direct UUID or disables the whole group incarnation and removes its derived sources. Other direct/groups remain, and normal reconciliation cannot recreate the denied parent. A separately authorized explicit new allocation is a new UUID and a new review window, not resurrection of a retired lease. Review-only code refuses to repair a missing projection by creating a new assignment.

### Conditional Policies and Decisions

Canonical routes under `/api/licenses/reviews` are:

- `GET /policies/:licenseId`, `POST /policies`, and `POST /policies/:id/retire` for known-pool configuration/reading. Policy intervals are 3,600–31,536,000 seconds; response windows are 60–604,800 seconds and shorter than the interval. One to twenty sorted unique canonical voters, a reason and explicit cutoff acknowledgement are required.
- `GET /self` returns at most 100 assigned reviews with explicit truncation, prioritizing locally actionable sources over obsolete history. `GET /:id` is a known-ID read, not a directory/license inventory; unassigned callers without all-read duty receive uniform `404`.
- `POST /:id/decisions` accepts only retain/deny, a reason and explicit scope acknowledgement. Its ETag binds lease version, policy version and current source version. A changed local membership, base expiry or retirement invalidates the captured form without silently advancing a cutoff.

Configuration and source mutations share a database pool gate, including direct SQL insertion waiting behind policy creation. Review actions lock local subjects in canonical order and then the pool, reread source/lease versions, and validate the original bearer through the live issuer before mutation and before commit. Source withdrawal, projection/capacity changes, immutable review evidence and provider desired-state/outbox work commit together. Audit failure, a revoked original session, stale versions or beneficiary-generation drift roll back the attempted vote.

Policies are immutable except one-way, versioned retirement. Retirement stops new enrollment but leaves pending deadlines binding; a timely independently reviewed retention after retirement can end recurrence only up to the unchanged base expiry. Replacement rebinds live leases without extending an earlier cutoff. Already denied/expired leases cannot be rescued by retirement, replacement or late retention. Reviewed pools remain human-only; device/mixed pools and unresolved legacy targets are rejected rather than partially advertised as governed. Configuration is bounded to 1,000 current source grants and 10,000 local user-source records per operation, with bulk canonical-order subject locks. A 30-second source-verification budget is checked between I/O and projection updates; this is not a transport/cutoff SLA. Oversized or timed-out operations fail rather than silently omitting targets.

The kiosk separates known-pool policy configuration, assigned voting and known-review reading. Forms display exact grant/cycle/policy/source versions, base expiry, deadline, whole-group consequences and unconfirmed manufacturer status. Reasons and explicit acknowledgements are mandatory. Strict client contracts reject wrong-status, wrong-scope, duplicate-queue and internally inconsistent receipts. Policy and decision forms are mutually exclusive, so even combined administrative duties cannot reuse a different acknowledgement. A changed canonical user or duty discards stale forms and late displays; failures never automatically retry the captured decision or report an uncertain write as success. This display cancellation is not a rollback of an already accepted server operation.

### Verification and Limits

Native PostgreSQL coverage includes current/future capture, an SQL insertion actually blocked behind configuration, recurrence, unanswered local cutoffs, finite base expiry, direct beneficiary generations, group withdrawal/overlaps, no reconciliation resurrection, policy retirement/replacement, migration idempotence, malformed scopes, self/unresolved membership, source-version fencing, concurrent votes, audit failure, post-DML authority loss and raw unbounded/device projection rejection. Provider coverage follows an unanswered review through the actual capped projection, an owned HTTP removal and a separate confirming observation.

Actual issuer/receiver cases enroll real TOTP factors, use the new protected duties, exercise the gateway's narrow reviewer endpoint and assigned queue, reject an ordinary license administrator, preserve base expiry through retirement, reject late retention after an unanswered cutoff, and revoke the original bearer while real review-audit DML is blocked. That withdrawal rolls back the cycle, projection and decision evidence. Actual Chrome covers separate configuration/voter/reader contexts, explicit whole-group withdrawal preserving a direct overlap, captured conditional versions and no profile or credential disclosure. The rendered review panel is inspected for readable target, source versions, deadline, reason and acknowledgement.

Deterministic review-time boundaries substitute `license_now()` only as an isolated test-database owner; production defaults to `clock_timestamp()` with no API/environment override. Actor MFA freshness and provider transport/backoff clocks remain real. These are domain-boundary/contract checks, not wall-clock cutoff latency or vendor SLA certification. Default role-review enforcement, the single global identity namespace, legacy unconditional mutation clients, notifications, off-host audit retention, tenant/resource scoping, device/application/group-membership reviews and live Microsoft tenant acceptance still require further work; this is not full Entra feature parity.

Final relevant gates pass **2,704 distinct tests**: issuer 397, identity 280, gateway 94, policy 399, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 662, native identity PostgreSQL 87, native license/group/provider/review PostgreSQL 139, full issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 145, browser mediation 39, real Chrome 32 and layout 5. Final affected receiver and Chrome slices are rerun separately without double-counting them. The receiver slice also checks that an expired group disappears from active source lists and reports zero effective users before any sweep, while a retained direct overlap survives. Legacy migration coverage proves that a pre-review grant is not retroactively stamped even when a positive cached user revision exists.

The dedicated provider login still fulfills with its original narrow grants, cannot read review-audit contents, and is rejected if it gains `TRUNCATE` on review leases. This prevents a nominally non-writing worker identity from erasing accepted cutoffs. Policy/decision acknowledgements are independently exercised even for a caller holding combined duties.

Two earlier development Chrome attempts were not fully green: session-menu waits timed out and an unchanged recovery fixture returned `401`. Those unchanged cases pass the final complete 32-case run; no production MFA check or automatic write retry is weakened to obtain that result. This is point-in-time acceptance, not a claim of a flake-free browser harness.

TypeScript retains exactly the same 17 unrelated diagnostics, with no new type error. JavaScript syntax, new-file whitespace and `git diff --check` pass. Both Compose variants validate offline with the delegated-orchestration and license-fulfillment profiles and disposable values; no image build or deployment is claimed. Owned native clusters, listeners, temporary credentials and isolated browser/build configuration are cleaned up. No production migration, license purchase or live Microsoft operation is performed.

## Thirty-First Implemented Increment: Resource-Scoped License Administration

License administrators no longer need global allocation authority for every delegated task. A separate, opt-in resource plane binds a canonical human to one human license pool, one fixed duty and a mandatory lifetime of 60–28,800 seconds. This is an actual allocation boundary, not a tenant label or a client-side catalog filter. The identity namespace remains global.

### Separate Control and Resource Duties

- `license-scope-administrator` can read known-pool delegation metadata, grant independent bounded leases and conditionally revoke them. It cannot allocate licenses merely by holding this role.
- `license-delegate` is a protected bridge role. Alone it provides no catalog, allocation, approval or delegation-configuration rights; a separate currently usable resource lease is mandatory. It receives only purpose-limited known-user/group snapshots needed by actual scoped operations, not directory profile inventories.
- `license-scope-reader` can read known-pool delegation metadata but cannot configure leases or allocate licenses. This control-plane role is distinct from the `reader` duty on a resource lease.

Both protected roles have fixed real MFA and a maximum original authentication age of 300 seconds across integrated clients. A lease grants `reader`, `allocator` or `approver` duty. Each can read the narrow pool summary, sources and pending requests; only allocators can assign user/whole-group sources and conditionally withdraw them, and only approvers can decide independent pending requests. These are not catalog-editing, lifecycle, provider-configuration or license-review-policy duties. A configurator cannot grant itself a lease.

Control-plane routes live under `/api/licenses/delegations`; scoped operations use only `/api/licenses/scoped/pools/:licenseId`. The existing global permission array and legacy/global licensing routes never incorporate pool-derived authority. Even a combined-role human cannot use a captured read or approval operation as an allocation operation. Writes require a reason and explicit confirmation; authority and audit requirements derive from the operation, not the presence of an optional reason.

### Canonical Origins and Real Cutoffs

Each immutable lease UUID binds issuer, pool, beneficiary eligibility revision and the live `license-delegate` role-source fingerprint. The fingerprint includes the actual delegate grants, activation/authentication policies and applicable role-review generations, not unrelated held-role changes. Disable/reactivate, removal/regrant, a new PIM activation or a changed applicable review generation cannot silently revive the previous lease, even after the human obtains a fresh valid token. Its old unrevoked incarnation must be explicitly retired before a replacement with the same pool/user/duty is created.

The purpose-limited `/api/license-delegates/:id/snapshot` exposes exactly schema version, canonical ID, eligibility revision, enabled/link status and a delegate-source hash or null. A configurator can read known recipients; an ordinary delegate can read only its own issuer-linked identity. The gateway forwards the original verified human bearer. No ambient service credential, cached positive role grant or profile-derived identity substitutes for failed canonical evidence.

Admission captures one exact usable lease. Allocation transactions check the original human, fresh canonical evidence, issuer state, exact lease version, pool and deadline before work and before commit. The final lease deadline is checked again after use-audit DML and the final issuer call. Grant revocation and resource writes serialize through the same pool lock; a captured lease is never replaced by another lease or a global-role fallback mid-operation.

Model-level operation guards and transaction-local SQL pool guards protect the source/rule/request/projection/history/audit write paths. A read context cannot write, a delegated mutation cannot change catalog metadata, and a write to another pool rolls back. A disabled-target lookup cannot invoke global offboarding under a pool-scoped context. Group approval checks fresh authoritative membership both before and after DML, so joining the beneficiary group during approval cannot authorize self-review. A successful mutation appends scope-use evidence in the same transaction as its existing allocation/source evidence; audit failure or original issuer revocation rolls back both.

Lease expiry or revocation removes administrative authority, **not business licenses already allocated**. Those retain their own immutable base expiry, recurring source review, eligibility and provider desired-state rules. A scoped receipt confirms only local state, never Microsoft provisioning. Historically delegated pools retain their lease/audit history: catalog deletion is rejected, changing them to a device pool is forbidden, and device materializations cannot be inserted into them.

### Real Console and Verification

The license kiosk now has separate known-pool control-plane and own-lease views. It shows only a six-field scoped pool summary, without private costs or notes. It requires a fresh reason/acknowledgement for each captured target and action, uses conditional source/delegation versions, discards late results on identity/permission changes and never automatically retries an uncertain write. Capacity-blocked group configuration stays distinct from successful allocation; vendor state remains explicitly separate. Actual Chrome exercises separate configurator/delegate logins, MFA, independent grant, forbidden foreign/global access, local allocation and revocation. The rendered action panel is inspected for legible targets, reason, confirmation and local-only meaning.

Native PostgreSQL coverage exercises pool/duty isolation, raw SQL reader/cross-pool writes, overlapping sources, immutable/bounded/replaced lease incarnations, lifecycle and role-source generations, independent approvals, a group membership change during review, disabled-target cross-pool cleanup prevention, lock-wait expiry, final-issuer-call expiry and post-DML authority loss. Actual issuer/receiver cases use real TOTP and canonical PostgreSQL roles, verify the minimal gateway snapshot, reject legacy global allocation and separate approval from allocation. Role revoke/regrant and disable/reactivate require new tokens but do not restore old scoped authority. Revoking the original bearer while actual scope-use audit insertion is blocked rolls back the allocation and evidence.

Final relevant gates pass **2,782 distinct tests**: issuer 397, identity 296, gateway 94, policy 409, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 696, native identity PostgreSQL 87, native license/group/provider/review/delegation PostgreSQL 154, full issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 147, browser mediation 39, real Chrome 33 and layout 5. The final affected two-case issuer slice and one-case Chrome slice also pass against current sources and are not double-counted. TypeScript retains the exact same 17 unrelated diagnostics; no new type error is introduced. JavaScript syntax, new-file whitespace and `git diff --check` pass; both Compose variants validate offline with the orchestration and fulfillment profiles and disposable values. Owned native clusters, listeners, credential fixtures and isolated browser/build configuration are cleaned up.

An earlier Chrome run exposed an ambiguous select label in the new console; its explicit accessible name and visible action spacing are corrected, and the final complete 33-case run passes. An initial gateway run had one socket-hang-up failure; the unchanged final 94-case gateway run passes. No production assurance check is weakened or automatic mutation retry introduced to make these tests green. These are point-in-time local acceptance results, not a deployment or a flake-free harness claim.

Apply policy migration **012** and identity migration **015** only through a reviewed coordinated rollout of shared contracts, issuer, directory, policy, gateway, fulfillment worker and web images. No production migration, image build, deployment, purchased license or live Microsoft operation is performed here. There is no tenant isolation, resource-scoped PIM/eligibility/review plane, custom scope hierarchy, automatic default delegation, assignment ownership partition or off-host audit archive. Existing global license roles remain global; the new bridge still needs limited known-ID eligibility/group evidence within the global directory. This is a first concrete resource boundary, not Entra feature parity.

## Thirty-Second Implemented Increment: Independently Approved Pool-Scoped Activation

The resource plane now supports real just-in-time activation rather than merely expiring an already active administrative grant. Immutable eligibility, an independent decision and explicit owner activation are separate durable objects/states. None adds a global role or expands the legacy license API permission array.

### Three Distinct Authorities

The existing protected `license-scope-administrator` configures or conditionally revokes known-pool eligibilities. Each binds one canonical `license-delegate`, issuer, pool, duty, target eligibility revision and delegate-role incarnation. Eligibility lasts 300–2,592,000 seconds; maximum requested activation is 60–28,800 seconds and cannot exceed the configured eligibility duration. One to ten distinct, currently available canonical reviewers must be named. Neither recipient nor configuring human can appear in that reviewer set.

The new protected `license-scope-activation-reviewer` duty requires fixed real MFA with at most 300 seconds original authentication age. It can read only its assigned pending queue and decide an explicitly named, independent request; it acquires neither allocation nor eligibility-configuration authority. An ordinary license approver, role-PIM approver, license-source reviewer or global administrator does not inherit this activation-review duty. Global administration must additionally hold the explicit protected role and be independently named; there is no self/global approval bypass.

Only the holder can request activation, cancel its unspent request, activate an approved request or deactivate its own resulting grant. Each write requires real fresh MFA, a confirmed reason and the exact captured eligibility/request ETag. Approval is not activation. The owner receives actual resource rights only after a separate successful activation transaction, and only through the existing `/api/licenses/scoped/pools/:licenseId` namespace.

### Durable State and Canonical Approval Origins

New routes are under `/api/licenses/delegations/pim`: known-pool eligibility reads, configuration/revocation, own entries, an assigned-reviewer queue, eligibility-bound requests, independent decisions, owner activation, cancellation and deactivation. Lists are explicitly bounded to 100 with truncation/pagination metadata. Known-pool readers cannot mutate; reviewer queues do not expose a directory profile inventory or provider credentials.

A request captures the eligibility version and requested duration. Its approval/activation response deadline is the earlier of 15 minutes after request creation or eligibility expiry. `pending → approved/denied`, `approved → activated`, `pending/approved → cancelled` and `activated → deactivated` are one-way, versioned transitions. Denial/cancellation creates no active grant. Expired unspent requests can be explicitly cancelled, not silently renewed. Parallel decisions or activations cannot consume one version twice.

The purpose-limited `/api/license-activation-reviewers/:id/snapshot` returns exactly schema version, canonical ID, eligibility revision, enabled/link status and a per-reviewer-role source hash or null. Configurators and delegates can read known reviewers; a reviewer can read its own proof. It is not the existing license-source reviewer endpoint or a role/profile inventory. The gateway and policy client retain the original verified human bearer; there is no service-token fallback.

Approval captures both the reviewer's canonical eligibility revision and actual activation-reviewer grant/policy/review incarnation. Both reviewer and delegate snapshot hashes additionally bind the issuer's nonreusable subject-link generation. Owner activation checks that evidence before and after DML, as well as the holder's original eligibility/role incarnation. Reviewer role replacement, disable/reactivate, applicable role-review change or unlink/relink cannot revive or consume the old approval, even when issuer, subject, canonical UUID and role grants are restored identically. Unrelated role changes do not alter a per-role source hash, although the issuer can still require normal reauthentication for its broader session fingerprint. A newly legitimate reviewer can decide a new request, not rewrite an earlier decision's origin.

Activation creates exactly one ordinary scoped-grant UUID linked immutably to its consumed request. Its deadline is the earlier of requested lifetime and eligibility expiry. The runtime `license_scope_grant_usable` check also requires the original eligibility version, unrevoked parent and activated request. It is used at admission, before/after allocation work and after the final original-issuer call, so parent revocation or expiry affects actual scoped authority without waiting for a privileged sweep. Role/lifecycle source checks from the preceding resource increment remain in force. Reviewer evidence is checked when issuing the activation; subsequently valid activated authority is governed by its holder, parent, explicit revocation and lifetime, not by continuous reassignment of the reviewer's own powers.

Owner deactivation retires only that administrative grant and request; it never deletes recipient assignments. An expired or externally revoked PIM grant can still be explicitly retired by its owner. An existing unretired standing/PIM grant of the same pool/user/duty blocks a new activation rather than being replaced or adopted. Reusing a retired eligibility or request UUID cannot restore authority.

### Transaction and Operator Evidence

Pool and canonical-subject locks serialize configuration, activation, parent revocation and existing resource mutations. SQL guards retain immutable eligibility/approval/grant origins, independent actor/issuer boundaries, lifetime caps and one-way state changes. An in-flight resource context cannot invoke the PIM control plane or fabricate PIM audit evidence. Eligibility history also prevents deleting a governed pool or retargeting it to a device pool.

PIM transition evidence and the existing scoped-grant audit commit atomically. Failed evidence, original-bearer revocation during actual audit DML, a late final issuer call, or loss of the original reviewer proof rolls back both grant creation and approval consumption. The fulfillment worker's protected-table privilege check now also rejects DML/TRUNCATE authority over eligibilities, activation requests and PIM audit; its existing narrow read/work grants are unchanged.

The actual license kiosk shows separate configuration, own eligibility/request and assigned-reviewer views. Each action displays pool, canonical target, duty, exact version and requested lifetime, requires a new reason/acknowledgement, discards late context results and never automatically retries an uncertain write. It distinguishes eligibility, approval and active authority, and explicitly keeps previously allocated licenses separate. The real three-actor Chrome flow enrolls actual TOTP factors, configures eligibility, requests/votes/activates, allocates through the resulting scope and deactivates it while retaining the seat. The rendered activation confirmation is inspected for readable identity, version, duration and acknowledgement.

Native PostgreSQL cases cover the entire flow, foreign pool/issuer and wrong-duty denial, independent naming, root/self rejection, exact versions, parallel votes/activations, request timeout, denial/cancellation, eligibility expiry/revocation, immutable SQL history, migration reapplication over an active grant, scoped control-plane bypass attempts, missed holder/reviewer incarnations, reviewer loss during DML, failed audit and final-issuer-call expiry. Actual issuer/receiver cases additionally verify the fixed minimal gateway proof, reviewer disable/reactivate fencing and identical reviewer/holder issuer unlink/relink with real signed MFA access, plus original-token revocation while activation audit insertion is blocked. A dedicated worker still fulfills normally, cannot read PIM audit, and is rejected when separately granted TRUNCATE on each new control/evidence table.

Final relevant gates pass **2,848 distinct tests**: issuer 397, identity 308, gateway 94, policy 417, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 722, native identity PostgreSQL 87, native licensing/provider/review/delegation/PIM PostgreSQL 170, full issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 150, browser mediation 39, real Chrome 34 and layout 5. Focused final acceptance slices are not double-counted. The complete issuer and Chrome runs are repeated after strengthening issuer-link proofs.
