Migration reapplication preserves the existing active grant exactly, and each new worker-table TRUNCATE grant is independently rejected.

TypeScript retains the exact same 17 unrelated diagnostics with no new error. JavaScript syntax, new-file whitespace and `git diff --check` pass; both Compose variants validate offline with the orchestration and fulfillment profiles and disposable values. Earlier API-fixture attempts had socket-hang-up failures and one different ID-token denial classification; the unchanged final cases pass. No production purpose/MFA check is weakened or automatic write retry added. This is point-in-time local verification, not a flake-free harness, deployment or Microsoft acceptance claim. Owned native clusters, listeners, temporary credentials and isolated browser/build configuration are cleaned up.

Apply policy migration **013** and identity migration **016** through a coordinated rollout of shared contracts, issuer, directory, policy, gateway, worker and web images. The strengthened delegate hash deliberately invalidates pre-link-bound pool grants/eligibilities; retire and independently reissue them rather than rewriting historical origins or restoring an older identity image. It does not convert standing grants into PIM or prohibit newly authorized standing pool/global grants. This is opt-in pool-scoped PIM, not mandatory per-pool PIM policy, delegation access reviews, tenant isolation, custom scope hierarchies, notification delivery, emergency access design or full receiving-service consolidation. Recipient-license source reviews remain a separate existing plane. No production migration, image build, deployment, license purchase or live Microsoft operation is performed.

## Thirty-Third Implemented Increment: Versioned Mandatory Pool Activation Policies

The scoped activation plane now has actual opt-in enforcement policy rather than merely offering PIM beside unrestricted standing allocation. A policy binds one immutable pool UUID and one of its `reader`, `allocator` or `approver` duties, with a mandatory-activation flag, 60–28,800-second administrative lifetime ceiling and nonreusable version. Unconfigured policy is explicit read-only origin version 1; the first saved policy is version 2 and every later change increments it, including relaxation or re-saving the same settings. Policy/audit history cannot be deleted through the administrative API.

### Separate Policy Authority and Prepared Paths

`license-scope-policy-administrator` is a distinct protected canonical duty with fixed real MFA and at most 300 seconds original authentication age. It has known-policy read/write capability and only purpose-limited known delegate/reviewer evidence, not eligibility creation, voting or license allocation. `license-scope-policy-reader` can inspect a known pool/duty without mutations or directory-profile inventory. A global administrator does not inherit policy-write authority unless it additionally holds the explicit protected role.

Enabling mandatory activation requires one to ten explicit existing eligibility UUIDs. Each must match the pool, duty and issuer, remain unrevoked with more than five minutes local eligibility lifetime, fit the proposed duration ceiling, and retain its actual canonical delegate/link origin and available named reviewer authorities. The policy writer cannot be the target, original eligibility grantor or any reviewer. This separates eligibility configuration, policy adoption, independent voting and owner activation rather than letting a writer prepare its own privileged path.

Known policies are read/changed only through `/api/licenses/delegations/policies/pools/:licenseId/:duty`. Writes require a reason, explicit invalidation acknowledgement and an exact policy ETag. Pool/subject locks serialize changes with eligibility/grant operations and existing source mutations. The original issuer is checked before/after DML and after final canonical peer checks; a final SQL cutoff verifies that prepared eligibility has not expired during the last issuer call. Audit failure, peer loss or original-bearer withdrawal rolls back policy, evidence and apparent authority invalidation atomically.

### Irreversible Origins, Not Silent Migration

Standing/PIM administrative grants, eligibilities and activation requests now capture their policy birth version. A new policy generation invalidates older administrative grants of that duty, whether they were standing or activated. Pending/approved activation requests cannot cross that boundary, and relaxing a policy cannot restore them.

The explicitly prepared eligibility IDs are an adoption path for **future** requests under the new generation. Their immutable original records are not rewritten, and their prior approvals are never adopted. Holders must retire stale unspent requests/grants as applicable, request again, obtain a fresh independent decision and activate a new UUID. New eligibilities born under the current policy remain possible through the separate configurator duty. Each subsequent edit must deliberately prepare any older eligibility it wishes to carry forward.

Runtime scope admission, source snapshots, pre/post-operation checks and the final lease check use the current policy-bound authority helper. SQL insertion guards reject new standing grants while PIM is required, reject overlong grants/eligibilities/requests and prevent mutation of their birth origin. Every saved version fences old authority even when the new mode is permissive. Existing business-license base expiry, source-review leases and licensed seats are not rewritten by this administrative policy.

### Actual Mutation Enforcement and Preserved Automation

Allocator requirements protect manual direct/group assignment, source withdrawal and manual group reconciliation in the canonical API, global license-role paths and the compatibility adapter. Request decisions then require a genuine scoped PIM approver, even if the approver's own duty policy is otherwise permissive. An approver requirement independently protects request decisions; it does not prohibit an independently permitted allocator's manual source configuration. Catalog `auto_approve` cannot bypass either writing requirement: new self-service requests remain pending. A valid PIM grant is never implicitly borrowed by a global/legacy request; the operator must use the scoped namespace.

Model guards check the exact operation after acquiring the pool lock. SQL guards additionally protect source/header/rule creation, manual source changes, request decisions and deletion of still-effective projections. A raw source insertion that was already waiting on the pool lock sees the policy committed during that actual wait, rather than using the earlier permissive state. Configured policy also retains the governed human pool against deletion/device retargeting.

Business automation remains distinct from interactive administrative authority. Already configured canonical group rules can derive their current members without an active administrator. The reconciler now updates the existing rule rather than issuing a misleading insert/upsert during maintenance, and marks only its bounded group derivation context. Expired sources and authoritative security offboarding can release seats without PIM. A named independent source-review denial can still withdraw its exact direct/whole-group source through the existing proof/versioned review plane; the reduction exception does not permit new allocations or source extensions. Protected worker privilege checks reject DML/TRUNCATE over both new policy/evidence tables while retaining its original narrow fulfillment grants.

Reader policy governs the `reader` administrative grant incarnation. It is **not** a confidentiality boundary over global catalog readers or the read capabilities of separately authorized allocator/approver grants. Group-membership administrators can still affect a preexisting group assignment through their separate canonical membership authority; this policy does not implement membership reviews or tenant isolation. Existing global roles remain broad outside the explicitly protected operations.

### Real Console and Verification

The license kiosk has a separate known-pool policy view showing duty, captured version, required/permissive mode, administrative lifetime and explicitly prepared IDs. Writers need a new reason/acknowledgement for each edit; identity/duty changes discard late or opened confirmations. An uncertain receipt is not a successful change and has no blind-retry control. Readers have no setters. The screen explicitly distinguishes administrative authority invalidation from recipient-license removal and global catalog confidentiality.

Actual Chrome uses separate configurator, policy writer, delegate and reviewer accounts with real TOTP, plus an independent read-only account. It invalidates a previously usable standing grant, explicitly retires it, requests/votes/activates a new policy-bound grant, allocates locally, and relaxes the rule without reviving that old activation or removing the business license. The rendered policy confirmation is inspected for exact pool, duty/version, prepared path, duration and explicit acknowledgement.

Native PostgreSQL verifies independent preparation, fixed duties, wrong/self/creator/reviewer paths, duration caps, old grants and pre-policy approvals, conditional edits, irreversible relaxation, raw SQL roots/projection deletion, actual pool-lock waits, auto-approval suppression, real scoped approval, canonical group maintenance, source expiry, security offboarding and independent direct/group source-review withdrawal under mandatory PIM. Audit failure, last-stage peer loss, final-issuer-call cutoff and original-issuer withdrawal cannot commit a policy generation. Actual issuer/receiver cases also reject global and real compatibility-adapter allocation, reject old activation requests, prove genuine scoped allocation and revoke the original policy writer while real audit DML is blocked.

Final relevant gates pass **2,893 distinct tests**: issuer 397, identity 309, gateway 94, policy 417, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 748, native identity PostgreSQL 87, native licensing/provider/review/delegation/policy PostgreSQL 185, full issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 152, browser mediation 39, real Chrome 35 and layout 5. The final four-case affected issuer/browser slice passes against the current policy and maintenance guards without double-counting it. A new integration case proves direct and group source-review denial under mandatory PIM, not just expiry/offboarding. TypeScript retains exactly the same 17 unrelated diagnostics; JavaScript syntax, new-file whitespace, `git diff --check` and both offline Compose variants pass.

An early new-console browser attempt exposed an ambiguous populated-textarea label; its explicit accessible name is corrected. Adding independent actors also moved an existing managed-user fixture past the first real directory page: the browser helper now uses the actual canonical search instead of assuming first-page placement, and the final complete 35-case run passes. No production pagination, MFA/purpose check or write retry is weakened. These are local point-in-time results, not a claim of a flake-free harness or live vendor acceptance. Owned native clusters, listeners, credentials and isolated browser/build configuration are cleaned up.

Apply policy migration **014** and identity migration **017** in a coordinated shared-contract/issuer/directory/policy/gateway/worker/web rollout. Unconfigured pools retain the implicit permissive generation; no pool is silently made mandatory. Review each duty deliberately, prepare independent eligible paths and plan explicit cleanup/re-request after invalidation. Mandatory defaults, emergency/recovery design, recurring delegation/eligibility reviews, global catalog confidentiality and canonical tenant boundaries remain outstanding. No production migration, deployment, image build, license purchase or live Microsoft operation is performed.

## Thirty-Fourth Implemented Increment: Recurring Delegation and Eligibility Reviews

Delegated **administrative** pool grants and PIM eligibilities now have an independent recurring review plane. This is distinct from reviewing a recipient's business-license source: losing the ability to administer a pool must not silently remove licenses already assigned to employees.

### Separate Duties and Real Independence

- `license-delegation-review-administrator` configures and retires policies and reads known review metadata. It has no allocation, eligibility creation or review-vote authority.
- `license-delegation-reviewer` reads assigned reviews and conditionally retains or denies their exact targets. It cannot configure policies or allocate licenses.
- `license-delegation-review-reader` reads known policy/review metadata without setters or votes.

The first two duties are protected and have non-weakenable verified MFA with a 300-second original-authentication age. The shared catalog contains 56 roles, 19 protected roles and 12 fixed-MFA roles. Global administration does not implicitly confer either new writing duty. A current original human bearer is required; no service-token or workload substitution is accepted.

Each policy governs one human pool, issuer, duty (`reader`, `allocator`, `approver`) and target kind (`grant`, `eligibility`). Issuer binding is not tenant isolation. One to ten named, currently enabled, issuer-linked explicit administrative reviewers must be available. No implicit global-administrator voter is added. The minimal five-field canonical snapshot at `/api/license-scope-reviewers/:id/snapshot` reveals availability, not a user profile or role inventory; only the separate configurator discovery permission can use it.

The holder, original grantor/eligibility configurator, original PIM activation reviewer and review-policy author cannot certify the target. Existing and future target insertion must leave at least one independent named path. Holding multiple roles cannot bypass these exclusions. Retention also compares the holder's current canonical lifecycle and role/link incarnation against the immutable target origin before and after DML and the issuer checks. A stale target can be denied, but not positively recertified into a new incarnation.

### Binding Cutoffs, Not Scheduler Labels

Policies have an immutable configuration and UUID; retirement is a conditional one-way version increment. Intervals are 60–2,592,000 seconds, response windows 60–604,800 seconds and no longer than the interval. The first review is immediately due, with its response window already constraining current and future target authority. There is one permanent review lease per target kind/UUID. Configuration inventories are bounded at 1,000 targets and fail explicitly rather than capturing a silent partial set.

An unanswered deadline removes actual scoped authority in the live admission, parent/request helpers and final checks, without waiting for a privileged scheduler. Timely independent retention advances the review version/cycle and schedules its next due date and deadline; it never changes raw target expiry. Denial atomically increments and revokes the actual grant or eligibility. A denied eligibility also makes its child grants unusable. Neither action removes business-license sources, releases their seats or claims a vendor removal.

Retiring a policy freezes, rather than erases, its accepted deadlines. A replacement policy cannot re-stamp an existing target UUID, extend an earlier pending cutoff or revive an expired/denied incarnation. Stale vote tags fail instead of being retried against a different state. The review tag binds review, policy and target versions; concurrent retain/deny consumes exactly one captured version. Reasons, proof and audit are part of the same transaction as target changes.

### PIM Parent and Approval Fences

Existing active PIM children receive an immutable ceiling when their eligibility first enters review. Later parent retention cannot extend those already issued activations. New activations physically cap their raw grant expiry to the current eligibility review deadline, in addition to the existing parent/policy/request limits. A separate grant review can impose a still-earlier administrative cutoff.

Pending/approved requests capture both eligibility-review and review-policy generations. Attaching a review, retaining its parent or retiring its review policy invalidates those old unspent approvals. The holder may explicitly cancel and request again, obtain a new independent activation vote and activate a new UUID. Already consumed requests are not retroactively unspent by a review cycle, but their active grants still honor immutable ceilings, base expiry and the live parent. Retention is therefore never a hidden activation extension.

Mandatory pool-PIM preparation now uses the eligibility's **review-effective** remaining lifetime, not merely its original expiry. Short or expired reviewed parents cannot be presented as a sufficiently live independently prepared path. Existing business-source withdrawal exceptions, canonical group maintenance, expiry and security offboarding remain distinct and operational.

### Console, Evidence and Restart Safety

The license kiosk has a separate administrative-review panel. Configurators load a known pool/duty/kind, see absence rather than an invented policy, then explicitly acknowledge immediate existing-target cutoffs before creation. Retirement separately acknowledges that accepted deadlines remain binding. Named voters see due targets, holder/pool IDs, all three versions, cycle, base expiry and effective cutoff before confirming retain/deny. Own bridge-role status exposes the holder's bounded review deadlines without giving them a vote. Metadata readers use a known review UUID rather than a global profile inventory. Lists are bounded at 100 with explicit truncation.

The strict client validates exact envelopes, canonical identifiers, bounded versions, tags and conditional receipts. It rejects retargeted IDs, skipped generations, changed base expiry, an unconfirmed denial target revision and an uncertain HTTP response. Identity, permission, pool/duty/kind changes discard pending forms and late responses. Every write needs a new reason/acknowledgement; failure offers reload, never an automatic or blind write retry. The PIM panel also explains that review cutoffs can shorten its displayed base lifetime.

The four new policy, lease, fence and append-only audit tables are forbidden to the dedicated fulfillment worker, including accidental `TRUNCATE` or read grants. Protected SQL proof/control triggers preserve target origins and resource-context separation. This is local transactional evidence, not an off-host immutable compliance archive or a complete audit viewer.

The policy service replays SQL files at startup. Its migration batch now uses one schema-bound advisory lock and transaction, with duplicate-object recovery isolated by savepoints. Failure rolls back **all** temporarily replaced authority helpers; successful restart exposes the final review-aware wrappers together. Wrapper parameter names remain compatible with the earlier policy/PIM definitions. Native verification interrupts the actual runner after older helpers were replaced, proves the expired review still denies authority after rollback, and then successfully replays without changing review identity, retained cycle or cutoff.

There is no application/API production clock override.

Revoking the original voter while real denial audit DML is blocked rolls back target revocation, review cycle and audit. ## Thirty-Fifth Implemented Increment: Independent and Recurring Group Memberships

The directory is still global; a tenant label cannot establish isolation over the current global user, role, group and application stores. Inspection also identifies a concrete privilege-source gap: group membership controls canonical app sign-in and previously configured group-license derivation, independently of an administrator's pool-PIM lease. This increment governs that actual source rather than hiding group controls or treating administrative lease review as membership approval. The separate cross-service tenant acceptance plan remains mandatory.

### First Approval and Independent Duties

- `group-review-administrator` configures and retires known-group review policies and reads known review metadata, without group CRUD, profile, role-assignment or licensing powers.
- `group-reviewer` reads named assigned reviews and independently approves/retains or denies their exact membership UUIDs. It cannot configure policy or directly manage arbitrary groups.
- `group-review-reader` reads known policy/review metadata with no setters or votes.

Configuration and voting are protected duties with non-weakenable verified MFA and a 300-second original authentication age. Global administration does not implicitly confer either new writer duty. The shared catalog now contains 59 roles, 21 protected roles and 14 fixed-MFA roles. Current issuer-linked reviewer availability, canonical actor generations and original bearers are checked, including after actual DML. Human evidence records canonical context, original authentication time and factor revision, not a reusable token or an OTP. SQL independently bounds the recorded freshness against the real clock.

An opt-in policy governs one canonical flat group and issuer, with one to ten named current reviewers, a 60–2,592,000-second interval and a 60–604,800-second response window no longer than its interval. The holder, original canonical grantor, policy author and **any configured member of the group** are not independent voters. Raw pending membership also excludes a voter: it cannot be used to manufacture self-approval. At least one independent named path must remain. Adding the sole voter to the group rolls back the new source and its capture rather than silently removing independence.

New governed memberships are configured links, **not effective authority**, until an independent first approval. The normal member-add response explicitly returns `membershipReview: { required, membershipId, effective }`; it does not represent a waiting link as app access or a delivered license. Governed additions also require the original group writer's fresh real MFA and live authority before/after insertion. Only the separately authorized group manager can request the link; the review configurator/voter does not gain that general mutation permission.

Existing links receive an immediately running first response window upon deliberate enrollment. Every link has an immutable origin and membership UUID. Existing unproven historical grantors are marked as legacy evidence and cannot be positively recertified by guessing old actor strings or usernames. They must be explicitly removed and reissued through a canonical writer, then independently approved. They may be denied or expire safely. Canonical disable/reactivate invalidates the reviewed recipient incarnation; it cannot revive the earlier approval.

### Recurrence Without Policy-Reset Bypasses

The review lease is permanent per membership UUID. Timely retention increments its exact version/cycle and schedules the next due date and deadline. First retention opens a new waiting membership; later retention recertifies it. An unanswered deadline removes canonical effective membership without a privileged scheduler. Conditional denial physically deletes only that exact membership and preserves the enabled user, other groups and independent license sources. Review and policy versions bind every vote; competing decisions consume one captured version, with no blind retry against a new state.

Retirement is one-way and freezes accepted old cutoffs. New admissions to a previously governed group require an active replacement policy; removing the policy's active flag is not an unreviewed admission bypass. Replacement cannot re-stamp an already captured membership UUID or extend its old cutoff. Explicit remove/readd creates a new waiting UUID under the current policy, never a revived old approval. Duplicate add does not replace the original provenance. Actual group deletion retains history; the existing group tombstone prevents reuse of its identifier.

Origins, policy/lease transitions and append-only local evidence have protected SQL roots. Configuration inventories fail above 1,000 raw targets instead of partially enrolling a group. Assigned and own lists are bounded at 100 with explicit truncation; removed historical memberships cannot crowd out a current waiting target. These bounds are deliberate operating limits, not complete enterprise bulk governance. The evidence is not an off-host tamper-resistant compliance archive.

### Actual App and Licensing Authority

The canonical issuer authorization source filters memberships through the same live review/lifecycle helper. First approval is therefore necessary for group-assigned app sign-in; a missed cutoff invalidates the affected app authorization/session even if the raw configured link remains in storage. Timely retention does not create a global role. Application-assignment independence checks deliberately still see raw configured membership, including waiting links, so an assignment manager cannot exploit a pending link to self-administer an app.

The licensing snapshot omits non-effective reviewed members from `memberIds`, retaining their canonical IDs in the non-effective member set. This does **not** report the human globally disabled or offboard other direct/group sources. Successful group reconciliation removes only that group's no-longer-desired sources and preserves direct overlaps.

Time-driven membership changes need a newer durable group revision: otherwise the existing license allocator correctly rejects different content under one captured source version. Snapshot reads now serialize with the group's writers and materialize only actual visibility changes, with atomic audit and revision updates. Parallel first reads perform that transition once. Metadata is read **after** the refresh lock is acquired, in a separate statement, so a real metadata-lock wait cannot mix an old name with a newly committed revision. A source audit failure rolls back the projection/revision and fails the read; the read-only issuer helper still denies expired membership independently.

At the close of this increment, this was not synchronous cross-database entitlement revocation: already projected local group licenses changed at the next **successful group reconciliation**, and outage-bounded source leases remained outstanding. The following thirty-sixth increment replaces that unbounded group-projection behavior with physical directory/member deadline caps. Vendor removal still additionally needs the separately configured fulfillment worker and observation; no instantaneous Microsoft action is claimed.

### Console and Receiving Paths

The Roles & Rights console now includes a distinct group-membership governance panel. It shows known-group policy, named assigned votes, own effective deadlines and known-ID metadata reads without borrowing profile or role-management rights. Confirmation shows group, holder, immutable membership UUID, review/policy versions, cutoff, independence exclusions and app/license effects. Unknown origins are explicit reissue work, not positive approval controls. Identity, permission and group changes discard opened forms, acknowledgements and late responses. Every write requires a new reason/acknowledgement; uncertain responses require reload, never automatic or blind write replay.

The strict client validates exact policy/review frames, canonical IDs, bounded string versions, conditional tags and semantic receipts. A skipped cycle, retargeted member/group/holder, changed policy generation, wrong tag or uncertain response cannot confirm success. The gateway has a specific original-path/original-bearer proxy before its older generic identity rewrite; the new namespace cannot be stripped or sent to an unrelated receiver. The direct directory and real Next path enforce the same domain rules.

### Verification and Rollout

Relevant gates pass **3,057 distinct tests**: issuer 397, identity 330, gateway 95, policy 429, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 833, native identity PostgreSQL 107, native licensing/provider PostgreSQL 204, full issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 156, browser mediation 39, real Chrome 37 and layout 5. Final affected issuer and real Chrome slices also verify the current source/receipt path without double-counting them.

Native PostgreSQL covers existing grace, first-approval gating, actual issuer memberships and license snapshots, scheduler-free expiry, once-only concurrent cutoff materialization, exact denial, independence, sticky retirement/replacement, explicit reissue, unknown provenance, canonical lifecycle non-resurrection, fixed fresh MFA, concurrent votes, raw tamper rejection, replay preservation, origin/source/audit rollback after original writer/voter authority loss, final issuer-call deadline crossing and bounded queues with over 100 removed incarnations. A real metadata row-lock race verifies name/revision coherence. The final boundary clock fixture is owned test data, not a production API time override.

Actual issuer cases use separate canonical protected reviewers and real TOTP. They prove that a configured waiting link cannot sign into its group-assigned app or consume a group seat, first approval enables both through the actual issuer/reconciler, cutoff invalidates the existing app token and causes successful reconciliation to release the real seat, and denial after explicit reissue preserves an independent direct license. Revoking the original voter while actual denial audit DML is blocked rolls back the raw link deletion, review version and audit.

The writer enrolls a genuine factor rather than posting an MFA flag; the policy/voter use the actual protected first-binding flow. The rendered console creates policy, displays the holder's waiting non-authority, independently approves a first membership, then withdraws its actual group source in a later due cycle and retires policy. Its target/version/cutoff/acknowledgement screen is visually inspected. ## Thirty-Sixth Implemented Increment: Outage-Bounded Group License Leases

### Mandatory Directory Bounds

Group-derived human licenses now require a bounded canonical directory observation, including groups without an opt-in membership-review policy. The strict **v2** source frame contains an observation time, an exact five-minute horizon and, for every effective member, its immutable membership UUID, canonical eligibility generation and any earlier membership-review deadline. Unknown, incomplete, contradictory, expired or future evidence cannot become a positive allocation. The shared contract caps groups at 10,000 identities and group transport at 4 MiB; unrelated identity reads retain their smaller limit. A v1 source is rejected, not guessed or silently upgraded.

The directory still serializes membership cutoff materialization with group mutation. A later observation of unchanged content does not manufacture a new business membership revision. Stable content hashing includes member incarnations, eligibility and review deadlines but excludes observation timestamps; same-revision content changes and backwards observations are rejected.

The receiver stores separate source observations, per-member leases and transition-derived audit evidence. A group member's effective source cutoff is the earliest of the observation horizon, membership-review deadline, original source expiry and any business-source review cutoff. Independent direct/other-group sources still compose into one physical seat using their effective maximum. Raw business expiry and root grant identity are not extended by a directory poll.

Most importantly, the bound is written into the actual `license_assignments.expires_at` projection. Capacity queries and the separate provider worker therefore cease treating a group-only seat as effective after that deadline **without a successful directory poll, lifecycle sweep or an open browser**. Historical rows/cached catalog counters may remain until housekeeping; active-seat reads use the deadline rather than the cache.

This is a bounded observation lease, not immediate cross-database revocation. An unreviewed membership removed just after observation may remain effective until its existing horizon. An already published earlier review deadline remains binding during a source outage. Direct-only licenses retain their own existing expiry/review/eligibility semantics; this increment does not create a universal outage lease for all direct grants.

### Race, Origin and Actor Fences

Freshness is checked after subject/catalog locking, during source evidence DML and after projection/audit changes. Deferred SQL constraints catch a lease that expires only at actual commit. Raw group-source insertion without a matching live member proof, unbounded human projections, backwards/retargeted observations and audit edits cannot bypass those checks. Missing member observations are non-authority, not an unbounded fallback.

Manual allocation/reconciliation, automatic group requests and group request approval now reconfirm the original bearer and the same canonical group content before and after the transaction's source work. Background derivation likewise reconfirms using only its configured source credential. A revoked human, source outage, changed membership or new self-beneficiary cannot commit using an admission-time snapshot. No broader service bearer is substituted, and an uncertain write is not blindly retried.

A canonical eligibility generation change requires explicit eligibility reconciliation; an incompatible surviving projection origin cannot be silently rewritten during renewal. Where other sources preserve that old projection, explicit withdrawal/reissue is required. A genuinely new current observation may restore still-configured desired group membership after a previous lease has lapsed; replaying the old expired observation cannot.

### Provider and Console Evidence

The dedicated Graph worker needs no additional directory-lease table privileges: it independently reevaluates the existing physical projection and canonical user generation. An owned group-source outage test proves expiry changes desired state to removal, emits one bounded remove request and requires a separate observation before reporting confirmation. Missing worker/provider credentials still cannot remove a real remote assignment; local expiry is not a claim of instantaneous Microsoft withdrawal.

The existing license-group read reports observed time, directory horizon, earliest member horizon and truthful unobserved/stale/member-expired states. A synchronization error no longer hides the expired state. The kiosk shows the deadlines and explicitly warns that missing automatic reconciliation means group rights expire within five minutes without a timely manual renewal. Status is an as-of-read view; SQL deadlines continue independently of reload.

### Migration and Operations

Apply identity migration **020** and policy migration **016** together with coordinated shared-contract, directory, receiver/worker and web images. This is a breaking source protocol and intentionally changes availability behavior. Legacy group projections are **not** restamped as freshly observed: migration removes group-only projections or reduces them to independent direct overlap, while retaining raw source/root/history evidence. A successful fresh, canonical reconciliation is required before a group can contribute again.

The actual service migration runner is transactionally serialized. An injected upgrade-audit failure rolls back projection fencing and newly created lease tables; replay preserves origins, observations, deadlines and evidence without generating a positive observation. Plan the initial reconciliation and any legacy-origin remediation before rollout; do not expose an older receiver as an alternate authorization path.

Automatic reconciliation remains explicitly configured through `LICENSE_GROUP_READ_TOKEN`, with the existing 30-second sweep. A valid source credential and successful processing comfortably within the five-minute horizon are required. Slow/large batches, source downtime or credential expiry deliberately fail closed. Each renewal writes observation/member evidence; provision database throughput and retention accordingly. The 10,000-member frame limit is not a throughput or group-count guarantee. Automatic workload credential renewal, performance guarantees and availability alerting are not added. Keep service/database clocks synchronized; the receiver permits only the existing bounded five-second forward observation skew, never an arbitrary future horizon.

No production migration, deployment, image build, secret provisioning, purchase or live Microsoft test-tenant acceptance is performed by this increment.

### Verification

The current verification includes strict source-frame/transport cases, native canonical directory/member-generation/deadline cases, physical cutoff without housekeeping, capacity reuse, direct/other-group overlap, pure lease renewal versus stable business lineage, duplicate/replayed/backwards observations, pool-lock and post-DML/commit expiry, raw tamper denial, audit rollback and actual service migration upgrade/replay. Dedicated-worker SQL tests reject reads and mutation privileges on the new protected tables.

Actual issuer tests verify that an independently approved member's published deadline caps the real local license projection before any group reconciliation, and that original bearer withdrawal during blocked lease audit DML rolls back the positive group allocation. Real Chrome verifies rendered stale evidence, zero effective group recipients with an independent direct seat preserved, explicit renewal and exact source withdrawal. The expired-evidence screen is visually inspected. Owned clocks and Graph endpoints are disposable test fixtures, not production time overrides or live vendor acceptance.

Relevant local gates pass **3,098 distinct tests**: issuer 397, identity 330, gateway 95, policy 448, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 836, native identity PostgreSQL 109, native licensing/provider PostgreSQL 220, full actual issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 157, browser mediation 39, real Chrome 37 and layout 5. Final affected issuer (two), Chrome (one) and kiosk-component (21) slices also pass against the current code, without double-counting them.

The actual blocked-audit issuer test exposed missing original-bearer reconfirmation in group allocation; the production path now performs the source/actor checks around its DML. Strict v2 validation and projection guards also rejected obsolete fake-source/raw-write fixtures, which now supply bounded canonical evidence instead of weakening enforcement. A fast broad run exhausted the unchanged production HTTP rate limiter; the owned issuer harness now reserves request-window budget between cases before producing fresh factors, without raising production limits or blindly replaying writes. Real OTP anti-replay intervals remain intact. These are point-in-time local results, not a claim of a flake-free harness or live vendor acceptance.

TypeScript retains exactly the same 17 unrelated diagnostics, with no changed diagnostic lines. JavaScript syntax, new-file whitespace, `git diff --check` and both offline Compose configurations pass. Docker image builds/live-container deployment are not verified because the local daemon is unavailable. Owned native clusters, listeners, isolated browser/build settings and ephemeral credentials are cleaned up; the broad development goal remains open.

## Thirty-Seventh Implemented Increment: Scoped Workload Source Consents and Automatic Tokens

### Unattended Licensing Without Human Role Borrowing

The bounded directory leases from increment thirty-six can now renew through explicitly configured OAuth client credentials instead of depending on a manually copied human bearer. The new `licenses.groups.derive` purpose grants only the group-source permission: no human role, profile, group administration, license allocation or Microsoft privilege. Directory application requests are restricted to the exact supported workload read paths; an application cannot borrow a human-looking subject alias through `/identity/subject`, or inherit a human license role from an unexpected claim. Policy-service rejects application principals at its human API boundary, including general policy configuration rather than just licensing; a valid narrow source token is not a policy administrator.

A group-source token alone is insufficient. Each known canonical group requires a separate, expiring directory consent for an exact issuer, dedicated confidential client and client generation. Discovery accepts only a client configured exclusively for client credentials and this single purpose, returning five minimal eligibility fields rather than secrets, profiles or a client inventory. Dot-segment/path-changing identifiers cannot redirect the original operator bearer to a different endpoint.

Consent administration extends the existing protected `license-scope-administrator` duty, with its fixed fresh real-MFA requirement; global administrator status alone is not the write duty. The existing `license-scope-reader` can read known-scope metadata without allocation/profile powers. No new global roles are introduced. The configurator cannot use a human console client as its workload target.

Creation acknowledges the exact group, client generation and absolute expiry, from at least sixty seconds to at most thirty days. `expectedClientRevision` is a real precondition against fresh canonical discovery, not an authoritative client version accepted from the body. A changed registration returns a conflict before creating a grant; post-DML client/actor changes roll back scope and audit. Conditional withdrawal requires the exact consent ID/version. It cannot rewrite the group, client, expiry or historical actor. Expired and withdrawn rows remain evidence; replacement is an explicit new consent, not an upsert that resets accepted deadlines.

A consent is an independently expiring data-read entitlement, not a child copy of the author's current human PIM activation. Later author logout/role expiry does not silently delete already committed consents; deliberate withdrawal and the absolute consent cutoff govern them. General independent app consent/ownership reviews and tenant governance remain open.

### Protocol and Physical Cutoffs

The current group-source transport is **v3** for both human and workload observations. It adds a strict nullable `sourceAuthorization` object: human reads have null; workload reads carry the consent ID/version, issuer, client ID/generation and absolute expiry, with no human viewer. The issuer and directory recheck the original workload's live token/client state and the current exact consent around the actual group read. A denied workload cannot first lock or materialize cutoffs in an unconsented group.

Transport version and canonical-content hash version are intentionally separate. The canonical v2 hash representation remains stable for unchanged directory/member data; viewer, observation and source-consent metadata do not manufacture a business membership revision. A manual-to-workload observation therefore does not conflict with the same canonical group revision or reset business grant/review identity. Authorization metadata is independently captured in the observation/audit and must remain exact across the operation.

The receiver bounds each member lease by the earliest directory horizon, member-review deadline, original business expiry/review and **source-consent expiry**. Physical local assignments, active seat accounting and the separate fulfillment worker consume that cutoff. Even an otherwise fresh five-minute directory frame cannot keep a group-only entitlement alive past its earlier known consent deadline. HTTP consumers reject already expired known consent/member deadlines instead of presenting an open transport horizon as current authority.

Client disable/re-enable, scope/grant changes or secret rotation advance canonical client generations. Saved consents do not silently adopt that new generation. After an explicit withdrawal and new consent, a newly configured credential can resume maintenance of already configured group rules; the source credential still cannot create those rules or allocate through the human API.

Withdrawal or an unexpected source/client failure is not synchronous cross-database revocation. A previously accepted projection may remain effective only until its existing earliest deadline, at most five minutes from observation. Independent direct/other-group sources retain their own legitimate bounds. Remote withdrawal still needs its separately configured worker and a later observation; no instantaneous Microsoft removal is claimed.

### Protected Source Configuration

First register a separate confidential workload through authorized OAuth client administration, with `grantTypes: ["client_credentials"]`, `scopes: ["licenses.groups.derive"]`, no redirect URI and explicit enablement. Its secret is a one-disclosure administrative result. A fresh source administrator then discovers its current generation and confirms known-group consents through `/api/identity/license-sources`. The receiving model uses canonical discovery before and after DML and enforces the original actor again; a body-provided issuer, creator, revision authority or expiry extension is not trusted.

Set `LICENSE_SOURCE_CONFIG_FILE` to an explicitly provisioned, owner-only regular JSON file, owned by the actual policy-service OS identity; symbolic links, group/world-readable files, excessive size, unknown fields and mismatched issuer origins are rejected. The token endpoint must be the configured live access-state authority's origin and the fixed `/oauth/token` path. Example structure, using placeholders rather than usable credentials:

```json
{
  "enabled": true,
  "tokenUrl": "http://oauth-provider:3010/oauth/token",
  "groups": {
    "clientId": "<dedicated-group-client-id>",
    "clientSecret": "<protected-group-client-secret>"
  },
  "users": {
    "clientId": "<separate-user-eligibility-client-id>",
    "clientSecret": "<protected-user-client-secret>"
  }
}
```

Either source block can be omitted. If user eligibility is automated, its separate client uses only the existing `licenses.users.read` bridge; it cannot replace the group consent or borrow its purpose. The file/secret mount and correct UID/mode must be provisioned by the operator; Compose passes the configuration path but does not create or securely own a secret file. This is protected plaintext configuration, not an encrypted vault. Provision appropriate TLS/network protection for credential-bearing service traffic; the internal HTTP example is not production certificate or network-isolation provisioning. No real credentials are committed.

The provider caches only a bounded in-memory token, checks the returned exact scope, coalesces concurrent requests and renews thirty seconds before advertised expiry. Issuer/source throttling and credential outages fail closed; a revoked cached token is discarded without replaying the failed operation. A denied particular group does not invalidate a still-live credential for a different consented group. There is no ambient human-token fallback. Stopping the worker latches its source providers, so a late token response cannot initiate a detached source read.

The existing thirty-second lifecycle sweep remains non-overlapping and checks eligibility before group maintenance. The new protected source setting and the corresponding legacy `LICENSE_GROUP_READ_TOKEN`/`LICENSE_USER_READ_TOKEN` cannot both configure the same source. Explicit legacy bearers remain a compatibility choice, identified in the console, not an automatic fallback when a configured workload fails.

This automates access-token acquisition/renewal, **not** client-secret rotation, configuration hot reload, consent extension, workload creation or universal credential lifecycle. Secret rotation needs protected configuration replacement/restart and an explicitly confirmed new-generation consent. Thirty-day expiry still needs deliberate consent renewal. Large/busy deployments must budget sweep latency and per-observation/member audit writes; the frame size limit is not a throughput guarantee.

### Console, Rollout and Evidence

The license kiosk has known-group consent lists, minimal client discovery, exact target/generation/deadline confirmation, a new reason/acknowledgement for every write and exact-version withdrawal. It contains no service secret field. Source readers have no write controls; actor/duty changes invalidate forms and late responses. Uncertain receipts require reload rather than a blind retry. A stable explicit textarea label preserves the confirmation's accessible name after entering a reason.

Group status includes an earlier expired service consent and its deadline, separately from directory freshness or membership review. The console distinguishes protected automatic token renewal from a legacy static source bearer and does not call configured metadata a live client or vendor entitlement.

Apply identity migration **021** and policy migration **017** after the preceding migrations, and coordinate issuer, shared-contract, directory, policy/worker, gateway and web images. This supersedes the v2 rollout from increment thirty-six. Old stored observations are not restamped or extended: they retain at most their previous bounded horizon. A v2 network frame is rejected; fresh v3 evidence is required for renewal. Service migration replay preserves existing consent/observation IDs, origins, hashes and deadlines rather than manufacturing consent.

Relevant local gates pass **3,215 distinct tests**: issuer 399, identity 346, gateway 96, policy 487, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 872, native identity PostgreSQL 126, native licensing/provider PostgreSQL 221, full actual issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 161, browser mediation 39, real Chrome 38 and layout 5. Final affected actual-issuer (four) and Chrome (one) slices also pass against the current source protocol and preconditions, without double-counting them.

Native cases cover exact per-group/generation access, fixed protected actor/MFA duties, bounded and conditional consent, no raw-row extension or audit deletion, client changes and original-actor withdrawal during DML, real group-lock waits, uniqueness, migration replay, audit rollback and final-check deadline crossing. Owned provider tests prove that an earlier source-consent expiry causes actual desired removal and separate observed confirmation without a source poll or local sweep.

Actual issuer tests use a second real issuer process with a sixty-second signed token lifetime, demonstrating proactive token renewal and a later physical lease without borrowing a human role. They verify forbidden profiles/aliases/license writes, exact consent deadline cutoff, secret rotation refusing a saved consent or stale creation precondition, explicit new-generation reissue and original author withdrawal inside blocked audit DML. Real Chrome uses genuine factor first binding, rendered exact confirmation, a separate metadata reader, exact source withdrawal and real workload access denial; browser storage never receives the test service secret/token. The confirmation is visually inspected.

During development, a workload license call exposed an unnecessarily attempted human binding: policy now rejects application administration before that lookup, rather than returning a misleading authority outage. Conditional creation also now rejects a changed client generation before creating consent, instead of relying only on a mismatched frontend receipt after commit. The browser accessible-name issue was fixed in the control, not bypassed with a looser security path or write retry.

TypeScript retains exactly the previous 17 unrelated diagnostic lines. JavaScript syntax, new-file whitespace, `git diff --check` and both offline Compose configurations pass. All results are local point-in-time evidence; no production migration/deployment, image build, purchase or live Microsoft acceptance is performed. Owned clusters, listeners, secret files and isolated browser/build configuration are cleaned up. The full development goal remains active; canonical tenant isolation, broader least-privilege defaults and the remaining governance/vendor work are not claimed complete.

## Thirty-Eighth Implemented Increment: Default-Deny Policy Operations and Actor-Preserving Delivery

### A Receiving-Service Boundary, Not Just a Hidden Menu

Inspection after source automation found a substantive remaining privilege gap: policy-service verified a human access token, but its non-license policy handlers did not distinguish ordinary sign-in from policy administration. The application-principal fence from increment thirty-seven did not solve that human authorization problem. Closing this receiver is a prerequisite for exposing more organizations, not a substitute for canonical tenant ownership or tenant isolation.

All forty-five currently registered non-license business routes now pass an explicit default-deny operation registry. This covers policy CRUD, status/assignment/link changes, templates, preview/compilation, baselines, update rings, GPOs and blueprints, including their existing fallback branches. New or unsupported operations are denied before business work; a route-inventory test requires deliberate review when the actual registered surface changes. Public health and the already guarded license namespace retain their separate boundaries.

The shared canonical role catalog now has **61 roles**, **22 protected roles** and **15 fixed fresh-MFA duties**. New `policy-reader` has policy configuration/preview/audit reads but no mutation, license or directory-profile authority. New protected `policy-administrator` can configure and deploy policies, but is not global administration and cannot use that role to allocate licenses or read user profiles. The existing `policy-deployment-operator` can deploy/assign existing policy configurations without CRUD authority over stored definitions. This is operational deployment authority, including narrowly permitted `update_policy` dispatch, not an independently approved, content-version-locked publication duty.

Only canonical human roles grant these permissions. Scope text, unexpected permission arrays, realm-looking administrative claims, body fields, actor aliases and application role-looking claims do not manufacture policy authority. Every configuration/deployment route requires a genuine bound MFA proof no older than five minutes, including global administrators; a weak per-role configuration cannot relax that receiver minimum. Side-effecting legacy evaluation also needs deployment authority rather than borrowing read-only preview permission.

Identity migration **022** initializes the two new role authentication/activation policies without assigning anyone a role or restamping existing generations. The new administration duty has a fixed MFA policy, protected by both runtime validation and a SQL update/delete guard. During validation, the new guard initially returned a null `NEW` for other-role deletes, suppressing the older evidence guard; the existing native regression caught that. Non-target deletes now preserve `OLD` so the established immutable-policy guard still runs. Replay preserves policy versions and creates no human grants.

### Original-Operator Policy Delivery

GPO and blueprint dispatch no longer exchange a human operation for `OAUTH_PROVIDER_INTERNAL_TOKEN`, nor omit authorization when requesting a certificate. A request-scoped transport captures the verified original bearer/claims, uses only configured fixed service origins and exact supported paths, rejects path-changing IDs, redirects and oversized/non-JSON responses, and applies a five-second HTTP bound with a two-MiB response limit. Original live token/session/client/directory/role state is checked around each HTTP call. Request completion, disconnect, denied authority or an uncertain receiver response prevents later dispatch. There is no alternate human credential, ambient token fallback or automatic write retry.

The actual issuer's device boundary now accepts a fresh canonical deployment operator only for minimal delivery inventory and the existing `update_policy` command. Inventory discloses only the canonical stored ID, hostname and platform, not device bearer/profile payloads. An unavailable persistent inventory is `503`, not a fabricated empty fleet. A narrow caller cannot queue a command for an unknown recipient or use its purpose for wipe, lock, restart, application installation, antivirus dispatch or general device administration. Existing global-admin/internal-consumer paths are not thereby retired or advertised as narrowly scoped workloads.

A successful command receipt proves only a genuine pending queue item. Responses identify `queued`, `no-targets` or `partial`, retain `enforcementConfirmed: false`, and identify unresolved non-device targets rather than silently counting them as executed. Blueprint notifications use `policy.delivery.queued`, not an unobserved `policy.applied` event. After an accepted remote side effect, later revocation cannot roll it back: the response explicitly reports uncertainty and stops subsequent delivery. There is still no durable operation journal, exactly-once recovery or observed endpoint enforcement. Certificate receiving-service authorization and actual endpoint interpretation remain independent prerequisites; rejection stops the operation rather than borrowing a stronger credential.

### Canonical Main Console and Routing

Both Compose variants explicitly configure the real issuer delivery port and no longer provision its ambient internal administration token into policy-service. Other existing consumers are not silently upgraded or granted a new workload purpose. Manifest regression checks also verify the shared runtime contract copies; Docker image execution is not thereby verified.

`/api/policies/access` exposes minimal original-user capabilities and current MFA readiness. The main `/policies` console now reads real persistent canonical policy rows; the former sample GPO tree, browser-local status toggles, fabricated copies and success-before-response writes are no longer its implementation. The console supports a captured draft preview/acknowledgement and actual activation/deactivation receipts, separates read-only operators, clears data/forms on authority loss, and rechecks the original actor before a confirmed mutation. It does not retry an uncertain write or label stored active status as device execution.

The frontend validates canonical IDs, pagination, capabilities, original-actor attribution and write receipts. PostgreSQL JSON key ordering cannot create a false content mismatch. Reads fail visibly on unavailable storage rather than displaying examples. This console does not claim exact SQL content-version publication preconditions or independent author/reviewer approval; those remain required for governed policy publication.

Next.js and the core gateway now route policy, blueprint, GPO and update-ring namespaces directly to the actual policy-service port without removing their namespace. Delegated policy roles do not need gateway administrator rights; the real receiving service authorizes their operation. The previous generic proxy both rewrote paths and demanded a separate global-like proxy grant. The actual gateway regression exposed that remaining proxy permission after the initial routing change; it is now a receiving-service delegation boundary, not a permissive alternate policy implementation.

Roll out identity migration 022 and coordinated issuer, policy, gateway and web images, including the shared policy authorization contract. An ordinary administrator with only password authentication must perform real MFA before policy writes. The new dedicated configuration administrator needs explicit authorized assignment or the existing independently reviewed PIM path; migration does not grant it to a user. Policy-service no longer needs an ambient issuer internal token for these HTTP dispatch paths. Provision appropriate protected service origins/TLS separately; packaging and local tests are not a deployed network or credential setup.

### Verification and Remaining Scope

Focused actual-issuer/PostgreSQL cases verify direct and gateway ordinary-user denial, read-only access, trusted original actor attribution, real fresh-MFA deployment, absence of license/profile powers, minimal inventory and unknown-recipient/wipe denial. A held first-command HTTP receipt lets the test revoke the actual original operator before delivery continues: exactly one real command remains pending and no second configuration is queued. Canonical role withdrawal and new-incarnation reissue cannot revive an old policy token. Native identity cases verify the immutable MFA floor and migration replay.

Real Chrome uses genuine factor first binding, the rendered main console, exact content confirmation, actual persisted draft/status changes and a separately logged-in read-only operator. A direct reader mutation is still forbidden. The confirmation/status console is visually inspected. Unit tests cover the full current route deny matrix, malformed capabilities/receipts, changed actors, unknown paths, stopped/late delivery, authority and receiver failures, fixed paths, queue receipts and no ambient fallback or write replay.

Completed local gates pass **3,375 distinct tests**: issuer 413, identity 346, gateway 102, policy 598, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 895, native identity PostgreSQL 128, native licensing/provider PostgreSQL 221, full actual issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 164, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 39 and layout 5. Final affected actual-issuer (three) and real-Chrome (one) slices also pass against the current delivery contracts without double-counting them. The long full issuer gate completes with its real TOTP/request-window waits, not reduced production limits or a replacement issuer fixture.

The existing TypeScript baseline remains exactly seventeen unchanged diagnostic lines. JavaScript syntax, new-file whitespace, `git diff --check` and both offline Compose configurations pass. Docker image execution/build remains unverified because the local daemon is unavailable. Owned native clusters, listeners, factor/credential fixtures and isolated browser/build configuration are cleaned up. No production migration, live-device deployment, external purchase or live Microsoft acceptance is performed; the full development goal remains active.

This increment closes ordinary-user policy access and original-actor HTTP substitution; it does not make the directory multi-tenant, add independently approved policy publication or complete endpoint management. Policy mutation/audit transactions still need the same complete post-DML/commit fencing, exact conditional writes and immutable off-host evidence as the more mature licensing plane. Legacy GPO/update-ring in-memory stores, blueprint fallback branches, alternate prototype views/static-route ordering, policy-as-code compatibility and persistent device-command recovery still need consolidation. Other internal-token consumers, receivers and certificate issuance must not be described as a universal least-privilege workload model.

## Thirty-Ninth Implemented Increment: Conditional Policy Configuration and Atomic Evidence

### One Persistent Mutation Path

The next inspection followed the policy receiver boundary into actual storage. Previously, policy status/configuration writes and best-effort audit were separate commits; a writer admitted before a lock wait could persist after losing its original authority. GPO/update-ring maps and blueprint/baseline fallbacks also created a second, unversioned apparent success path. These are removed rather than presented as reliable configuration.

Canonical configuration now has one transaction executor and one SQL-governed lineage for policies and blueprints. The existing policy domain/repository remains the policy mutation implementation, scoped to the executor's transaction rather than publishing events before commit. GPOs and update rings are explicit `gpo`/`update-ring` policy records with canonical UUIDs. New records are drafts; there are no operational `gpo-1`, `stable`/`beta`/`dev` memory objects, fabricated device counts or implicit whole-fleet GPO recipients. Create a real draft and use explicit versioned target assignments. The main `/gpo` console uses the canonical policy console rather than an unrelated sample view.

Blueprint metadata, configurations and assignments now use persistent SQL only. Every child addition/removal or target assignment advances the same parent version in its transaction. Duplicate policy targets cannot create duplicate dispatch recipients. Known old inconsistent target sets are rejected rather than silently merged into an apparently current frame. Unresolved group/OU/device expansion is not guessed; managed dispatch remains explicit about unsupported targets.

The operation registry still guards the full forty-five-route policy surface. Static recipes and previews no longer sit behind the single-record route; source outages cannot manufacture an empty effective fleet or a successful saved baseline. Baseline/template calls confirm a recipe hash and create a draft, not a certified compliance result or immediate activation. Legacy user-supplied violation claims no longer publish an authoritative `policy.violated` event through the preview endpoint. These static recipes are not complete or independently certified benchmark implementations.

### Exact Versions and Original Authority Through Commit

Creation requires a reason and explicit scope acknowledgement. Existing-record mutations also require the exact `If-Match` tag: `"od-policy:<UUID>:<version>"` or `"od-blueprint:<UUID>:<version>"`. GPO/ring aliases use their canonical policy tag. Parent rows serialize configuration/status/target changes; immutable IDs, type, creator and original timestamps cannot be rewritten by an update. Status changes have their separate deployment duty. New target/configuration receipts carry the actual next parent version. Stale or missing versions do not silently upsert, overwrite a different draft or adopt a newer definition. Null clearing and zero priorities now persist the acknowledged value rather than coalescing back to an old one.

Only verified canonical human roles remain authoritative. The executor checks the original bearer before entering storage, after an actual parent-lock wait and after DML including mandatory audit. Failed authority, storage, evidence, disconnect or final checks roll back the whole transaction. Deferred SQL guards independently enforce the captured known MFA and access-token expiry at commit, so a successful last network check cannot extend those known deadlines. Body-provided creators, status, versions or actor aliases cannot manufacture a new permission or replace the SQL-derived origin. This is checkpointed cross-service authorization, not a distributed transaction that serializes every remote revocation with a local commit.

Policy migration **018**, after the preceding policy migrations and identity role migration 022, records retained resource origins, immutable transition audit and matching pending outbox rows. Child triggers derive target/configuration evidence and the actual parent transition; a caller cannot directly append a fake audit or edit its history through the normal mutation path. Payload content hashes, not complete policy/configuration payloads, are copied into the new audit scope. This is not a credential vault: configuration and free-text fields still require appropriate secret handling, and database-owner/host tampering needs stronger off-host evidence and deployment-role separation.

Deleting a resource retains a terminal version, child-removal scope and its original UUID origin, preventing identifier reuse. Replay observes existing versions without restamping them, assigning roles or inventing historical approval. Existing persisted records retain their actual state and a `legacy-observed` origin; that label is not an independent publication approval. Unverifiable or invalid data needs explicit operator review, not guessed adoption. The old policy audit table is retained read-only as legacy evidence, not relabeled as new verified transitions.

Audit and pending events are mandatory parts of the commit. The former best-effort/precommit policy-change emits are removed. Outbox rows are **pending evidence**, not delivered broker messages or user notifications; a separately authorized reliable publisher/consumer path remains required. Compliance evaluation can still read configuration, but no immediate configuration-change notification or automatic broker-triggered reconciliation is claimed merely because a row committed.

### Version-Bound Managed Delivery and Consoles

Managed GPO/blueprint dispatch captures a coherent known configuration and requires its exact version plus acknowledgement. The original actor and the captured source version are checked around every bounded HTTP call. A later parent/child change stops the old dispatch; it cannot continue with new settings under an old confirmation. Uncertainty is cumulative: even a failure before a later HTTP request must not erase the fact that an earlier command may already have been accepted. Queueing is not endpoint enforcement, and prior accepted side effects are not transactionally undone.

These fences apply to canonical policy-service managed dispatch. The issuer's existing explicitly granted `update_policy` purpose and global/internal direct device paths are not yet a universally receiver-bound, independently approved content-publication protocol. Manual dispatch, content proof at every receiving device/service, durable operation/idempotent recovery and actual endpoint observation remain separate work. Certificate authorization and canonical group/device resolution are not supplied by a parent-version label.

The policy/GPO console now captures the exact stored version and a new acknowledged reason for each write. It can create real GPO/ring drafts using explicit JSON settings. The new main blueprint console separates known-scope reading, configuration and target/delivery duties; it reads coherent matching parent/configuration/assignment frames, captures exact child/target scopes and checks conditional receipts. Authority changes and uncertain responses discard stale forms instead of replaying writes or showing local success. It makes no unrelated prototype MDM/DEP inventory calls and does not call a pending command applied.

Policy capabilities are now **protocol v2**. New clients reject v1 metadata before displaying write controls or sending a mutation; they do not discover missing version enforcement only after an old server has already performed an irreversible write. Older clients also cannot treat the new metadata as their old six-field v1 contract. Coordinate policy, gateway and web images with migration 018; do not rely on ignored new body fields/headers as a rollout strategy. Direct API consumers must update their acknowledgement/reason, recipe hash and exact-version requests. Old prototype IDs are not silently mapped to new UUIDs.

### Verification and Remaining Scope

Native PostgreSQL cases cover actual version increments, conditional conflicts, concurrent writers, source loss behind locks/after DML, audit rollback, deferred MFA/token cutoffs, cascading deletion, UUID nonreuse, migration replay/legacy observation, persistent GPO/rings and correctly bounded evidence. API contract tests that previously expected in-memory policy success during SQL outages now require fail-closed storage behavior, while real PostgreSQL tests prove successful changes. This changes what is verified, rather than preserving a fake success assertion for a higher test count.

Actual issuer/receiver tests revoke the real original token while its policy audit DML is blocked, proving that settings, version, audit and outbox remain unchanged. Another test changes a blueprint after its first real command was accepted: the captured version fails and no later old-frame command is queued. Real Chrome exercises the versioned policy and blueprint forms, genuine factor first binding, parent/child/target receipts, a separate read-only operator and real queued command evidence. A separate down-level capability case verifies that the new client sends no privileged write to an old protocol. The browser fixture explicitly supplies its owned issuer delivery origin; it does not borrow a production token or relax rejection to hide an unavailable receiver.

Completed local gates pass **3,393 distinct tests**: issuer 413, identity 346, gateway 102, policy 575, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 914, native identity PostgreSQL 128, native licensing/policy PostgreSQL 239, full actual issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 166, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 41 and layout 5. Final affected actual-issuer (five) and real-Chrome (three) slices also pass against the current source without double-counting them. The full issuer/receiver gate completes with real authentication/request-window waits rather than reduced production limits. The updated policy total replaces obsolete SQL-outage-success assertions with fail-closed contract checks and actual PostgreSQL success evidence.

The existing TypeScript baseline remains exactly seventeen unchanged diagnostic lines. JavaScript syntax, new-file whitespace, `git diff --check` and both offline Compose configurations pass. The real blueprint console is visually inspected. Docker image execution/build remains unverified because the local daemon is unavailable. Owned native clusters, listeners, factor/credential fixtures and isolated browser/build configuration are cleaned up.

No production rollout, live endpoint execution, purchase or live Microsoft acceptance is performed. Canonical tenant/resource isolation, independently approved publication, default policy coverage, delivery/notification workers and off-host evidence remain unproven; the complete development goal stays active.

## Fortieth Implemented Increment: Independent Content-Bound Policy Publication

### Separate Review, Not Another Administrator Bypass

Exact-version configuration alone did not stop an author or ordinary policy administrator from immediately activating their own settings. Canonical policy activation and managed GPO/blueprint dispatch now require an explicit independent publication. This is mandatory on these paths, not an optional flag or a second permissive implementation. Deactivation remains a separately authorized safety operation and does not need a new positive approval.

The new protected `policy-publication-reviewer` role has policy reading/preview/audit-read and publication-review duties, but no policy editing, deployment, license allocation or directory-profile permissions. Its real MFA floor is fixed at five minutes in both the canonical role policy and the receiving mutation checks. Requesting publication belongs to the existing policy administrator (or explicit global administration), not to an ordinary reader or deployment-only operator. The catalog now contains 62 roles, 23 protected roles and 16 immutable fresh-MFA roles. Migration does not assign any of them to a person. Use authorized protected-role administration or the existing independently reviewed PIM path to appoint reviewers.

A canonical UUID, not a display name, subject alias, body-supplied actor or administrator label, identifies every participant. The requester and all retained content/target contributors are excluded from review, including someone who later acquires the review role under a different subject. Contribution evidence comes from the actual append-only configuration transitions. Assigning targets or linking scope also makes a person a contributor; an author cannot ask another deployment operator to change the target and then let that contributor certify it. An administrator still needs the explicit reviewer role and must be independent.

### Exact Scope and One-Way Evidence

Policy migration **019** retains a publication configuration head alongside the existing policy/blueprint lineage. The canonical SHA-256 frame includes the real resource fields, configuration generation, every stored assignment and all policy links or blueprint configuration payloads. Status/activation timestamps are not new content. Every configuration or target change, even a no-op content update, advances the configuration generation and invalidates old approvals. Editing an active policy returns it to draft in the same SQL transaction. Status activation/deactivation preserves the reviewed content generation while still advancing the ordinary conditional resource version.

Publication starts with an exact parent `If-Match`, captured frame hash, known independent reviewer UUID, reason and scope acknowledgement. A caller explicitly chooses 300–28,800 seconds of validity starting at request creation; approval does not restart or extend that deadline. Only one unexpired pending request or approved publication may exist for a content generation. Replacing it first requires withdrawal, expiry or an actual new configuration, preventing parallel votes or an older positive record from bypassing a replacement denial.

The assigned reviewer confirms or denies the captured resource version/hash with an exact `"od-policy-publication:<UUID>:<version>"` precondition. Pending decisions cannot silently adopt a later parent version. Denial is terminal; a denied or withdrawn row cannot be revived or retargeted. The original requester or assigned reviewer can explicitly withdraw a pending/approved publication under their current respective duty. Withdrawal stops new managed actions but does not erase its prior decision or compensate accepted device commands.

SQL guards derive append-only `policy_publication_audit` and matching pending outbox evidence atomically with the request/decision. Those rows retain actor, reason, source scope, content hash and one-way state/version; they do not copy complete payload secrets. Audit/outbox failure, post-DML loss of the original actor, stale resource/request tags or failed source checks roll back the decision. Deferred guards independently enforce known actor token/MFA and publication deadlines at commit. Direct normal-path SQL cannot fabricate publication history, replace the retained head or activate without a current independent approval.

Replay observes existing actual resource versions without inventing historical approval. Unverified `legacy-observed` origins cannot request publication; recreate them as explicit new drafts with reviewed scope instead of relabeling old active rows.
