Existing canonical origins with real retained creation evidence can request review normally. A stored legacy/active status alone is not a publication grant. Head/audit tables remain ordinary PostgreSQL controls, not protection against the database owner or a compromised host. Application database credentials and the executor remain trusted: transaction settings are not cryptographic issuer verification inside PostgreSQL.

### Current Participants and Version-Bound Managed Use

The narrow known-ID `/api/policy-publication-actors/:id/snapshot` source returns only enabled/link state and bounded request/review authority revisions, never a user profile or credentials. Dedicated policy configuration/deployment/review roles can use this read without general directory access. Its fingerprints include user eligibility/security generation, issuer-link incarnation, applicable role grants, role authentication/activation policies and recurring-review origins. Unrelated role changes do not restamp this particular duty, while removal/reissue, disable/reactivate, relinking or a manual directory security reset cannot revive an earlier approval. Global administration is not silently mapped to the separate review duty.

The original requester's and assigned reviewer's captured authority are checked after the real parent lock, before/after publication DML and before/after activation. Managed dispatch captures the explicit publication ID together with the exact current parent version and checks both participants and the unchanged approved frame around every bounded HTTP step. Withdrawal, expiry, changed content or participant authority stops subsequent sends. An already accepted first command remains a possible side effect; failures preserve cumulative uncertainty instead of reporting a clean rollback or retrying the command.

These are live cross-service checkpoints, not a distributed commit lock on every remote role/user change. The approval window authorizes new activation/managed delivery actions; it is not a device-runtime lease. Expiry/withdrawal does not automatically unapply an existing policy, rewrite every active-status label, remove an accepted queue entry or prove endpoint execution. Separate immutable effective-versus-draft runtime versions and automatic rollback are not implemented by the publication hash/history. The issuer's separately granted raw `update_policy` purpose and remaining global/internal device paths still need universally receiver-bound approved content and durable operation reconciliation. This increment does not call those alternate paths governed publication.

### Main Consoles and Breaking Rollout

The main policy/GPO and blueprint consoles share a real publication panel. Operators can inspect the complete captured content/targets and excluded contributors, request review with a known UUID and bounded lifetime, and inspect retained status. A separately signed-in reviewer sees only their independent pending decisions, with fresh reason/acknowledgement confirmation; author/reader/unassigned/stale frames have no positive voting control. The requester/reviewer can explicitly withdraw. Configuration/deployment roles select a specific locally matching stored approval before activation or managed delivery. The displayed `current` match is local content/deadline evidence, not a cached substitute for the participant source checks required during use.

The client checks request/decision receipts, exact versions, original requesting/deciding actor, unchanged scope and deadline. It clears captured approvals/forms after resource/session changes or uncertain writes, never retries a privileged mutation and never calls a queued command applied. New publication routes are covered by the default-deny operation registry; the non-license policy surface now has fifty-one registered routes.

Capabilities are **protocol v3**, retaining the bounded six-field envelope but advertising the mandatory independent-publication contract. New clients reject v1/v2 before any privileged write; old clients cannot accept the new metadata as their old contract. Deploy identity migration 023, policy migration 019, the shared role contracts and coordinated policy/identity/gateway/web images. Direct API consumers must supply the captured publication ID for activation/GPO/blueprint delivery and conditional publication requests/decisions. No reviewer, default approval, device credential or live Microsoft consent is provisioned by these source changes.

### Verification and Remaining Scope

Native PostgreSQL tests exercise mandatory independent approval, contributor exclusion, exact content/targets, no-op invalidation, concurrent decisions, terminal states, withdrawal, source loss after DML, mandatory audit rollback, raw-SQL bypass denial, one live request/approval, deferred expiry, legacy observation and replay. Canonical identity tests prove the separate immutable MFA role, absence of automatic grants and actual participant incarnation/security-reset changes.

Actual signed issuer/gateway/identity/policy/device tests revoke an independent reviewer's original token inside blocked publication audit DML and prove that approval/audit/outbox do not commit. Other cases withdraw/reissue the real reviewer role, withdraw a publication after the first accepted blueprint command, and exercise approved GPO activation plus actual pending queue evidence. Real Chrome uses genuine first-factor binding and distinct author/reviewer/reader sessions for request, exact-scope vote, selection, activation/blueprint delivery and subsequent withdrawal denial. A v2 receiver cannot receive a new-client privileged write. Confirmation layout and visible scope are visually inspected.

Completed local gates pass **3,485 distinct tests**: issuer 413, identity 356, gateway 102, policy 603, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 948, native identity PostgreSQL 130, native licensing/policy PostgreSQL 253, full actual issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 170, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 41 and layout 5. The affected actual-issuer nine-case slice, the final four-case publication-contract slice and the three-case real-Chrome slice also pass without double-counting their cases. The full authentication gate completes with real TOTP/request-window waits in approximately fourteen minutes, not reduced production limits or replacement issuer state.

The existing TypeScript baseline remains exactly seventeen unchanged diagnostic lines. JavaScript syntax, focused new-file whitespace, `git diff --check` and both offline Compose configurations pass. Confirmation layouts are visually inspected. Docker image execution/build remains unverified because the local daemon is unavailable. Owned PostgreSQL clusters, HTTP listeners, credential/factor fixtures and isolated browser/build configuration are cleaned up; unrelated user changes are preserved.

No production rollout, live endpoint enforcement, external purchase or live Microsoft acceptance is performed. Tenant/resource isolation, all-receiver content proof, separate effective/draft runtime publication, durable event/operation recovery, observed enforcement, default policy coverage and off-host evidence remain required; the complete development goal remains active.

## Forty-First Implemented Increment: Receiver-Bound Policy Commands and Durable Device Receipts

### No Raw Policy Payload Bypass at the Canonical Receiver

Sender-side publication checks still left a second authority: the issuer's device queue accepted arbitrary `update_policy` payloads from deployment operators, global administrators and ambient internal callers. It also acknowledged a memory entry before best-effort SQL, while local quarantine/compliance paths fabricated policy remediation without independent approval. Those are not reliable receiving-device governance and are removed from the canonical policy command path.

The canonical issuer now accepts only a reference to an exact policy/blueprint version, publication and blueprint item, plus one captured operation UUID. It does not accept posted settings, actor aliases or a caller-selected command ID. Every policy command needs an actual canonical human `policies.deploy` duty and fresh verified MFA, including a global administrator. Application administrator scopes, realm aliases and the internal service secret are not alternative policy command authority. Other command families and their existing global/internal paths are not made universally least-privilege by this change.

Before any managed command POST, policy-service verifies a fixed read-only receiver protocol and the original operator identity. An old receiver or a generic success body fails before a write, rather than accepting an irreversible old-format command and discovering missing evidence afterwards. Managed GPO inventory uses a new minimal persistent `/api/devices/policy-registry` purpose, including for global administrators; it cannot adopt the legacy administrator registry's sample/fallback fleet. No new roles are added: the catalog remains 62 roles, 23 protected duties and 16 fixed fresh-MFA roles.

### Independently Derived Content and Atomic Receiving Evidence

The receiver calls the fixed `OAUTH_POLICY_SOURCE_URL` origin at `/api/policies/delivery/frame` with the original bearer, never an ambient credential or a caller-selected URL. This default-deny source purpose requires current deployment authority. It checks the exact locked parent version, the current independent publication and both captured participant authorities, one explicit unique device assignment, the stored blueprint item or active GPO, and the receiving platform. It derives the payload from actual stored settings; missing, changed, unassigned or unsupported scope is not guessed. The source packet carries the real content generation/hash, publication version, item payload digest, original actor/operation and known deadlines.

Issuer migration **012** guards the existing `mdm_commands` table rather than creating a permissive parallel queue. A receiving transaction locks its captured operation and actual persistent device row, rechecks the original operator after the lock, reads the authoritative source, and inserts the derived payload. Mandatory SQL-generated operation origin, `policy_device_command_audit` and matching pending outbox commit atomically. Source and original-actor authority are checked again after actual receiving DML/audit, not only before sending HTTP. The SQL guard independently compares the actual stored JSONB payload digest with source evidence; a well-shaped but inconsistent source packet cannot manufacture acceptance.

The source admission observation lasts at most thirty seconds. The deadline for further device offers is at most five minutes, capped sooner by publication expiry, the original operator token and original MFA age. Those known cutoffs are independently enforced by deferred SQL commit guards. Receiving transactions have bounded admission below the issuer pool's capacity, preserving connections for live authority callbacks instead of exhausting the same pool they need to finish. Excess attempts fail before storing work. Failed source, authority, device binding, mandatory audit, caller closure or final checks roll back instead of leaving a ghost memory command. Signed credentials themselves are not persisted in command evidence: token hashes and bounded original actor/source metadata provide attribution.

One captured operation cannot exchange original user/token/client or adopt another resource version/publication/content hash. Concurrent identical receiver retries reuse one command/acceptance event and its original deadline; they do not extend authority or consume a second queue item. Policy-service itself still does not automatically replay an uncertain write. A new apply call gets a new operation and can create additional authorized commands. This is bounded receiving idempotency, not a durable publisher workflow planner, client-reserved operation recovery, exactly-once device execution or cross-database commit serialization.

The receiving assertions and transaction settings rely on trusted service code, source origins and application database credentials. They are not cryptographic issuer verification inside PostgreSQL, proof against arbitrary application-DB credential holders/owners, an off-host immutable archive or attestation by a potentially compromised receiver. Pending outbox entries are evidence, not delivered notifications.

### Persistent, Generation-Bound Device Offers and Reports

New enrollment waits for an actual persistent device row before disclosing a signed device credential. The credential captures that row's `device_generation`; a storage error cannot acknowledge enrollment or mint a memory-only policy credential. Bootstrap expiry, rotation during the storage wait and caller closure are rechecked before credential disclosure. A failed write releases its in-process quota reservation; a committed but unacknowledged enrollment still needs reconciliation rather than pretending it rolled back. Enrollment bootstrap secrets, quota/rotation HA, issuance audit, hardware trust and independent device admission remain legacy governance work, not supplied by a generation field.

Persisted platform, bootstrap-token or status changes advance the device generation. Policy polling/reporting requires the actual subject-bound device-purpose credential, matching persistent platform/generation and live token revocation state. An administrator/application access token cannot impersonate the agent on these paths. Old device credentials without a generation, or from a previous generation even after later reactivation, need explicit renewed enrollment/target handling; they are not silently upgraded. Caller input cannot select the registered UUID or its credential generation. Startup no longer writes demo devices into authoritative enrollment SQL.

Policy command polling reads the actual SQL queue, including from a fresh issuer process, and never serves legacy memory policy entries. Offers are bounded to one hundred pending items and the captured delivery horizon. Each actual offer attempt derives durable device-attributed audit/outbox evidence before response; repeated device polls may offer the same command again. Expired or differently bound commands are not offered, without a sweeper inventing completion. The agent must still deduplicate and safely execute commands; a committed offer does not prove response delivery or execution.

A current matching device can report one bounded terminal `completed`/`failed` outcome only after a recorded offer. An identical report can be acknowledged again, but a terminal result cannot be rewritten. Payload, source, actor and origin cannot be replaced through reporting. Report/audit failure rolls back the whole terminal transition. The response deliberately retains `enforcementConfirmed:false`: a device-reported success is not independently observed enforcement or hardware attestation.

Existing `update_policy` rows without this evidence remain `legacy-unverified` history with omitted payloads; migration cannot retrospectively bless or offer them. Administrative history is bounded/paginated and signals truncation. Other command families still have legacy persistence/authorization behavior and are not described as consolidated endpoint management. The main blueprint console validates the original actor, exact source/publication and committed receiving receipts, and displays accepted command IDs and their offer deadlines rather than an applied badge.

Publication withdrawal, source/role changes and receiver checks stop **new admission** at their live checkpoints. They do not automatically cancel commands already committed by the receiver: those can remain offerable until the captured deadline or device-generation fence. Already offered effects cannot be undone by an HTTP error. Immediate cancellation, source-aware execution leases, stale-command supersession and independently observed endpoint enforcement still need explicit workflow/device protocols. The console states this distinction rather than promising that withdrawal retracts accepted work.

### Breaking Rollout and Verification

Coordinate issuer migration 012, policy/issuer images with the shared policy-command contract, preceding identity/policy migrations and **policy capability protocol v4**. The six-field policy capability envelope now advertises receiving-content/persistence requirements; new clients reject v1–v3 before privileged writes. Receiver command protocol v1 is a separate explicit handshake, not acceptance of old policy protocol v1. Direct consumers must replace free payloads with exact references and captured operation headers. Provision the fixed policy source URL with appropriate network/TLS protection; no network trust setup is performed by these source changes.

Stop old receiving images before enabling the new plane. A SQL migration cannot make an old process's memory-first agent queue safe, and older source/receiver mixes are not a supported permissive fallback. Renew generation-less device credentials and explicitly reconcile assignments/publications instead of guessing device identity or blessing old queues. Non-policy/internal commands, certificate/package/lifecycle endpoints, complete enrollment governance, tenant/resource isolation and the separate effective-versus-draft runtime remain outstanding.

Native PostgreSQL tests prove persistent queue/origin/audit/outbox atomicity, original actor loss behind device locks/after DML, source change/outage, mandatory audit failures, receiver payload digest checks, concurrent retry uniqueness, immutable operation ownership, generation-bound durable offers/reports, report rollback, legacy observation/replay and deferred admission/token/MFA cutoffs. Canonical policy PostgreSQL tests derive actual approved GPO/blueprint packets and reject unassigned, changed, wrong-platform and reviewer-only delivery.

Actual signed issuer/gateway/identity/policy/device cases deny arbitrary payloads even to a fresh global administrator and valid internal secret. Receiving audit is held while the real operator is revoked or the independent reviewer withdraws approval, proving that the receiver itself does not commit. Genuine persisted enrollment credentials pull from a fresh issuer process and report without an administrator acting as the device. A separate real policy process targets an old receiver and makes zero command POSTs. Real Chrome confirms the v4 UI, independent publication, actual persistent receipts/offer deadlines, read-only separation and refusal of down-level metadata. The browser fixture provisions its owned policy-source origin before starting the separate issuer process; it does not weaken failure handling to hide an unavailable source.

Completed local regression gates pass **3,553 distinct tests**: issuer 438, identity 356, gateway 102, policy 611, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 955, native identity PostgreSQL 130, native licensing/policy PostgreSQL 255, full actual issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 196, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 41 and layout 5. The full issuer/receiver gate includes the twenty-one native queue cases; they are not counted again. The affected five-case actual receiver slice and three-case real-Chrome slice also pass. After the final bounded-admission change, all twenty-one native queue cases, the five-case actual receiver slice and the actual blueprint Chrome case pass again without double-counting. Concurrent queue admission leaves control-query capacity available and rejects excess work before storing it. The full authentication gate retains real request/TOTP windows and completes in approximately fourteen minutes rather than weakening production limits.

The frontend type check retains exactly the seventeen previously recorded diagnostics, with no added diagnostics. Syntax checks pass for twenty affected JS/CJS files; whitespace checks pass for all six new increment files and `git diff --check`. Both Compose configurations validate offline using disposable values. The Docker daemon is unavailable, so no container build/runtime claim is made. The actual blueprint receipt screenshot is visually inspected, and owned test processes, listeners and disposable PostgreSQL clusters have exited. No production migration/rollout, image build, live-device execution, external purchase or live Microsoft acceptance is performed. Full lifecycle/tenant/resource governance, cancellation/recovery/observed execution and off-host evidence remain required; the complete development goal stays active.

## Forty-Second Implemented Increment: Owned Policy Command Cancellation and Recoverable Receipts

### Stop Further Offers Without Borrowing Another Operator's Authority

Accepted commands previously remained offerable until their captured deadline or device-generation fence, even after a publication withdrawal. An operator had no supported way to stop their own stored work or recover its state after losing a browser response. A publication label, deletion of a memory entry or a browser-only cancelled badge would not stop the actual SQL receiver. This increment adds explicit receiving-side cancellation and durable own-command observation; it does not claim automatic cancellation for every source change.

`POST /api/devices/:deviceId/commands/:cmdId/cancel` requires the current canonical human `policies.deploy` duty and genuine fresh MFA. The caller must be the original command's canonical user, but may use a newly authenticated session rather than the original bearer. Even a fresh global administrator cannot cancel another user's work on this purpose; a reviewer, application, device credential or internal secret cannot impersonate its owner. There is no new ambient service authority or automatic role grant. A future incident/reviewer cancellation duty needs explicit scope and independent lifecycle design rather than a hidden administrator exception.

The request contains only the exact original thirteen-field receiving receipt, a ten-to-five-hundred-character reason and explicit scope acknowledgement, with a separate captured cancellation operation UUID in `X-OD-Operation-Id`. Actor, payload, destination, source version and execution result cannot be caller-selected replacements. Missing/extra fields, control characters, mismatched receipts and absent operation capture fail before transition. The service compares the entire immutable stored receipt, not merely a command UUID or current blueprint version.

Cancellation reduces future delivery authority, so it does not require a still-valid source approval, current target assignment or deliverable device generation. The owner can stop a pending stored command after source withdrawal, expiry or device retirement/re-registration. It does not reopen a previous grant or assume that an obsolete device credential is current. A completed/failed device result cannot be relabelled as cancelled, and a legacy unverified queue cannot acquire approved origin through this path. Already cancelled work cannot be requeued by replaying its original acceptance operation. An independently authorized new apply operation may still create new work; cancellation is not a permanent resource prohibition.

### One Atomic Receiving Transition and Retained Recovery Evidence

Issuer migration **013** extends the existing command transition rather than adding a parallel permissive queue. Cancellation takes the same persistent device lock used by polling/reporting and the actual command row lock, then rechecks the current original owner after waiting. SQL checks own canonical user, fresh human evidence, exact immutable receipt and the pending-to-cancelled transition. Payload, original actor/operation/source, original offer horizon, device offer evidence, report evidence and completion fields remain immutable. Cancelling an already offered command retains its first offer timestamp instead of inventing an unoffered state or successful execution reversal.

The transition, derived `cancelled` audit event, matching pending outbox and append-only `policy_device_cancellations` record commit together. The latter retains the cancellation operation, current actor metadata/token hash, original receipt, exact reason, audit ID and whether work had previously been offered. It stores no bearer or policy settings. Mandatory evidence/storage failures and post-DML original-actor revocation roll back the entire attempted stop. Deferred SQL guards independently enforce the cancellation actor's known JWT and MFA cutoffs at commit; expired original admission/offer deadlines are not mistaken for permission to renew a command.

Concurrent identical cancellations serialize and reuse one retained result without a second audit/outbox event. Reuse of the operation for a different command or reason fails, as does changing an existing cancellation operation. A fresh process and a newly authenticated session of the same current canonical user can recover the original result. SQL migration replay preserves retained status, receipt, actor, reason, deadlines and evidence. Direct status changes, evidence forgery/deletion, payload edits and reopening a cancelled command are rejected by the normal guarded application path. As before, these SQL assertions trust configured service code/application credentials; they are not protection against arbitrary database owners or cryptographic issuer verification inside PostgreSQL.

After cancellation commits, future SQL polls cannot offer that command and a later device report cannot overwrite its cancellation. A poll already committed before cancellation may still have a response in flight, and a device may already have received or acted on it. `furtherOffersStopped:true` therefore does not mean remote receipt was recalled, execution was prevented, configuration was rolled back or the endpoint is now compliant. Cancellation receipts and device reports explicitly retain `enforcementConfirmed:false`. No device-side safe-execution lease, cancellation feed or compensating rollback is added.

### Recoverable Own-Command Console, Not a Global Fleet Shortcut

`GET /api/devices/policy-commands` reads only the current canonical deployer's own verified command history for one explicit policy/blueprint resource. It requires fresh MFA, rechecks live original authority after the read, bounds pages to two hundred rows and signals truncation. Its purpose-specific projection contains immutable receiving receipts, effective pending/expired/terminal status, prior-offer presence and retained cancellation receipts, never command payload/settings, credentials, another operator's rows or legacy fleet samples. A storage/protocol failure cannot substitute an empty list. It also covers old content versions without requiring the present source version to equal their captured origin.

The main blueprint console uses this SQL history on load and after delivery, with explicit newer/older-page controls. It can recover a committed stop after a lost response or page reload, rather than assuming that clearing local delivery state cancelled anything. Each stop captures one receipt/operation, requires a reason and a separate acknowledgement, and rechecks the same original browser actor's live deployment permission/MFA before POST. Actor/resource/session changes discard stale confirmations and late reads. An unconfirmed mutation is not automatically retried: the UI asks for an explicit stored-state reload, and cannot leave a false stopped badge or an enabled stale action. Strict client contracts reject down-level history, mismatched actor/source/command/operation/reason, fake memory receipts, missing cancellation evidence and claimed endpoint enforcement.

Only the fixed own-history route and exact command-cancellation path are newly rewritten to the canonical issuer. Other device command families are not silently redirected or declared least-privilege. Read-only/stale-factor viewers cannot use this purpose. The API also accepts exact GPO receiving receipts; the new interactive own-command panel currently lives in the blueprint console. Complete global history, deleted-resource discovery, cross-owner incident administration and a durable publisher/retry planner remain separate work.

### Rollout and Verification

Apply issuer migration 013 after 012 and coordinate issuer/web images. Existing policy capability protocol v4 and the receiving-content v1 contract remain unchanged; owned history/cancellation uses its own strict v1 envelope and refuses missing/down-level storage rather than guessing support. No prior command is automatically cancelled or retrospectively approved by migration. Stop obsolete memory-first receiver processes as required by the previous increment; migration cannot recall their already accepted effects. No tenant boundary is inferred from command ownership: the directory is still global and requires reviewed resource/tenant adoption.

Native PostgreSQL verification covers original ownership, fresh-session cancellation without source authority substitution, full receipt binding, concurrent same-operation retries, cross-command operation collision, offer/cancel serialization, old generation/expired work, terminal report protection, lock/post-DML actor revocation, mandatory evidence rollback, deferred JWT/MFA expiry, raw tampering, bounded own history and migration replay. Actual signed issuer/gateway/identity/policy/device tests verify stopping after independent publication withdrawal through a fresh receiver process, denial of another global administrator/reviewer/device/internal caller, durable history recovery, rejected subsequent offers/reports, receiving-DML revocation rollback and storage failure. Real Chrome exercises the actual own-history and cancellation rewrites after a full page reload and publication withdrawal, then reloads the retained stop again. The browser fixture must forward these exact canonical purposes instead of returning its unrelated-module empty-list stub.

Completed local regression gates pass **3,610 distinct tests**: issuer 438, identity 356, gateway 102, policy 611, compatibility adapter 32, delegated quick-actions 393, frontend 991, native identity PostgreSQL 130, native licensing/policy PostgreSQL 255, full actual issuer/receiver PostgreSQL 217, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 41 and layout 5. The full issuer/receiver gate includes the thirty-nine native queue cases; those and the three-case actual cancellation slice are not counted twice. The final thirty-nine-case native proof also expands raw-SQL proof tampering and performs a genuine migration-012 receiving/offer followed by migration-013 adoption and cancellation, not only replay on an already upgraded schema. The final thirty-four-case client/panel slice and actual blueprint Chrome slice pass again without additional counting. The full native authentication gate completes in approximately sixteen minutes with real request/TOTP windows, not relaxed production limits.

The first full Chrome attempt passed the new blueprint cancellation flow but had one existing application-MFA setup assertion return 401 rather than 200. That unchanged case passes in isolation and the final unchanged full Chrome rerun passes all forty-one cases. Inspection shows the old fixture submits a previous-window OTP, making wall-clock rollover a plausible explanation, not a cause established by its status-only assertion. Neither the verifier's window nor production rate/security limits were changed. A diagnostic-only disposable-cluster startup failed before tests ran; a shorter temporary label starts normally and the isolated case passes. The earlier blueprint-only fixture failure was corrected by forwarding the two real canonical device purposes instead of fulfilling them with its unrelated-module empty-list stub, not by weakening the receiving contract.

The frontend type check retains exactly the seventeen previously recorded diagnostics, with no added diagnostics. Syntax checks pass for eight affected JS/CJS files; whitespace checks pass for all five new increment files and `git diff --check`. Both Compose configurations validate offline using disposable values. The Docker daemon is unavailable, so no container image/runtime claim is made. The final actual cancellation screenshot is visually inspected; owned test listeners, processes and disposable PostgreSQL clusters have exited. No production deployment, image build, live-device execution, external purchase or live Microsoft acceptance is performed. Automatic source-triggered/cross-owner cancellation, stale-command supersession, complete publisher planning/recovery, tenant/resource governance, effective-versus-draft runtime and independently observed endpoint effects remain required. The overall Entra-like development goal remains active.

## Forty-Third Implemented Increment: Read-Only Manufacturer Capacity and Bounded Reconciliation

### Separate Observation From Licensing Authority

Local catalog sizes and effective assignments were not a manufacturer subscription inventory. This increment adds an actual read-only collection process and durable comparison, not another entitlement authority, a successful-assignment badge or automatic pool expansion. The existing allocator/fulfillment worker remains separate. Neither a positive observation nor an observation failure changes local seats, user grants, provider mappings or observed individual-user fulfillment.

Microsoft documents `LicenseAssignment.Read.All` as the least-privileged application permission for [listing subscribed SKUs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/subscribedsku-list?view=graph-rest-1.0). Use a **separate application with reviewed read-only consent**, not the write-capable fulfillment application's secret. The collector requests the fixed tenant token endpoint and `.default` resource scope; that request does not independently prove the actual consent granted in Microsoft. The protected file's acknowledgement records an operational review requirement, not token attestation. No live consent or Microsoft tenant is configured here.

The collector retains only canonical SKU IDs/part numbers, target class, capability status, consumed units and the four explicit prepaid-unit counts. Microsoft's [SKU resource](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/resources/subscribedsku?view=graph-rest-1.0) and [unit-status definitions](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/resources/licenseunitsdetail?view=graph-rest-1.0) distinguish enabled, warning, suspended and locked-out units. These are subscription observations, not invoice/payment evidence, prices, confirmed service activation or exclusive OpenDirectory seat ownership. Other applications may consume the same tenant's units.

`GraphCapacityClient` has no user or license mutation method. It uses only the fixed global-cloud `subscribedSkus` GET with selected fields. Complete collection is bounded to sixteen pages, one thousand unique SKUs, bounded JSON per response and a ten-second overall deadline. Continuations must remain HTTPS on the exact Graph host/path with only a bounded opaque skip token and matching field selection. Redirects, other paths/origins, duplicate tokens/SKUs, partial/invalid counts, unknown statuses, delta links and incomplete collections fail rather than being summed or interpreted as zero. Token/transport failure clears the cached credential and never replays a page automatically. Additional Microsoft clouds are not silently accepted.

### Connector-Bound Collector Storage and Atomic Evidence

Policy migration **020** adds immutable source incarnations, one active source per connector/collector identity, retained capture attempts, derived audit and matching pending outbox. Sources bind an explicit existing connector, a database role and Graph application UUID. Source creation is disabled first, requires a reviewed offline reason, and rejects administrative/membership-bearing/schema-creating database roles. Bindings cannot be retargeted: changed applications or collector identities need a separately reviewed incarnation. Enablement is versioned; pause/re-enable cannot refresh old evidence.

`license-capacity-grants.sql` grants the collector only its source/capture purposes. RLS isolates source/run rows by the actual authenticated database identity. A private source getter checks that identity, never an assumed `SET ROLE`, and verifies the fixed connector/tenant/application file tuple. Security-definer routines pin their schema, catalog and temporary-schema order; temporary shadow tables cannot substitute connector ownership. Startup additionally rejects administrative/schema-owner identities and access to recipient, entitlement, provider-configuration, fulfillment or evidence tables. The existing fulfillment-worker startup guard also rejects capacity publication/configuration rights. Source setup remains a reviewed host/database provisioning duty, not an unauthenticated HTTP grant or a replacement for canonical tenant isolation.

A session advisory lock serializes collection for one connector. The first actual SQL insert records `checking` before external inspection, so a crash/outage cannot leave an older successful snapshot presented as current. A complete result requires the same enabled source/connector generations, valid normalized content and admission within thirty seconds of the SQL-derived start. Its five-minute validity is anchored to that start, not response completion or a later read. SQL rechecks known cutoffs at deferred commit. Source withdrawal during HTTP, storage/audit/outbox failure, expiry or a closed collector cannot acknowledge a successful capture. Failed results preserve a bounded code, not raw provider messages or credentials. A durable connector-wide HTTP-429 deadline survives fresh collector processes and source incarnations; raw insertion cannot bypass it. Graceful shutdown aborts the current read and makes no follow-up cloud write.

Audit/outbox rows derive from actual source/run transitions, not arbitrary caller event inserts. Capture/source identity, original start/deadlines and retained completed/failed results cannot be rewritten or deleted through the guarded path; migration replay does not restamp evidence. This trusts the configured collector, migration owner and database credentials: it is not cryptographic Microsoft attestation or protection against an arbitrary database/host owner. Outbox presence does not mean notification delivery or an off-host immutable archive.

### Bounded Comparison Without Automatic Rights

`GET /api/licenses/fulfillment/connectors/:id/capacity` requires a canonical human with `licenses.fulfillment.read`, not ordinary local license administration or a workload/application alias. The original bearer is checked before and after SQL; a revoked reader held behind the actual product-table lock receives no report. Query/body-selected tenant aliases and mutation/refresh requests are not accepted. No browser action makes a Graph request: it reads the separately collected SQL state.

Only explicit product mappings for that connector are returned, bounded to one thousand. The latest attempt wins: checking/failed attempts do not fall back to an earlier successful snapshot. Currentness additionally requires the active source/connector generations and unexpired observation. The report retains unknown/missing/paused/stale states explicitly. Local figures mean configured pool size and currently unexpired assignment-projection rows, not a fresh canonical directory/eligibility observation. A local `0` pool remains the existing unlimited convention, never zero purchased vendor seats.

For a fresh enabled user SKU/product only, the comparison shows `max(0, enabled - consumed)` as observed unassigned active units. This is deliberately not a reservation, assignment guarantee or proof that consumed units belong to this installation. Company SKUs, missing observations and nonenabled statuses do not yield positive headroom. Flags expose unlimited local pools and configured/unexpired local counts above observed enabled units, without automatically shrinking/removing grants or expanding the pool. Atomic vendor-capacity reservations, actual fulfillment admission, independently reviewed pool adjustments, billing/renewal/cost reconciliation and live service-level acceptance remain required.

The main license console exposes this read-only view to the existing narrow fulfillment reader. It requests no recipient list or credentials, makes no write, discards stale results on subject/permission changes and removes a positive headroom display at its known local expiry without automatically calling Graph again. Strict client contracts reject wrong connector/SKU identity, incomplete counts, contradictory headroom/flags, malformed/stale success and claimed allocation/fulfillment effects. A read/source failure is not an empty inventory or zero available seats.

### Reviewed Rollout

Apply migration 020 with coordinated policy/web/fulfillment-worker images; collectors do not run migrations. Provision a dedicated nonadministrative login without role memberships or schema/database creation. Grant only `scripts/license-capacity-grants.sql`, using `license_schema` and `collector_role` psql variables. As a reviewed schema owner, run `scripts/license-capacity-source.sql` with `license_schema`, `reason`, a known `connector_id`/exact `connector_version`, chosen `source_id`, `collector_role` and separate `graph_client_id`. Its SQL guard requires the reviewed connector to be enabled/current and creates a **disabled** source. Use `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1`; check the returned ID/version rather than assuming creation.

After independently checking Graph consent and file/database identity, explicitly enable only that source with its captured version and reason in a transaction:

```sql
BEGIN;
SELECT set_config('opendirectory.capacity_source_reason', 'Reviewed separate read-only Graph consent and collector custody', true);
UPDATE license_capacity_sources
SET enabled = true, version = version + 1
WHERE id = '<reviewed-source-uuid>'::uuid AND version = 1
RETURNING id, version, enabled;
COMMIT;
```

Run that example only in the reviewed license schema and require exactly one returned row. The owner-only nonsymlinked configuration file contains exactly `enabled:true`, `readOnlyConsentReviewed:true`, `sourceId`, `connectorId`, `tenantId`, `graphClientId` and `graphClientSecret`. Credentials are file-only and never stored in observation/audit/API/browser data. Use the separate `LICENSE_CAPACITY_DATABASE_URL`, `LICENSE_CAPACITY_CONFIG_HOST_FILE` and matching `LICENSE_CAPACITY_UID`/`LICENSE_CAPACITY_GID`. The hardened optional `license-capacity` Compose profile is **off by default** and mounts the existing file read-only without creating a missing host path. It cannot reuse the administrative service or fulfillment database identity. Explicit pause/versioned reissue and protected file/process rotation remain operational work, not automatic provisioning performed here.

### Verification

Owned PostgreSQL cases cover actual limited-role capture, RLS/cross-role denial, `SET ROLE` and temporary-schema attacks, complete/failed/checking precedence, source/connector generations, physical expiry, actual in-flight revocation, commit-time expiry after audit DML, mandatory evidence rollback, concurrent collection, durable throttling, immutable retention, reviewed provisioning-template execution, role startup and actual standalone-process pause/shutdown. Owned HTTP tests cover fixed read-only Graph paths, pagination, malformed/partial/count/redirect/credential failures and cancellation without writes. Actual signed issuer/gateway/policy cases separate reader/local-license duties and revoke the original reader while its SQL report is blocked. Real Chrome runs the separate restricted collector against an owned HTTP provider, observes actual SQL counts, leaves the local pool unchanged and shows unknown headroom immediately after an explicit source pause/reload. None of these fixtures is a Microsoft test tenant.

Final regression evidence comprises **3,690 distinct passing tests**: issuer 438, identity 356, gateway 102, policy 640, canonical license adapter 32, quick-actions 393, frontend 1,021, native identity PostgreSQL 130, native policy/licensing PostgreSQL 275, actual issuer/receiving-service PostgreSQL 218, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 41 and layout 5. Focused reruns, including the twenty actual capacity cases and one actual signed capacity-reader case, are not counted twice. The full issuer/receiving-service gate completed in 997,483 ms with no failed, skipped or cancelled tests. The frontend-wide TypeScript check retains exactly the same seventeen existing diagnostics, with an empty baseline diff; this is not a clean project-wide typecheck.

Initial native regression attempts exposed two real integration problems: migration 020's own transaction wrapper broke the runner's batch transaction/savepoint, and eight independent test files exhausted the native cluster's forty connection slots for restricted roles. The migration wrapper was removed, a regression now prohibits top-level migration transaction control, and independent native-file parallelism is bounded to two. Actual within-file concurrency/race tests and production privilege/pool limits were not relaxed. The final 640 policy and 275 native policy/licensing cases pass with these fixes. Narrow Git exceptions also retain all three required reviewed collector/fulfillment SQL provisioning templates instead of silently excluding them as database dumps.

Syntax checks pass for fifteen touched JavaScript/CommonJS files; all fourteen new increment files pass the explicit whitespace check, and `git diff --check` passes. Both Compose configurations validate offline with the optional capacity profile and disposable secrets. The Docker daemon remains unavailable, so no image build or container execution is claimed. The real Chrome capacity screenshot was visually checked; the final process/listener audit finds no owned test process or Node/PostgreSQL/Chrome listener left behind. The existing local-settings diff and deleted legacy callback page remain unchanged.

No production migration/deployment, live Microsoft operation, external purchase or device execution is performed. Tenant isolation, source-aware device cancellation, vendor-capacity admission/reservations and the other remaining production boundaries are not completed by an inventory report. The overall development goal remains active.

## Forty-Fourth Implemented Increment: Conservative Vendor-Write Admission

### Connect Inventory to the Actual Addition Boundary

A comparison report was not an allocation gate. Policy migration **021** now rejects a new Microsoft addition intent without both current complete enabled user-SKU inventory and an explicitly reviewed finite admission budget for the immutable product/source generation. A local catalog size, unlimited local pool, provider-administrator bearer, captured positive inventory alone or ordinary retry cannot grant that budget. Adoption/observation of an already-present direct assignment is not a new addition. Known owned removals deliberately remain possible without a budget or current capacity inventory.

Each budget is an immutable, conditionally chained review generation. Its initial units are derived by SQL from the named latest capture's `max(0, enabled - consumed)`, not a submitted number. A connector advisory transaction lock serializes charges across different recipients. The latest source, connector and product generations, enabled state, whole latest capture, five-minute capture lease, user target class and enabled SKU status are checked under actual SQL locks. Newer checking/failed captures supersede success. Warning/suspended/company/missing SKUs are not free seats. A separate read-only collector cannot publish budgets, read recipients, reserve units or admit a dispatch; the fulfillment role cannot publish inventory, edit a budget or directly change its charge ledger.

The finite budget is a **burn-down admission quota**, not an automatically renewable snapshot allowance. Every accepted addition charges it once. A new capture, known/unknown provider failure, receipt expiry, target withdrawal, process crash, successful provisioning observation or ordinary retry does not erase that charge or refill the budget. Current inventory independently constrains remaining admission: writes during/after the capture, undispatched attempts and outcomes not separately confirmed before that capture remain uncovered liabilities. A confirmed owned direct assignment observed before a newer capture need not be double-subtracted from that capture's headroom, but still consumes its original budget unit. This does not attribute every tenant subscription unit to OpenDirectory.

### Atomic Intent and One-Time Pre-POST Receipt

The actual `in_flight` addition transition derives its retained charge, audit and matching pending outbox in the same SQL transaction as existing write intent. Direct initial in-flight target insertion and the old worker protocol fail closed. Missing evidence, concurrent over-admission, known cutoff reached behind actual audit DML or commit failure cannot acknowledge a successful intent. The original attempt/target generation, budget, capture and deadline cannot be rewritten, refunded or deleted through the guarded path. Indexes cover budget heads, budget/target charges and actual observation outcomes; migration replay does not restamp them.

Before its Graph POST, the worker must consume exactly one receiving SQL receipt. Dispatch refreshes the local desired state, checks the captured attempt/target generation, subject enablement, current budget/source/connector/product state and latest usable inventory, and records dispatch admission with mandatory evidence. Its deadline is at most ten seconds from reservation, capped by the capture and known local entitlement expiry; SQL rechecks that deadline after audit DML at deferred commit. Parallel claims cannot admit the same receipt twice. Source withdrawal, binding reissue, local withdrawal or physical expiry before dispatch blocks POST without returning the charge to circulation. An admitted receipt is not observed external success; the existing subsequent Graph inspection remains required. Failed/unknown writes retain the existing explicit review and ownership rules.

Dispatch uses the allocator's bounded SQL transaction/lock limits rather than an unbounded autocommit lock wait. The process stop flag is checked again after the awaited receiving SQL operation: shutdown during admission cannot start a later POST, even if the receipt already committed. Such a retained charge is not silently refunded or called a successful provider write.

SQL and Microsoft are **not one distributed transaction**. A change after the final committed SQL checkpoint but before/during HTTPS cannot be instantaneously withdrawn, and other applications can consume the same vendor capacity. The gate trusts the dedicated worker's fixed Graph implementation and database/host custody; a receipt is not cryptographic proof that an arbitrary credential holder honored it. No manufacturer-exclusive reservation, guaranteed Graph consistency, undo of an accepted effect or automatic external purchase is claimed.

### Explicit Offline Review, Not Ambient License Administration

Only the actual provisioned table-owner identity, without assumed role, can use the budget insertion path. `scripts/license-capacity-budget.sql` requires the explicit license schema, immutable product ID, exact current product version, named latest capture/run ID, captured previous budget ID (empty only for the first generation) and a bounded reviewed reason. Its acknowledged review means checking external usage and **all** previous attempts. The script qualifies the actual schema and pins temporary-schema order. Use `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1` as the reviewed owner, inspect the returned IDs/units/generations, and never guess versions or repeat a failed conditional review blindly.

Rebase needs a later current capture, not reuse of the old evidence. Previous attempted targets must have stable retained actual observation events for their current target generation before that capture, showing either absence or a confirmed owned active matching direct assignment. Unfinished, unknown, verifying, missing/mismatched or later observations cannot be waived just because a lease expired. The old budget and all charges remain history. An explicitly reviewed new generation starts only with the SQL-derived then-observed headroom. Mandatory budget-review audit/outbox and deferred capture cutoffs apply to this operation too.

This is a trusted **offline database-owner review acknowledgement**, not server-verified Microsoft consent/stock attestation, a canonical MFA-authenticated independent reviewer workflow or enforced separation between two human custodians. Such application-level review/governance, commercial evidence and automated credential rotation remain outstanding. The script is not an HTTP grant, a new global licensing role or permission to expand the paid/local pool.

### Console and Coordinated Rollout

The existing narrow fulfillment reader receives capacity report **schema version 2**. Each explicitly mapped product additionally exposes only the reviewed quota ID, reviewed/charged/remaining unit counts and binding-generation currentness. No recipient inventory, provider secret, Graph token or review-mutation control enters this report. Strict client validation rejects old inventory-only protocol, incomplete/contradictory quota counts and impossible active-source claims. The console distinguishes retained local quota from observed stock and warns that missing/exhausted/withdrawn quota or stale inventory blocks additions. Positive observed headroom is neither a budget nor reserved vendor capacity. Existing expiry/subject-change/late-response fencing remains in place.

Stop old workers and independently reconcile already admitted/in-flight vendor calls before migration: SQL cannot cancel a POST an older process already admitted. Apply migration 021 and coordinate policy, web and fulfillment images. Reapply the narrow `license-fulfillment-grants.sql` for the dedicated role's `license_capacity_dispatch(uuid,uuid)` function only; it receives no direct capacity-table read/write grant. Provision/review the separate source and budget before expecting new additions. Startup rejects accidentally granted capacity configuration/publication/ledger rights; new old-protocol addition intents are rejected by SQL. Both optional worker/collector profiles stay off by default. Existing legacy attempts without ledger evidence are not retrospectively invented or silently reclassified; stable actual outcomes need review before the first budget. No production rollout is performed by this increment.

### Verification

Actual native SQL and owned Graph HTTP cases cover missing/zero budgets, two different concurrent recipients for one unit, latest checking/failure/expiry, company/suspended/missing SKU, source/product/connector generations, local withdrawal/physical expiry, parallel one-time receipt claims, mandatory intent/dispatch/budget evidence and deferred cutoffs. Uncertain real HTTP outcomes remain charged across fresh captures and restarts; successful observations do not automatically rebase a budget. Actual owned removals still work without usable capacity. Restricted collector/worker roles cannot gain ledger/configuration access, and raw SQL cannot forge protocol/evidence or rewrite retained charges. Conditional reviewed rebasing and migration replay exercise the real supplied SQL templates, not mock storage.

The actual signed issuer/policy/gateway chain now observes addition denial before offline review, then an actual separately authenticated read-only collector, finite budget, addition and separate successful observation, while preserving license-duty separation and original-reader revocation. Real Chrome observes missing quota, a blocked actual worker attempt, explicit offline review, a subsequent actual addition, retained quota counts and source-pause denial without browser credentials or a budget mutation UI. These are owned fixtures, not a live Microsoft tenant.

Final regression evidence comprises **3,723 distinct passing tests**: issuer 438, identity 356, gateway 102, policy 640, canonical license adapter 32, quick-actions 393, frontend 1,032, native identity PostgreSQL 130, native policy/licensing PostgreSQL 297, actual issuer/receiving-service PostgreSQL 218, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 41 and layout 5. The native policy/licensing gate includes twenty-two new admission cases; the frontend adds eleven strict-contract/console cases. Focused reruns, including current actual signed-provider acceptance and actual SQL-template/cutoff proofs, are not counted twice. The full issuer/receiving-service gate completed in 957,014 ms, full actual Chrome in 249,084 ms and final native policy/licensing in 34,880 ms, with no failed, skipped or cancelled cases in those final gates. The final 640 policy tests and 55-case focused authority check pass. The frontend-wide typecheck still reports exactly the same seventeen existing diagnostics with an empty baseline diff; this is not a clean project-wide typecheck.

Initial integration checks caught an overly strict new intent trigger that tried to re-admit a legitimately superseded in-flight attempt during local withdrawal. It now charges only a fresh attempt transition, preserving the original receipt while pre-POST generation checks reject obsolete additions; the existing actual compensation case passes. The owned browser fixture was extended to assert exactly its original removal followed by the newly tested addition, rather than incorrectly accepting removals only. Its cleanup closes the owned listener even after a failure. The report test's no-capture invariant is scoped to its actual connector, not another test's legitimately collected inventory. An initial full Chrome run returned 401 in the unchanged MFA-enrollment case, and a later unit run returned 401 instead of the expected 403 in an unchanged mocked ordinary-user denial. Subsequent complete Chrome/policy runs and the focused authority run pass without changing those assertions, weakening production MFA or relaxing authorization. These intermediate failures are not represented as continuously green evidence.

Final syntax checks pass for nine touched JavaScript/CommonJS files; all three new files pass the explicit whitespace check, both actual SQL rollout artifacts are visible to Git, and `git diff --check` passes. Both Compose configurations validate offline with the optional collector/fulfillment profiles and disposable secrets. Docker remains unavailable, so no image build/container execution is claimed. The latest real Chrome quota screenshot was visually checked; the final process/listener audit finds no owned acceptance process or Node/PostgreSQL/Chrome listener remaining. The pre-existing local-settings diff and deleted legacy callback page are unchanged. No production migration/deployment, live Microsoft operation, external purchase or device execution is performed. Manufacturer-exclusive reservations, canonical human capacity-review governance, tenant isolation and the other remaining production boundaries are not completed by this local admission gate. The overall development goal remains active.

## Forty-Fifth Implemented Increment: Canonical Tenant Ownership and Lifecycle Anchor

### First Establish Actual Ownership

Identity migration **024** adds retained tenant identities, disabled-first conditional lifecycle generations and immutable ownership of the existing canonical user/group/OU UUIDs. It creates **no default tenant**, copies no credential-store identities, guesses no ownership from names/domains/headers and automatically adopts no existing data. This is not a parallel directory. New and historical unowned records remain metadata, not positive canonical authentication or license-eligibility authority, until explicit review. Existing global administrative roles do not become tenant-scoped merely because their holders receive ownership.

Provisioning/adoption/enablement are deliberately reviewed offline database duties, not a new global license/directory-administrator permission. `services/core/identity-service/scripts/tenant-provision.sql` requires `identity_schema`, a chosen `tenant_id`, bounded immutable `external_key`/`display_name` and reviewed `reason`; it creates a disabled tenant. `tenant-adopt.sql` additionally requires the exact `expected_version` and a JSON `manifest` of only `{kind,resourceId}` objects. The manifest is bounded to one thousand explicit canonical user/group/OU entries. `tenant-enablement.sql` requires exact tenant/version, explicit `enabled` and reason. Use `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1`, inspect returned identities/generations and preserve captured preconditions; do not blindly retry with a guessed current version.

The guard checks actual database-session ownership privileges, reviewed reason and explicit acknowledgement; an assumed `SET ROLE`, ordinary directory role, extra manifest field or accidentally granted adoption function is not sufficient. This is an **offline custody acknowledgement**, not an independently MFA-authenticated human approval workflow or proof that commercial/legal tenant ownership was externally verified. Review existing roles, PIM, memberships, app assignments and other privileges before enabling adopted accounts. Protected role/bootstrap custody is not automatically provisioned by these scripts.

Ownership cannot be moved, erased or retargeted. Canonical user/group/OU UUID updates are rejected, and owned deletions retain both the binding and a derived retirement so an old namespace cannot be reused. A coherent existing membership/OU component must be adopted together; unresolved or differently owned references cannot be made to match by guessing an owner. Large connected components require a separately reviewed bulk-migration/lock-capacity plan, not disabling the guards or assuming this bounded manifest is a bulk performance guarantee. References created later must remain within one owner. Sorted resource advisory locks serialize adoption against membership changes, while actual row locking/tombstone checks preserve deletion safety. A migration replay changes neither reviewed ownership nor historical generations/evidence.

### Parent Authority Is a Real Generation Fence

Canonical human authorization and subject-binding now require an owned user under an enabled tenant as well as an enabled individual record. Positive authority frames use **schema version 5**; the issuer rejects the old pre-ownership version 4 instead of treating an older identity image as a compatibility bypass. Inactive/unlinked frames remain purpose-limited negative results. Coordinate identity and issuer images: an unmigrated source or a down-level positive frame is unavailable, not a fabricated active identity.

Adoption and each reviewed tenant lifecycle generation derive new existing user authorization/eligibility revisions. Ordinary profile edits and client-supplied generation fields cannot manufacture or reset them. Tenant pause does not overwrite the individual's `enabled` flag, and re-enable cannot revive an old access token, refresh family, captured authorization code, approved human OAuth-device grant or old entitlement origin. The established issuer context/family checkpoints carry that fence through existing direct/gateway consumers; this is not a claim that every receiving service is already tenant-aware. Individually disabled people remain disabled after parent re-enable.

Actual user eligibility, license delegate/reviewer/activation-reviewer and policy-publication actor sources also use effective parent authority. A paused or unowned account is a known ineligible identity, not a source outage silently converted to a positive result. SQL/storage failures remain failures. Effective eligibility events/outbox agree with the parent state even if the underlying individual flag is toggled. Group frames advance their real revision on parent transitions and retain explicit inactive members, so a paused group does not masquerade as a deleted or empty unknown inventory. The existing bounded group leases and later provider reconciliation remain necessary; tenant pause does not synchronously undo already accepted external/device effects or publish the outbox.

Canonical application-target metadata additionally requires the captured actor and selected user/group to have the same actual owner. Foreign selection yields no positive target revision, including for an actor holding the canonical global administrator role. Same-owner membership snapshots exclude foreign/unowned groups. This closes those explicit reference/admission paths; it does not retrospectively establish OAuth-client ownership or universal tenant resource authorization.

### Evidence and Rollout Boundaries

Tenant configuration, adoption and retirement derive mandatory actor-attributed audit and matching pending outbox from actual SQL transitions. Parent changes, user authorization/eligibility evidence and group changes commit together. Missing receiving evidence cannot acknowledge a tenant pause or adoption. Security-definer helpers pin the actual schema/catalog/temporary-schema order and expose no caller-selected tenant override. This trusts migration/host owners and the configured identity implementation; it is neither protection from arbitrary DBA/DDL control nor an off-host immutable archive.

Stop public issuance and coordinate migration 024 plus v5 identity/issuer images before rollout. Keep tenants disabled while reviewing the real adoption manifest and privileged bootstrap identities; explicitly enable only the reviewed generation afterward. No automatic production adoption or emergency HTTP bypass is supplied. Newly created ordinary metadata records likewise need reviewed ownership until a future canonical tenant-bound creation workflow exists. The automatic allocation hook in owned regression fixtures is **test-only**, uses explicitly chosen isolated fixture owners and is never installed by a production migration or service startup.

**Full multi-tenant isolation remains unimplemented.** Directory collection/profile and subject-link administration, global role/PIM/authentication/policy configuration, OAuth-client/application ownership, license pools/provider configuration, devices, workload identities and remaining receivers still need canonical tenant resource boundaries. Credential-subject mapping remains a separately privileged global operation, not a tenant-scoped administration grant. This increment must not be used to advertise a shared multi-customer deployment, isolated directory reads, tenant-scoped global administrators or a tenant administration portal. Tenant pause currently fences canonical human authority and the supported user/group sources, not every workload/device credential or previously accepted effect.

### Next Required Acceptance Gate

1. Derive a purpose-bound tenant authorization context from this canonical ownership, capture it in interactive/pending/session/workload origins and require it at receiving APIs; no body/header-selected authority or silent unscoped-token fallback.
2. Explicitly adopt pools, applications, provider mappings, policy/device resources and other existing cross-service records; validate actor/target/reviewer ownership rather than creating another independent directory.
3. Enforce the boundary on direct and gateway reads, lists/counts, writes, joins, reviews, reconciliation and evidence, with appropriately restricted database identities. Existing global administrator labels cannot substitute for a resource's tenant authority.
4. Prove real separate-tenant operators, including administrators, cannot read or mutate one another's resources through SQL races, direct APIs, gateway aliases, workload sources or browsers. Preserve independent duties, live revocation and truthful unknown/outage behavior throughout.

### Verification

Actual PostgreSQL cases cover no default ownership, disabled-first provisioning, exact bounded/coherent adoption, pause/re-enable and effective eligibility generations, retained individual disablement, cross-owner membership/OU references, an actual administrative actor's rejected foreign application target, concurrent adoption/member insertion and deletion, immutable IDs, mandatory evidence rollback, limited-role/assumed-role denial even after function grants, temporary-schema attacks and replay without restamping. The actual supplied owner SQL templates run in those cases.

Actual signed issuer/identity/policy/gateway cases pause one separately owned tenant, reject its old access/refresh at all supported boundaries, leave another tenant's user active, reject a body-selected tenant alias and require fresh authentication after re-enable. Pending codes and already approved human device grants cannot mint a family across that transition. Real Chrome loses only the paused tenant's session, rejects restored old cookies after re-enable and signs in afresh, while the other tenant's browser stays active. These are owned local fixtures, not a production rollout or proof of complete cross-tenant data isolation.

Final regression evidence comprises **3,751 distinct passing tests**: issuer 439, identity 356, gateway 102, policy 640, canonical license adapter 32, quick-actions 393, frontend 1,032, native identity PostgreSQL 154, native policy/licensing PostgreSQL 297, actual issuer/receiving-service PostgreSQL 220, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 42 and layout 5. The increment adds twenty-four actual tenant SQL cases, two actual issuer/receiver cases, one down-level authority-contract case and one actual browser case. Focused reruns, including the final twenty-four-case ownership/role-spoof proof, are not counted twice. The full issuer/receiving gate completed in 945,017 ms, real Chrome in 242,874 ms, final native identity in 20,047 ms and final native policy/licensing in 27,889 ms; final native/Chrome gates have no failed, skipped or cancelled cases. The frontend typecheck retains exactly the same seventeen existing diagnostics with an empty baseline diff, not a clean project-wide typecheck.

Initial checks exposed a shared trigger's wrong record-field access, missing SQL UUID casts and an adoption-function alias collision; the typed statements and table-specific branches were corrected at their source. A new deletion advisory lock initially created an actual membership/delete deadlock. It was removed in favor of existing row/FK/tombstone serialization and adoption's resource-row touch; the original deletion race still requires successful deletion, only the expected missing-user rejection and no orphan, and passes unchanged. Legacy-corruption setup now disables both applicable validators only while constructing its explicitly historical invalid row; normal new-reference denial is not relaxed. One intermediate unit run had two socket-hang-up failures; subsequent complete 356-case runs pass without changing those assertions. Earlier broad runs were deliberately superseded when review identified the missing identity-source version fence; final runs require v5 and explicitly reject v4 rather than silently retaining pre-tenant behavior.

Final syntax checks pass for eighteen touched JavaScript/CommonJS files. All six new files pass explicit whitespace and Git-inclusion checks, including the three reviewed SQL templates; `git diff --check` passes. Both Compose configurations validate offline with optional collector/fulfillment profiles and disposable secrets. Docker remains unavailable, so no image build/container run is claimed. The real Chrome reauthentication screenshot was visually checked as a login-flow fixture, not tenant administration or data-isolation proof. Final process/listener inspection finds no owned acceptance process or Node/PostgreSQL/Chrome listener left behind. The existing local-settings diff and deleted legacy callback page are unchanged. No production migration, automatic adoption, deployment, live Microsoft operation, purchase or device execution is performed. Full tenant resource/subject-link isolation and the other remaining boundaries are still required; the overall goal remains active.

## Forty-Sixth Implemented Increment: Tenant-Bound Subject-Link Administration

The canonical credential-to-person linking receiver now rejects attaching credentials to another tenant's person, removing another tenant's binding and recycling an unlinked subject across owners, even with a global role label. This increment is not a claim of complete directory/resource isolation.

Implemented sequence:

1. Require positive private identity-source protocol v6, with the actual immutable owner's exact tenant UUID, version and generation; retain the existing exact signed canonical identity context.
2. Add a fixed, uncached `/oauth/tenant-state` proof. Validate the original human access token, live session/client/revocation, canonical identity/role/application/MFA authority, and bind the response to its exact issuer, subject, token ID, expiry and identity context. Application/demo/old-source responses cannot invent a tenant. No request field selects the tenant.
3. Use that proof for the existing subject-link mutation receiver. Derive the tenant from the original actor, lock and validate canonical local authority after waits, require same-owner targets, recheck the original token after DML, and retain mandatory transaction-local evidence. Self-unlink requires another operator rather than a generic revocation bypass.
4. Retain an immutable issuer/subject-to-tenant binding after unlink and user deletion. Actual link/adoption transitions claim only an already explicitly owned user's tenant. Backfill only already owned links, without adopting users or inventing a default owner; reject conflicting historic bindings. Enforce local authority/snapshot/deadline again at deferred commit.
5. Verify genuine two-owner SQL operations, original-token denial/rollback, retained subject ownership, concurrent waits, evidence failures, down-level contracts and signed issuer/receiver behavior. Record actual results below, not intended checks.

Global directory reads/writes, role/PIM/policy administration, clients, devices, licensing resources and other receiving planes still require equivalent tenant scopes. This step must not be deployed or described as isolated shared-customer hosting. Pending outbox evidence is not delivery or an off-host audit archive; original-token HTTP checks and PostgreSQL commit are not a distributed transaction.

### Implemented Contract and Rollout

`OIDC_TENANT_STATE_URL` is operator configuration for the fixed `/oauth/tenant-state` path, not a request parameter or a caller-selected service. Only the original header bearer is forwarded; redirects, query aliases, API keys, alternate token purposes, workloads, unbound/demo humans and down-level source data cannot acquire a tenant. A positive response contains exactly the schema/active flags, original issuer/subject/token ID/expiry, the existing exact four-field canonical identity context and the exact three-field tenant UUID/version/generation. The receiving client checks all origin fields and rejects extra fields, stale expiry, malformed ownership, unavailable/oversized/non-JSON responses and timeouts without a cache or fallback. A negative live proof is an authentication denial, not an invented default company.

The existing `POST /api/identity/links` body remains exactly `{issuer, subject, userId}`. `DELETE /api/identity/links` now accepts exactly `{issuer, subject}`, with no ignored user/tenant/approval selectors; clients sending extra fields must update. The trusted issuer is configured, the target must be actually owned by the original actor's enabled tenant, and nonexistent/unowned/foreign targets receive the same bounded not-found rejection. Ordinary role labels and supplied profile identifiers do not replace the original canonical identity. The current session's own subject cannot be unlinked through this handler; a second operator must do it, avoiding a general 'ignore revocation' escape hatch. The ordinary user disable/delete/reset flows retain their separately defined semantics.

Admission obtains current original-token tenant authority before the transaction, after target/tenant/user lock waits and after link DML. The transaction holds the actual enabled tenant's shared row lock and the original actor's canonical user lock, verifies the original subject/link/user revisions and current canonical role fingerprint/assurance, and derives its SQL context server-side. Deferred receiving guards compare the original local tenant/user/link/retained-subject binding, a canonical role/policy/review snapshot including physical role/review expiry, and the original token/role-MFA cutoff again. Link, retained namespace, existing link audit, verified-actor tenant events and pending outbox commit or roll back together. No bearer or credential is stored in that evidence. Other issuer session/application/MFA changes are rechecked online, not atomically serialized with PostgreSQL commit; this is not protection against an arbitrary database/host owner or a distributed transaction.

Migration 025 retains issuer/subject ownership without a cascading user foreign key. Linking to an already explicitly owned user or subsequently adopting an existing unowned user claims its known tenant; unknown owners are never guessed. Unlinking and user deletion do not release that namespace to another tenant. A same-tenant replacement uses a new canonical link generation, not revival of an old token. Replay backfills only already explicitly owned live links and preserves existing binding timestamps; conflicts fail instead of moving or deleting retained ownership. Canonical positive v6 sign-in also joins the retained binding, so a historical privileged import that bypassed transition guards cannot become fresh foreign-tenant authority. A wrong original tenant binding has no ordinary remap API; a separately reviewed repair design remains required.

Guest or external cross-tenant access still needs a separately reviewed guest/reference and effective-tenant authorization model; it must not be implemented by moving the retained home subject binding.

Apply migrations through 025 and coordinate identity/issuer images. Positive private v5 is intentionally rejected rather than silently treated as tenant-aware. Compose configures the new internal endpoint; native installations must configure `OIDC_TENANT_STATE_URL` explicitly when the issuer is not at the default service hostname. This does not auto-run adoption scripts, activate tenant administrators, start event delivery or authorize shared-customer deployment.

### Verification
