Fourteen new actual PostgreSQL cases cover original-actor same-tenant writes, uniform foreign/unowned denial despite global administration, retained ownership after unlink/user deletion, raw-SQL cross-owner rejection, same-owner replacement, immutable binding/replay timestamps, adoption of old links, exact fields/duties/self-unlink, original revocation after DML and during an observed real target-lock wait, deferred role loss, physical original-token expiry, concurrent first claims, conflicting privileged historical adoption, missing mandatory event/outbox rollback and positive-source rejection of a historically imported foreign link. Production transition guards are disabled only while constructing those explicitly invalid historical fixtures, never to make an ordinary assertion pass.

Twenty-four new strict receiving-client cases check exact token/context/tenant matching, purpose and transport bounds, down-level/extra data, negative authority, outages, timeout, expiry and no cached fallback. Four new issuer contract cases reject private v5 or missing/malformed tenant metadata. Three new actual signed-issuer/receiver cases verify token-bound tenant metadata, foreign link/namespace denial and a real issuer revocation while actual link audit DML waits; the latter proves rollback of the link, retained namespace and both evidence planes rather than only middleware denial. These are owned local fixtures, not a Microsoft tenant or a production migration.

Final regression evidence comprises **3,796 distinct passing tests**: issuer 443, identity 380, gateway 102, policy 640, canonical license adapter 32, quick-actions 393, frontend 1,032, native identity PostgreSQL 168, native policy/licensing PostgreSQL 297, actual issuer/receiving-service PostgreSQL 223, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 42 and layout/driver 5. The full issuer/receiving-service gate completes in 947,346 ms, final native identity in 25,957 ms, final native policy/licensing in 27,732 ms and final actual Chrome in 237,147 ms, with no failed, skipped or cancelled cases in these final gates. Focused and earlier complete reruns are not counted twice. The final frontend typecheck still reports exactly the same seventeen existing diagnostics with an empty baseline diff; it is not a clean project-wide typecheck.

Initial native execution caught a reserved SQL alias in the new role snapshot; the alias was corrected at its source. Two focused harness starts failed with an overlong macOS Unix-socket path (110 characters from the supplied run label); the shorter owned-cluster label runs the actual test successfully without changing its assertions. An early broad run was deliberately cancelled when review identified the need to join retained subject ownership in positive canonical sign-in, not because a polling observation expired. The final complete native and Chrome gates load that predicate, and an actual historical-import case proves the negative source behavior. These intermediate executions are not presented as continuously green evidence.

Syntax checks pass for thirteen touched JavaScript/CommonJS files; all five new files pass whitespace and Git-inclusion checks, including migration 025. `git diff --check` and both offline Compose configurations with optional capacity/fulfillment profiles pass. Docker remains unavailable, so no image build/container run is claimed. The local Git HEAD contains the observed GitHub `main` (`0ea42c7a53c50462a0de41ab0fd1e2ce24c833ff`) plus 253 commits, with no remote-only commit at verification; the existing dirty worktree is retained, not reset or automatically synchronized. The existing local-settings diff and deleted legacy callback page are unchanged. No commit, push, production migration/deployment, live Microsoft operation, purchase or device execution is performed. Remaining directory/resource tenant boundaries are still required; the overall goal remains active.

## Forty-Seventh Implemented Increment: Tenant-Bound Ordinary Directory Reads

The ordinary directory GET receiver now derives its sole tenant from the original verified human token, including user/group/OU collections/details, targeted user role history and aggregate identity search. Counts, pages, membership IDs and OU parent references are scoped in actual SQL, not filtered after global data reaches a browser. Global administration does not bypass this read boundary. This is not a claim of complete CRUD or shared-customer isolation.

Implemented sequence:

1. Add one canonical read receiver using the existing fixed issuer tenant proof before SQL and again after the read. Validate the original canonical identity/link/role context, retained subject ownership, current tenant generations and role assurance inside one repeatable-read, read-only transaction. Discard results on final revocation/outage or origin change.
2. Route every ordinary directory GET, user-role history and identity search through it. Keep explicit SQL ownership predicates for rows and counts. Reject foreign/unowned/missing details uniformly, do not expose imported foreign member/parent IDs, and reject incoherent/cyclic OU trees rather than inventing root relationships. Apply finite collection/depth boundaries without silently truncating results.
3. Require durable ownership, including in demo mode; no unpartitioned memory fallback. Reject ignored tenant selectors and unsupported LDAP selector parameters instead of pretending to implement them. Let the main user console negotiate the read protocol before requesting profile data from a down-level receiver.
4. Verify genuine two-owner collections, counts/search/detail/history/trees, original-token revocation after an actual read wait, storage/protocol failures and actual signed issuer/browser behavior.

### Read Contract and Rollout

The protected GET routes are `/api/users`, `/api/users/:id`, `/api/users/:id/roles`, `/api/groups`, `/api/groups/:id`, `/api/ous`, `/api/ous/:id` and `/api/identity/search`. This does not cover every GET under identity governance, licensing or other services. They use the fixed `/oauth/tenant-state` protocol introduced in increment 46; callers cannot select an issuer, tenant, alternate backend or fallback by body/header/query aliases. Supplied tenant headers do not affect ownership. A human token must have the exact canonical context and the operation's actual permission, not merely a role-looking scope string or workload claim.

`DirectoryReader` checks current local positive identity/retained-subject/role/MFA/tenant evidence against the original token in the same repeatable-read, read-only transaction as the selected data. Each statement inherits the canonical one-second source timeout. Original live authority is checked again after releasing the SQL connection and before any positive response is serialized; expiry, origin/generation changes, revocation or an outage discard the result. This neither locks the issuer into a distributed commit nor promises that every target row remains unchanged after its authorized snapshot. The directory transaction cannot mutate SQL or stamp a new license/source entitlement.

User count/filter/pagination predicates include actual owner UUIDs before limits. Groups and their selected member IDs are read together; an imported foreign/unresolved member is not disclosed. OU reads reject a foreign/missing parent instead of leaking its ID or pretending the node is a root; forest serialization also rejects cycles and paths beyond 256 nodes. Groups, OUs and each aggregate-search component have a 10,000-row boundary and reject excess rather than claiming a silently truncated complete collection. User paging retains its existing 100-row limit; role history retains its explicit bounded/truncated contract. General group/member/OU pagination and enterprise-scale/performance guarantees remain work, not implied by these boundaries.

Only user list `page`, `limit`, `search` and `enabled` selectors are implemented. Other detail/collection/search selectors are rejected. In particular, the old aggregate search's ignored `filter`, `base` and `scope` parameters now fail instead of pretending to provide LDAP filtering. Real scoped LDAP protocol/filter support is still required. Missing, unowned and foreign canonical detail UUIDs are indistinguishable not-found results; invalid UUID syntax is a bad request. Authorized tenant readers may still see their own disabled people as directory records, not positive sign-in/eligibility authority.

`/api/identity/access` advertises `directoryReadProtocol: 1`. Main user list/detail and targeted role-history clients negotiate it immediately before reading and require a non-null canonical actor ID; they neither cache that capability nor send a chosen tenant. An absent, old or malformed read protocol stops the target request before its data is fetched. This capability is not availability or ownership attestation for an arbitrary malicious backend; the receiver still enforces real authority. Other API clients require coordinated rollout and the same scoped backend.

Identity and web images must be upgraded together, with migrations through 025 and the increment-46 issuer's configured `OIDC_TENANT_STATE_URL`. Explicit demo mode no longer makes unpartitioned in-memory directory GETs a valid source. Legacy demo writes are not advertised as tenant-scoped creation. The old unused global collection/search/tree helpers were removed instead of left as a fallback; mutation-only target helpers remain part of the next write-boundary work.

**Write boundary remains open:** ordinary creation and user/group/OU/membership/role mutations still require same-tenant target admission, actor-derived new ownership, exact origin fences and carefully preserved self-revocation semantics. In production, a newly created unowned resource is intentionally invisible to the new scoped reads until explicit offline adoption; runtime creation must be fixed next rather than assigning a guessed default owner or weakening reads. Existing isolated test fixtures explicitly predeclare their own new resources, which is not production creation governance. Do not deploy this staged change as complete directory isolation or a finished Entra replacement. Licensing/device/policy/client and other tenant/resource planes remain required as well.

### Verification

Fourteen new actual two-owner PostgreSQL cases verify scoped collections/search/totals, user pagination and literal filters before SQL limits, identical foreign/unowned/missing detail and history denials, foreign historical membership suppression, coherent OU forests, foreign historical parents and same-owner cycles, final revocation/outage, an observed actual OU read-lock wait, one repeatable-read count/page snapshot across a concurrent owned insertion, actual SQL read-only enforcement, stale canonical/tenant evidence, rejected selectors/workloads/unbound actors and parent pause/re-enable. Historical corruption is constructed only with the relevant transition guard temporarily disabled in owned test setup; ordinary production assertions do not disable it.

Twenty new receiving-unit cases cover explicit owner predicates, original/canonical binding, fresh proof on both sides, live assurance, actual transaction options, no durable-storage fallback, physical expiry and finite hierarchy/collection limits. Eleven new frontend contract cases ensure unsupported/malformed protocol and unbound demo data cannot cause target profile requests, profile/history reads negotiate without selecting a tenant, and malformed profile IDs fail before any request. Two new actual signed issuer/receiver cases verify global-admin cross-owner count/detail/search/history denial and original issuer revocation during an observed read wait, with no records released afterward. One new actual Chrome case displays only its separately owned tenant's people and proves foreign UUID/query/header behavior at the real receiver. These are isolated owned fixtures, not live Microsoft acceptance or proof of the still-open write planes.

Final regression evidence comprises **3,844 distinct passing tests**: issuer 443, identity 400, gateway 102, policy 640, canonical license adapter 32, quick-actions 393, frontend 1,043, native identity PostgreSQL 182, native policy/licensing PostgreSQL 297, actual issuer/receiving-service PostgreSQL 225, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 43 and layout/driver 5. The full issuer/receiving gate completes in 919,039 ms, native identity in 20,392 ms, native policy/licensing in 33,358 ms and the final actual Chrome gate in 237,235 ms. Final native/Chrome gates have no failed, skipped or cancelled cases. Focused, earlier and repeated cases are not double counted. The frontend typecheck still has exactly the same seventeen existing diagnostics with an empty baseline diff; this is not a clean project-wide typecheck.

Initial acceptance correctly rejected legacy placeholder role actors without real subject links; those fixtures now create actual canonical role principals rather than bypassing the new guard. The native paging fixture's explicitly owned reader is now counted as a real directory person (32 rather than 31 records). Legacy demo tests now assert unavailable tenant reads instead of accepting unpartitioned memory; no production fallback is restored. An existing first-factor enrollment confirmation timed out in one initial full Chrome run. The subsequent unchanged complete 43-case Chrome gate passes without weakening MFA, extending the failed assertion or replacing the test with a narrow substitute; the intermediate failure is not presented as continuously green evidence. ## Forty-Eighth Implemented Increment: Original-Actor Tenant Ownership for New Resources

The canonical creation receiver now closes the unowned-creation gap left explicit in increment 47. New users, groups and OUs receive the verified original creator's tenant in the same transaction as the real new row, not a caller-selected/default company or the offline adoption marker. Existing ownership and retired UUIDs remain immutable; runtime creation cannot adopt old resources.

Implemented sequence:

1. Add a dedicated original-token creation receiver for the existing three POST routes. Validate exact whitelisted input, generate a new server UUID, require canonical human creation permission, derive ownership only from the fixed live issuer proof, and recheck original canonical authority after locks and DML. New users remain disabled and groups empty.
2. Add migration 026 with a narrowly nested new-row ownership claim. Preserve actual-owner offline review for existing-resource adoption. The runtime claim needs original local identity/role/tenant evidence, the exact server-generated kind/UUID/content and actual database ownership; no caller body or ordinary limited SQL role can select an owner or reuse this branch for an existing/retired namespace.
3. Commit the new row, immutable owner, verified-actor tenant creation event and pending outbox together. Recheck local source/role snapshot, known token/role-MFA deadline and exact new-row content at deferred commit. Lock/validate OU parents in the same tenant. Do not claim distributed serialization with issuer revocation or a delivered event.
4. Negotiate a dedicated creation capability in the main user console before POST, and verify actual production ownership rather than letting the isolated fixture hook supply it. Check two-tenant creation/readback, immutable/offline boundaries, limited SQL roles, concurrent waits, revocation, evidence/content/deadline rollback and real signed issuer/browser flows.

Existing-resource updates/deletes, membership and role mutations still need complete tenant-bound admission and preserved self-revocation behavior. Other licensing/device/policy/client/workload/guest planes remain open. This increment must not be presented as complete CRUD, shared-customer isolation or a finished Entra replacement.

### Creation Contract and Rollout

The canonical `POST /api/users`, `POST /api/groups` and `POST /api/ous` now use `DirectoryCreator`, not the old unowned or demo-memory creation helpers. They require durable canonical storage and the exact original human actor's creation permission. UUIDs are generated by the server; neither query/body tenant selectors, supplied IDs nor workload/demo identities choose the owner. User input remains the whitelisted profile fields; enablement, credentials, roles and owner fields are rejected. Group input is exactly a bounded `name` and optional `description`; OU input adds an optional canonical `parentId`. Names are at most 255 characters and descriptions at most 2,000, with control-character validation. New users remain disabled, without credentials/subject links/role assignments; new groups are empty. No license is allocated or externally provisioned by creation.

The receiving transaction holds the actual enabled tenant and original creator's user row, checks current canonical identity/link/retained-subject/role/MFA state against the original context, and locks a same-owner OU parent when requested. The fixed issuer proof is rechecked after those waits and again after DML; the original bearer is never persisted or replaced by an ambient service token. Every creation has one exact server-generated resource kind/UUID/normalized-content intent and a transaction-local role/tenant/original-actor snapshot and known token/role-MFA deadline. This is not a durable cross-service transaction or proof against an arbitrary host/database owner; an issuer-only withdrawal after the last HTTP checkpoint is not atomically serialized with PostgreSQL commit.

Migration 026 claims ownership only inside the actual new resource's BEFORE INSERT path. The nested claim must match the exact new kind/UUID/content and enabled original tenant, and cannot find a live old row, existing owner or retired namespace. It requires actual database ownership and no assumed role; an INSERT-capable limited login cannot launder even a complete copied proof through definer triggers. The API never sets the offline adoption review marker. Existing adoption still requires the original explicit actual-owner review and exact manifest; no historical resource or new tenant is adopted/provisioned automatically.

Ownership creation records a distinct `resource-created` tenant event with verified issuer/subject/canonical creator ID, current tenant version/generation and a SHA-256 digest of normalized content, plus its matching pending outbox. The retained ownership column `reviewed_version` carries the observed tenant version here, not a claim of independent human offline review; runtime and offline origins have distinct action/reason evidence. Ordinary core user/group audit/outbox remains mandatory as before. Deferred guards recheck original local authority/roles/time, exact actual new-row content and owner, and reject late changes or a suppressed/missing resource. The receiving service also demands a matching actual owner/event/outbox/actor/content receipt before reporting success, so absent/disabled runtime guards or fixture-only adoption cannot impersonate the new contract. Pending evidence and the legacy post-commit bus publication are not confirmation of broker delivery or downstream effects.

Migration replay preserves existing owners, generations and creation evidence. Transaction-local creation/claim flags must clear even on the same physical connection, and do not turn subsequent raw SQL into adoption. Historical/raw unowned records continue to require explicit reviewed offline adoption; the old transition/tombstone guards are not relaxed. Existing global profile-name/email constraints are unchanged; tenant-specific profile/login namespaces, verified domains and guest/external references need their own design. Runtime creation does not implement guest access, independent administrator approval, bulk transactions or general idempotent retry recovery.

`/api/identity/access` additionally advertises `directoryCreationProtocol: 1`. The main user console confirms it with a non-null canonical actor before POST, without requiring unrelated profile-read rights or sending a chosen company. An old/malformed capability sends no creation request; new-code/old-schema mismatch is also rejected by the receiving receipt. Coordinate identity/web images and apply migrations through 026; the existing v6 identity-source and increment-46 tenant-state endpoint remain required. Other API callers require the same upgraded receiver. The isolated adoption fixture now explicitly skips runtime creation, and acceptance separately uses no fixture hook at all. These tests do not turn test-only default ownership into production behavior.

### Verification

Twenty-two new actual PostgreSQL cases run the production migration and receiver in an isolated schema with **no fixture adoption hook**. They cover disabled owned users with immediate scoped readback and no credentials/roles, empty actual group membership, original-owner groups/OUs and normalized parent UUIDs, uniform foreign/missing-parent rejection, exact fields/duties/workload/unbound rejection, post-DML revocation and mandatory owner/user evidence rollback, observed real parent and namespace-advisory waits, deferred role loss and physical token expiry, exact user/group/OU content drift, pre-existing and retired UUIDs for all three kinds, migration replay, context cleanup on the same physical connection, missing runtime guards/receipt denial, copied proof without offline review, and actual limited-login/assumed-role denial even with a complete valid proof and ordinary INSERT/sequence grants. Offline adoption of an actual old row remains possible only through its genuine supplied reviewed script.

Twenty-six new receiving-unit cases check strict profiles/group/OU input, generated IDs, original/canonical permission and assurance, parent scope, actual receipt requirement, suppressed writes, no memory fallback, expiry and late original revocation. Eight new frontend contract cases ensure old/malformed creation capability or unbound demo metadata causes no POST and that a confirmed client sends profile fields only. Existing create-only UI assertions now include the second capability check while still proving that every GET is metadata, not unrelated profile access.

Three new actual signed issuer/receiver cases create users/groups/OUs under two real original tenants, prove non-default runtime ownership rather than fixture allocation, deny foreign parents/readback and caller owner aliases, roll back real creation/ownership/evidence after issuer revocation during an observed actual audit wait, and reject a down-level receiving schema without a fake ID. A new actual Chrome case creates in its separately owned tenant, confirms immediate disabled readback and genuine runtime origin evidence, and rejects another tenant's reader. The older browser reader's exact expected set now includes the explicitly added creator principal. These are owned local fixtures, not a live Microsoft tenant or production migration.

Final regression evidence comprises **3,904 distinct passing tests**: issuer 443, identity 426, gateway 102, policy 640, canonical license adapter 32, quick-actions 393, frontend 1,051, native identity PostgreSQL 204, native policy/licensing PostgreSQL 297, actual issuer/receiving-service PostgreSQL 228, browser mediation 39, actual Chrome 44 and layout/driver 5. The full issuer/receiving gate completes in 1,015,573 ms, final native identity in 20,537 ms, native policy/licensing in 29,187 ms and actual Chrome in 240,200 ms. Final native/Chrome gates have no failed, skipped or cancelled cases. Earlier/focused reruns are not double counted. The frontend typecheck retains exactly the same seventeen existing diagnostics with an empty baseline diff; it is not a clean project-wide typecheck.

Initial gates identified legacy unbound creation fixtures, an old placeholder audit subject, changed insert-returning expectations in the SQL fake and the main create-only console's additional metadata request. Fixtures now use their actual canonical creator and returned SQL row rather than relaxing production identity/receipt checks. The existing exact missing-name error contract is preserved. Demo tests now reject creation instead of inventing a company in memory. Review also added an explicit matching receiving receipt so a missing SQL guard cannot be treated as success; actual SQL and signed-provider down-level cases prove that rejection. No final assertion, MFA rule or original-token checkpoint is bypassed to obtain passing evidence.

Syntax checks pass for twelve touched JavaScript/CommonJS files; all five new files pass explicit whitespace/Git-inclusion checks, including migration 026. `git diff --check` and both offline Compose configurations with optional capacity/fulfillment profiles and disposable secrets pass. Docker remains unavailable, so no image build/container run is claimed. The final process/listener audit finds no owned acceptance process or Node/PostgreSQL/Chrome listener left behind. The existing local-settings diff and deleted legacy callback page are unchanged. No commit, push, production migration/deployment, live Microsoft operation, purchase or device execution is performed. Existing-resource mutation isolation and the other remaining platform requirements are still open; the overall goal remains active.

## Forty-Ninth Implemented Increment: Tenant-Bound Existing User Mutations

Canonical user profile/status changes, authorization invalidation and deletion now use `UserMutator` and identity migration `027_directory_user_mutations.sql`. An existing global administrator role or guessed strong ETag cannot authorize a different tenant's user. This receiving boundary does not infer ownership from request fields, headers, a default company or demo memory.

### Receiving Contract

- `PUT /api/users/:id` retains exact profile fields and `users.update`; any `enabled` field additionally needs `users.status.manage`. Profile/status writes require the existing exact strong user ETag: missing `428`, malformed `400`, stale/mismatched `412`. Caller-selected tenant, roles, credentials, revisions and self-reduction flags are rejected.
- `DELETE /api/users/:id` requires the separate `users.delete` duty and current strong ETag. Deletion removes the canonical user and actual subject links, not external vendor accounts. Ownership, subject/tenant binding, retirement and negative identity evidence survive.
- `POST /api/users/:id/authorization/invalidate` retains its empty-parameter, separately authorized `users.sessions.invalidate` contract. It advances current canonical authorization monotonically without accepting profile content or requiring profile-read access. It deliberately does not require a caller ETag: the receiver instead captures the actual locked before-version for this non-restorative action.
- Mutation query selectors are rejected. Foreign, unowned and missing valid target UUIDs return `404` before target data or version is released; there is no fallback to a global legacy lookup.
- `/api/identity/access` advertises `directoryUserMutationProtocol: 1`. The main user console negotiates it before each edit/delete/reset, refuses down-level/unbound/demo authority, preserves conditional writes and supplies no client-selected owner. Reset-only operators still need no user-profile GET.

The core gateway now preserves the canonical `/api/identity`, `/api/users`, `/api/groups` and `/api/ous` namespaces and configured `IDENTITY_SERVICE_URL`, rather than using the generic prefix-to-`/api` rewrite and a portless hard-coded host. Original bearer and conditional headers are forwarded unchanged; allowed browser origins can send `If-Match` and read `ETag`. Receiving identity authorization still decides permissions and tenant scope; the gateway grants no additional directory duty. This fixes the broken gateway transport found by the new actual signed mutation checks, not an alternative authority path.

### Original Actor, Data and Evidence

The receiver obtains the existing fixed-path, uncached original-bearer issuer tenant proof before opening a durable transaction. It locks the exact enabled tenant/version/generation, then the unique actor/target user rows in sorted UUID order with ownership predicates. This avoids opposite actor/target lock order and never waits for a foreign user's row lock. It reads canonical authorization/role/MFA state using the same SQL client and rechecks original issuer authority after lock waits.

The actual before-row is normalized in SQL so every bigint generation/revision stays a string, including above JavaScript's safe integer range. A server-generated operation UUID binds that exact row, normalized expected public values, action, original issuer/subject/context/tenant, raw role snapshot and physical token/held-role-MFA deadline. Existing user update/delete/reset implementations execute on the same outer client; none may commit separately.

Receiving triggers enforce actual database-owner sessions without assumed roles, one exact DML transition, same target/owner/before-version and only requested fields. They create `identity_directory_user_events` and a pending `identity_directory_user_outbox` atomically. Each retained event includes verified actor, operation, owner/target/action, scope digest, before/after record versions and content hashes, not the bearer or copied public profile. A mandatory matching event/outbox receipt prevents fake success when receiving guards are missing. Evidence has no user foreign key that could erase it on deletion.

Deferred admission checks actual final row content, immutable metadata and unchanged parent generation, exact record/authorization/eligibility advances, core record/security/negative-eligibility audit/outbox, raw role snapshot and physical token/MFA deadline. A second unrequested transition, cleared receiving scope, missing evidence or late role/deadline change rolls back all effects. Migration replay does not restamp users, owners or evidence. Trusted offline/raw maintenance remains separate; these guards do not claim protection from a compromised host or actual table owner.

### Legitimate Self-Reduction

Own disable, reset and deletion remain possible. The local deferred guard derives this case only from the admitted original actor UUID, actual target/action and locked before/expected values, never a caller boolean. Before DML, the actor must still have normal positive canonical authority. At commit, disable/reset must have the exact reduced own row and original link incarnation; deletion must have the actual absent user/link, original-subject unlink audit, retained tenant binding/owner, retirement and negative evidence. The original role snapshot and physical deadline still apply. Ordinary own profile editing is not an authority-reduction exception.

There is no weaker issuer endpoint, skipped revocation check or service-token substitution. The normal post-DML original-bearer tenant proof uses the issuer's separate SQL connection: before local COMMIT it still sees the original uncommitted local user/link state. Independent issuer token/session/client/MFA/application withdrawal remains visible and rejects the transaction. After COMMIT the reduced canonical generation closes old access and refresh normally; re-enable cannot resurrect them. These cross-service checkpoints are not distributed issuer/SQL commit serialization.

### Verification and Rollout

Final verification: **3,963 distinct passing tests**, including 59 new cases in this increment. Focused runs and repeated full runs are not added again. All final native suites report zero failed/cancelled/skipped tests.

| Gateway contracts, including five new routing/header cases | 107 |

| Delegated quick-actions | 393 |
| Frontend, including 11 new mutation-protocol cases | 1,062 |
| Native identity PostgreSQL, including 34 isolated mutation cases without a fixture ownership hook | 238 |
| Native policy PostgreSQL | 297 |
| Full actual signed issuer/PostgreSQL regression | 234 |
| Browser mediation | 39 |

The initial broad issuer run exposed the gateway transport bug with three unchanged positive/foreign assertions. After the actual routing correction, all seven focused signed checks and the fresh **234/234 full issuer regression** pass; the latter finished in 1,018,670.77 ms. It verifies actual direct/gateway owner isolation, own negative actions, original-token withdrawal during own-reset audit SQL and missing-receiver denial alongside the existing issuer/role/MFA/PIM/application/licensing paths. The final 47/47 Chrome run finished in 242,990.05 ms and native identity 238/238 in 29,792.32 ms. Source hashes confirm the fifteen relevant implementation/contract files stayed unchanged during final verification. Final logs are under `/tmp/od-mutate49-*` on this development machine.

Syntax and tracked/new-file whitespace checks pass. Frontend type checking still reports exactly the same 17 pre-existing diagnostics, with an empty diff against the prior captured baseline; it is not a clean type-check claim. Tests use disposable native PostgreSQL and owned HTTP/Chrome fixtures, not Docker deployment, production-origin acceptance, live Microsoft acceptance or purchased licenses. The separate production gateway transport limitation identified below remains unresolved.

Apply migration 027 after 024–026 and coordinate identity/gateway/web images with the existing verified issuer tenant-state configuration. Older user consoles must not bypass capability negotiation. No new external service, purchase, automatic tenant adoption or Microsoft operation is enabled by this change.

Group/OU mutations, memberships, role writes and other licensing/device/policy/client/guest planes remain required. This increment does not establish complete tenant isolation or an Entra replacement. Notification/outbox delivery, off-host evidence, emergency restoration and independent lost-factor recovery remain separate work.

## Fiftieth Implemented Increment: Canonical Tenant-Bound Group Management

This increment must include real administration rather than only another hidden security layer. The original `/usergroups` page read the separate `/api/auth/groups`, invented object identifiers/types and substituted `MOCK_GROUPS` on empty/error responses. That component is now replaced: the sidebar exposes **Gruppen**, the page reads actual original-tenant `/api/groups`, and authorized creation uses the existing disabled/empty-first canonical creation receiver. Empty/error responses never become example administrators, distribution lists or dynamic groups.

### Active Receiving Boundary

- New `GroupMutator` and migration `028_directory_group_mutations.sql` implement conditional group profile/delete/member-add/member-remove operations in one original-token/tenant-bound transaction. They lock owned actor/member rows in UUID order before the owned group, capture exact before state/strong group ETag, retain actual membership incarnations and refuse foreign coherent group/member pairs even for global administration.
- SQL still generates a fresh membership UUID on every insert, including raw caller attempts to supply a retired UUID. The receiver captures that actual generated identity after early admission rather than weakening the existing generator to accept client/Node-selected source identities. Only one admitted root and its exact membership-induced group updates/cascade are allowed; unrelated user/profile changes are blocked.
- A required finalizer validates actual final content, core group audit/outbox, original membership origin and pending independent first approval where configured, then writes immutable verified-actor group events and pending outbox. Deferred checks revalidate physical role/token/MFA deadlines and the final evidence digest. Original issuer withdrawal after DML rolls back source identity, rows and outbox together. Readback uses the scoped directory projection, not the global helper that can expose foreign legacy IDs.
- New `directoryGroups` implements strict real read/create/edit/member/delete envelopes, strong ETags, current-version response confirmation and separate protocol checks. The new page includes real forms and destructive confirmations, exact member UUIDs without unrelated profile reads, actual pending-review messaging, empty/unavailable/stale states and own-access-loss warnings. It does not infer effective app/license permission from linked-member counts or confirm an external Microsoft operation.
- Public group PUT/DELETE and member POST/DELETE now invoke `GroupMutator`; the global group helper and unscoped target-user lookup are removed from these handlers. Creation/detail/mutation responses carry actual strong group ETags, and the identity access response advertises `directoryGroupMutationProtocol: 1`. Unsupported query/body owner selectors remain rejected. There is no public memory or legacy write fallback.
- Public membership-review routes now use `ScopedGroupReviews`, with original bearer proof on reads and writes and separate `groupMembershipReviewProtocol: 1` negotiation in the console. Known policy/review targets, recipients, original grantors, policy creators and named reviewers must belong to the original tenant. SQL applies that predicate before the review queue limit; foreign legacy rows cannot crowd an owned review out of the queue or appear in a beneficiary's own history. A known owned but incoherent policy fails unavailable instead of becoming a fabricated absent policy.
- Migration `029_scoped_group_reviews.sql` admits one exact review root and only its actual capture/version/denial effects. Required immutable event/pending outbox, original local context/raw roles and physical MFA/token/voter deadlines are checked at commit. Policy replacement never resets an old lease. Policy capture rejects missing/incoherent origins and retained foreign grantors in both the application and SQL receiver; it cannot silently adopt them. Original group UUID/member source, unanswered cutoffs and independent first approval remain binding.

This replaces the previously identified coherent-foreign-pair gap: mixed-owner constraints alone did not stop tenant A changing an otherwise internally coherent group/member pair in tenant B. Both active group mutation and review receivers now derive the target boundary from the same verified original actor, not a selected resource or an `admin` label. Group administration still does not confer protected review configuration/voting authority. A missing receiver guard cannot report a successful mutation, and the down-level capability test still keeps browser controls disabled.

Current verified active-boundary evidence includes all 1,104 frontend cases, 426 identity unit/API cases, 107 gateway cases and 301 native identity/PostgreSQL cases, including 34 isolated group-mutation and 29 scoped review cases. The review cases include an actual 101-row foreign overflow ahead of an owned row, retained foreign provenance, limited SQL login/assumed-role rejection, late cutoff tampering, physical expiry, migration replay and observed policy/group/user lock waits with original withdrawal, recipient disable/delete and group deletion. All 49 actual Chrome cases also pass against the final receiving source with zero failures/cancellations/skips (257,961.34 ms); the final native identity run takes 43,813.74 ms. Unchanged issuer-unit (443), policy-unit (640), native policy (297), quick-actions (393), license-adapter (32), browser mediation (39) and layout (5) gates pass. Focused/repeated runs are not counted again as distinct cases.

The first full signed-issuer run completes 240 cases with 231 passes and nine failures, so it is not counted as an accepted gate. The later failures explicitly hit the unchanged identity receiver's 1,000-per-15-minute limiter after the expanded suite's additional real HTTP work; preceding provider-source failures also remain part of that failed run. The fixture now observes actual remaining/reset response headers and waits between cases with a finite reserve. It does not increase/disable production limits, retry rejected operations or change expected receiving outcomes; a final assertion requires zero identity 429 responses. The corrected full rerun at `/tmp/od-group50-active-signed-full2.log` passes **all 240 cases** with zero failures/cancellations/skips in 1,359,915.28 ms, including actual issuer/client/MFA/role revocation and licensed group reconciliation. It visibly waits 301,589 ms at 149 remaining receiving requests rather than exceeding the quota. Combined with the other named gates, increment 50 has **4,076 distinct passing cases**, without counting focused/repeated runs again. Its migration/runtime source hashes remain stable in `/tmp/od-group50-active-source.sha256`; the separate transport increment below has its own gate and is not silently included in that earlier receipt.

Actual Chrome tests now exercise read/create/edit/add/remove/delete, stale-form 412 with explicit recovery, separate destructive confirmation, retained membership origins, reader versus group-only duties, foreign known-UUID writes and no legacy/profile reads. The missing-capability case deliberately removes the mutation protocol from a real metadata response rather than pretending the active receiver is down-level. A generic old browser intercept returning `200 []` for `/api/groups` was corrected to forward this namespace to the actual server. The active CRUD screenshot `/tmp/od-groups50-active-console.png` has been visually inspected. Focused actual signed tests additionally prove foreign coherent denial, strong versions through the gateway, guard failure rollback and legitimate group-derived application self-removal/deletion. Independent issuer revocation while self-removal's actual audit DML waits still rolls the whole operation back; the revocation fixture uses that application's own client credentials, not the unrelated portal client's deliberately opaque no-op revocation response. Type checking still reports the same 17 diagnostic messages; this is not a clean type-check, production rollout or live vendor acceptance.

An additional unchanged legacy `services/platform/api-backend` test invocation fails all 25 cases at module loading because that installation cannot resolve `pg`; it is not the canonical license adapter and is not counted among passing gates. No unrelated source/dependency repair or fully green repository-wide test claim is made. The canonical `services/core/license-management` adapter separately passes all 32 cases.

### Required Receiving Work

1. Scope group metadata update/deletion and member addition/removal from the same fixed original-bearer tenant proof as user mutations, never a caller tenant or selected group. Require canonical human permissions, live original issuer and same-client local authority checks after waits and DML. Remove public-path use of global/memory helpers; foreign/unowned/missing groups and member targets must remain indistinguishable.
2. Preserve the existing group revision, with an exact strong group ETag on conditional metadata/member/deletion requests. Membership removal must bind the current source incarnation rather than deleting an unrelated re-grant by an old recipient UUID. Verify version advances from real receiving SQL; do not allocate client revisions, automatically retry stale forms or return a cached success.
3. Use one outer transaction for the existing membership-review/origin logic and all group DML/evidence. Lock actor/member user rows in sorted order before group rows, compatible with current membership reviews and user disable/delete. Do not introduce a resource advisory lock on user deletion that recreates the previously tested adoption/membership deadlock. Parent tenant lifecycle and foreign resource locks must remain bounded.
4. Bind server-generated operation, exact before/expected-after group/member content, same-tenant owner, original actor/context/raw roles and physical token/role-MFA deadline to receiving evidence and a mandatory atomic pending outbox receipt. Allow only the exact group updates induced by the admitted member transition; a generic 'skip nested trigger' or 'ignore revocation' flag is not acceptable. Keep actual group revision/audit/outbox, retired IDs, membership origins and deletion evidence coherent and retained.
5. Scope membership-review policy targets, referenced reviewers, known review reads, lists and decisions to the original tenant too. Preserve independent first approval, current policy/lease versions, unanswered cutoffs and immutable source identity. Group/member administration must not silently become review configuration/voting authority, and neither a global role nor a review denial may mutate a foreign group.
6. Keep actual application-group and license-group semantics: linked membership is not automatically effective access when first approval/review is pending. Own removal or group deletion may legitimately end the actor's group-derived application access after commit, without accepting an independently withdrawn token before commit or substituting an internal credential. A local mutation receipt does not confirm external license assignment/removal.

### Required Visible Feature

- Build a strict canonical group client and replace `/api/auth/groups`, fabricated identifiers/types and all sample fallback with verified `/api/groups` data. Negotiate the existing read/create contracts and a separate receiving group-mutation capability before any write. A legacy receiver cannot be treated as tenant-safe because its response happens to look plausible.
- Implement real group creation, metadata editing, known-UUID member addition/removal and separate destructive deletion confirmation with current versions. Render only capabilities actually granted by the verified directory access response. A group-only administrator must not need unrelated user-profile read permission; show exact member UUIDs instead of invented people when names are not authorized.
- Represent the supported flat canonical group model honestly. Do not label it distribution/dynamic/AD security governance, infer effective membership from a count or describe local group changes as completed Microsoft provisioning. Show actual pending-review results and link to the existing review workflow rather than fabricating approval.
- Preserve explicit loading, empty, denied, stale, unavailable and unconfirmed states, including after a failed reload. No local selection/delete animation, toast or placeholder row is a receiving confirmation. Include recovery guidance when an action ends the actor's own access.

### Required Evidence Before Calling It Implemented

- Native PostgreSQL: two real owners; foreign coherent group/member pairs; foreign legacy IDs; exact stale versions/incarnations; missing guards/audit/outbox; late content drift; physical role/MFA/token expiry; migration replay and pool-scope cleanup; independence and tenant scope of membership reviews; observed concurrent lock waits with user disable/delete, group deletion and review operations. Demonstrate rollback, not only rejected HTTP status.
- Real signed issuer/gateway: same-tenant positive operations, foreign denial even for global administration, original revocation during actual DML, group-derived application self-reduction, unchanged bearer/ETags and down-level receiver failure. Verify actual local license-source snapshots/review cutoffs remain coherent without making a live vendor call.
- Actual browser: reader versus group-only administrator versus review duties; real empty/error responses without examples; create/edit/add/remove/delete with confirmation and version preconditions; denied known foreign UUIDs; no user-profile/legacy-auth requests by a group-only administrator; no automatic retry or success after an unconfirmed response. Broader unchanged regression gates must stay green.

This implements the stated canonical flat-group receiving and visible-management boundary, not complete platform tenant isolation. Existing OU mutations, role/PIM/policy/client/device/license/guest tenant scopes, universal least-privilege defaults and the other stated platform gaps still remain in the original objective.

## Fifty-First Implemented Increment: Production Gateway Transport

This step fixes the production gateway receiving path, not all remaining resource tenant boundaries. The previous CORS callback rejected every originless production caller before bearer verification, including legitimate server-to-server directory requests, and recommended an already retired API-key path. A second issue became visible only through the actual proxy: downstream identity-service CORS overwrote the gateway's approved origin with `*`, breaking the intended browser policy even when preflight was correct.

### Active Fix And Receiving Evidence

- An absent `Origin` now continues to ordinary signature/live-state/receiving authorization without gaining browser CORS headers. Empty, `null`, suffix-lookalike, wrong-scheme or wrong-port origins fail 403, even if `null` or a trailing empty entry was accidentally placed in configuration. The approved-origin list is not expanded.
- A common wrapper covers every registered HTTP proxy, including canonical direct namespaces and generic service proxies. It removes all downstream `Access-Control-*` response headers, preserves the gateway's own CORS decision, and merges `Vary` without losing `Origin`, compression or a downstream `*`. Receiving ETags, status, cache-control, original bearer and version preconditions are unchanged. This is not a new credential or resource-permission bypass.
- Originless public API documentation and OpenAPI now advertise only actual OIDC bearer access and the existing ingress/authenticated/public limiter tiers. Protected service/module routes are no longer labelled public, and OpenAPI explicitly exempts only its liveness path. API-key issuance remains retired; client-type, service headers, cookies and origins do not authorize protected requests.
- All **120 gateway cases** pass, including eight production-mode CORS cases and five proxy-header contracts beyond the earlier 107. The dedicated `npm run test:gateway:production` gate in `services/core/oauth-provider` now contains **nine actual native cases**, including role withdrawal without restoration by fresh tokens. It uses the actual issuer/JWKS/access-state receiver and actual identity/PostgreSQL DML, not mocked proxy or authorization results.
- Native cases prove originless conditional group CRUD and exact actor receipts; missing/wrong-purpose/retired credentials and ordinary-user denial; actual allowed-origin ETag exposure and precise preflight; foreign coherent tenant denial even for global administration; independent original revocation after admission during observed audit DML; live-state outage; and missing receiving guard rollback. The first native attempt exposed the downstream wildcard override and failed two cases; neither failure was hidden by relaxing the expectations.

The first expanded full run at `/tmp/od-prod51-signed-full.log` finishes 248 cases with 241 passes and seven failures; it is not accepted. Earlier role-governance tests legitimately invalidate/withdraw the shared portal administrators, and the new production cases incorrectly assumed those old actors still had `groups.create`. Their 403 responses were correct security behavior, not a receiving bypass to remove. Production positive cases now use explicit separate canonical group-only actors. The foreign-denial case uses a new, audited, finite offline fixture bootstrap for a dedicated global actor followed by real optional MFA enrollment and normal issuer sign-in; it does not restore any withdrawn historical grant or accept fabricated token roles.

An actual governance-order reproduction first runs the independent global-admin review/cleanup, then all nine production cases. **All ten cases pass** with zero failures/skips/cancellations in 21,634.70 ms (`/tmp/od-prod51-authority-order2.log`). A new case independently withdraws a real group-administration source: the old token fails 401, and a newly issued token still fails the write with 403 even with forged administrator/client-type headers. An earlier wider reproduction also selected a PIM test without its prerequisite setup and failed; that selection is not counted as a passing gate or repaired by changing production permissions.

The final standalone production gate also passes **all nine cases** with zero failures/skips/cancellations in 6,528.04 ms (`/tmp/od-prod51-signed-final3.log`). The corrected complete regression at `/tmp/od-prod51-signed-full2.log` now passes **all 249 cases**, with zero failures/skips/cancellations in 1,371,223.32 ms. This includes all nine production cases after the full preceding MFA/PIM/review lifecycle, not only the shorter order reproduction. It visibly waits 301,587 ms at 149 remaining identity requests and passes the zero-429 assertion without relaxing the receiver limits.

The dedicated production cases are included by the normal signed suite/root CI because they live in its existing native receiver file; the focused script/order reproduction is not substituted for the complete gate. The final source hashes, including public documentation/OpenAPI and separate canonical actor fixtures, remain unchanged throughout the corrected full run (`/tmp/od-prod51-source-final2.sha256`). Together with the other named regression gates from increment 50 and the new 120-case gateway gate, the current verified set contains **4,098 distinct passing cases**. Focused/order/repeated runs and the separate diagnostic of the still-missing role/PIM tenant boundary are not counted again. The earlier group-only 240-case full receipt remains at `/tmp/od-group50-active-signed-full2.log`.

### Acceptance Scope And Deployment Prerequisites

The production mode here is the gateway process handling actual HTTP requests; the test issuer/identity fixtures remain explicit native fixtures. It is not a Next production build, Docker image, live Microsoft tenant or production deployment. Coordinate the gateway image containing both the origin callback and proxy wrapper with identity/web protocol migrations, a correct `CORS_ORIGIN`, canonical `OIDC_ISSUER`/`OIDC_AUDIENCE`, reachable JWKS/access-state endpoints and explicit identity-service URL. Never work around an authority outage with API keys, a wildcard browser origin or an internal credential. Known TypeScript failures and unavailable Docker still prevent any complete rollout claim.

The implemented transport acceptance requirements are:

1. Admit requests without an `Origin` to normal authentication in production without granting them CORS browser access or trusting client-type/service/API-key headers. Preserve the explicit allowed-origin list and reject disallowed or `null` browser origins.
2. Keep canonical signature, access-purpose/audience, uncached original-token state and receiving permissions mandatory. Do not exchange a human bearer for a background service credential or turn an origin check into authentication. Preserve canonical directory namespaces, `If-Match`, ETags and error statuses through the proxy.
3. Add an actual production-mode gateway gate for authorized originless reads/conditional writes, missing/retired/withdrawn credentials, allowed/disallowed browser origins, preflight version headers and response exposure. Prove both same-tenant success and foreign-target denial at the real receiver, not only a mocked proxy handler.
4. Remove misleading API-key transport guidance and document coordinated deployment prerequisites and the difference between a native production-mode transport test and a successful container/Next production rollout. Existing TypeScript failures and unavailable Docker are not deployment acceptance.

Unscoped OU/role/application/device/policy/license/guest planes, mandatory least-privilege defaults and the license-provider gaps below remain part of the original platform objective after this transport step.

## Fifty-Second Increment (In Progress): Tenant-Bound Authority Lifecycle

The next complete security boundary is direct role administration together with its PIM, role-review and authentication-policy alternatives. Migration 032 now scopes the actual direct-role/PIM target paths and PIM queue to the current canonical actor home, including SQL receiving defenses. `RoleReviews`, `RoleAuthentication`, `PrivilegedAccessPolicy` and the live global-policy role source still lack the complete owned governance/receiving boundary. A partial target fence is useful defense, not a substitute for the coupled source, policy, receipt and console cutover below.

### Observed Gap, Not A Passing Security Gate

The original isolated native PostgreSQL diagnostic applied all 29 then-current migrations, provisioned two genuinely distinct enabled tenants, explicitly adopted two canonical users and obtained the administrator's actual `AuthorizationState` context. The pre-032 `DirectoryRoles.grant` accepted tenant A's actor granting `license-manager` to tenant B's user; B's actual canonical role source acquired `license-manager`, and the role audit correctly retained A as actor. `PrivilegedActivation.grantEligibility` also accepted B's target. Retained ownership and a correct actor audit did not themselves fence these authority mutations. These new transitions now fail under migration 032; historical/imported contaminated positive source and global policy coupling remain separate unresolved problems.

The original diagnostic and receipt remain `/tmp/od-authority52-probe.cjs` and `/tmp/od-authority52-probe.log`. Their successful diagnostic exit confirmed an **unwanted accepted transition**, not a passing isolation test, and is excluded from all passing counts. Current native and actual signed production-mode receiving tests require **404 plus unchanged foreign authority, rows and evidence**. Candidate/source-plan tests that need pre-fence contamination now explicitly import that historical SQL with only the new target trigger disabled for fixture setup, restore it immediately, and then test quarantine without pretending the current public receiver accepted the foreign grant. No production import/bypass endpoint is added.

### Coupled Source Inventory

| Source / receiver | Present coupling | Required boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `DirectoryRoles` and direct grant/revoke routes | Current enabled canonical actor home, same-home deterministic user locks, current role/MFA recheck and SQL target fence; original bearer checked before transaction and after effects. Global policy/source remains. | Uncached original-bearer tenant proof and exact owned source/conditional root/effect/receipt through commit, including legitimate reductions. |
| `PrivilegedActivation` and catalog/self/queue/eligibility/request routes | Target/self reads and owned request/eligibility transitions are scoped; queue filters precede limits; activation's saved reviewer locks stay owned. Original bearer is checked before each write transaction and after effects. Catalog/governance policy remains global. | Full owned policy and original receiving lineage, independent reviewers, exact source versions/receipts and deferred original-authority checks. |
| `RoleAuthentication` | One mutable row per global role; MFA policy joins affect every holder of that role. Writes now require original-bearer pre-/post-effect checks and final fresh assurance. | Independently versioned tenant/role policy, fixed protected-duty MFA minima and exact original-actor receiving admission. |
| `PrivilegedAccessPolicy` | One global activation row per role, shared policy versions/reviewer UUIDs and a global eligible-admin search. | Owned role policy, owned named reviewers and an actual same-tenant activation/recovery path. |
| `RoleReviews` and SQL attachment helpers | Active policy keyed only by role; policy insertion attaches matching assignments/eligibilities across users. | Owned policy and source lineage, tenant-bounded captures/queues and no reset of inherited deadlines. |
| `RoleState`, authority snapshots and issuer `IdentityAuthority` | Live grants join global authentication/activation/review policy; active identity authority requires protocol 6. | One canonical owner-bound policy/source resolver, namespace-bound fingerprint and a coordinated stricter authority protocol; no global fallback. |

### Migration And Receiving Decisions

1. **Canonical policy identity is tenant plus role, not role alone.** A mutable authentication/activation/review setting cannot be made safe by filtering a user query while leaving `WHERE role=$1` updates global. Separate tenant-owned current governance from the retained global legacy policy evidence. Sharing immutable role names/catalog metadata is allowed; sharing mutable policy authority is not. The new owned resolver must be the eventual single positive authority source, not a parallel permissive role engine.
2. **Adoption is explicit and content-bound.** Review the exact legacy source, owner, policy versions, original grantor/configurer/decision identities and all reviewer references before any positive adoption. A beneficiary's retained user home is necessary but cannot legitimize a historical foreign grantor or a global policy containing another tenant's reviewers. Unknown/foreign lineages stay quarantined or negative until a separate legitimate new grant. Do not copy an old positive grant to every tenant, infer a default owner or resurrect a revoked/expired source. Preserve original IDs, old policy/cycle history, unanswered cutoffs and source expiries; adoption does not restart time.
3. **Bind all positive source resolution, not only HTTP commands.** Direct assignments, eligibility, accepted PIM decisions/activation and both assignment/eligibility review leases must resolve through their owned governance. The authority fingerprint must include the owning namespace/generation and applicable policy/adoption versions. Update `AuthorizationState`, issuer parsing and the other `readRoleState` consumers together. A new active authority protocol must reject old/global-only positive envelopes; missing/unreviewed owned policy is unavailable/inactive, not an empty permissive policy. Do not advertise a higher protocol number before the resolver actually supplies this guarantee.
4. **Receiving SQL remains mandatory.** Original-bearer tenant proof precedes the transaction; enabled tenant/version/generation is held, owned actor/recipient/reviewer users are locked in deterministic UUID order, then owned policy/target rows. Resolve foreign UUIDs before taking their locks. Admit one exact root with exact before/expected-after content, immutable original actor, strong owner-bound versions and physical role/MFA/token deadlines. A required event/pending outbox and deferred content/authority checks must reject cleared scopes, late extra effects, missing guards and limited-role definer laundering. Old public or internal compatibility commands cannot mutate already adopted rows without equivalent scope, even for negative revocation.
5. **Planned self-reduction is not a revocation exemption.** Deactivation, own admitted source removal or a policy change that intentionally removes the actor's own effective role may make the local after-fingerprint differ. Derive only that exact admitted delta; do not add a generic skip/ignore-current-authority flag or substitute an internal bearer. The ordinary original issuer checkpoint still runs after actual DML and must roll back independently withdrawn authority. The browser receives confirmed effects and explicit reauthentication/recovery guidance, never a fabricated remaining admin session.
6. **Switch the complete public boundary together.** Direct-role, PIM, role review and mutable role-assurance policy handlers/known-ID reads/catalogs/queues need the same original tenant. Negotiate a distinct scoped authority-management capability in their real consoles; older receivers must not look safe because they return familiar envelopes. Preserve independent protected duties and current first approval, not only grant visibility. A scoped direct-grant page while another PIM/policy path still creates foreign authority is not an implemented tenant-authority boundary.
7. **Prove the source-to-license consequence.** The currently observed foreign role/PIM transitions must become 404/rollback cases with two real owners, including global administrators and coherent foreign references. Test source migration/replay/quarantine, before-limit queues, policy isolation, original withdrawal after DML and observed races. Actual signed issuer/gateway and browser lifecycles must show fresh owned role/PIM activation enabling only its legitimate local allocation authority, and withdrawal/expiry closing it without removing unrelated existing business licenses or claiming any external Graph effect.

### Implemented Inactive Migration Review

Migration `030_authority_migration_candidates.sql` now supplies the first explicit migration-review boundary, not a second role engine or the live authority cutover. `identity_authority_inventory` collects the complete bounded owned assignment/eligibility/request/review graph, retained audit-origin classification, link/tenant generations, current legacy policy settings and content hashes. It labels the shared policies `global-not-owned-authority`; their presence is not treated as per-tenant ownership. Personal names, email addresses, raw source reasons and PIM authority objects are not copied into the inventory; their relevant source/audit content is bound by hashes.

`identity_authority_stage` accepts one complete manifest with a separate proposal for all 62 current catalog roles and an explicit disposition for every retained owned source. It requires the real database owner/inheriting owner privileges without an assumed role and an explicit transaction-local offline-review marker. The approval actor is recorded as the actual database role. `reviewAnchorUserId` is an owned enabled canonical context anchor, **not proof that this user signed an approval, held a duty or performed MFA**. Named proposed reviewers must be own enabled canonical users and distinct from that anchor; their future owned duties, full independence and publication authority still require the later source/receiving implementation.

- Source intentions are `adopt` or `quarantine`, never newly created privileges. Unknown/foreign history cannot be adopted, even if its legacy global role source is currently positive. A recognized exact offline-bootstrap origin additionally needs `acceptOfflineOrigin: true` on that specific adopted source; it is not an override for another provenance class.
- Required dependency components must be classified together. Pending/approved global PIM decisions are not portable activation authority: only an already active coherent bounded component can be proposed, and no original source/cycle/deadline is rewritten. Revoked/expired sources stay quarantine intentions. Existing mandatory MFA/PIM and active recurrence cannot be weakened by a proposal; the 16 fixed-MFA duties retain true/300-second minima.
- Exact tenant version/generation, issuer, catalog and inventory hashes are checked before insertion and again at deferred receiving commit. Only owned users are locked, in bounded UUID order after the enabled tenant row; known foreign reviewer references are rejected without waiting on their row locks. Candidate validity is at most 15 minutes and cannot exceed an adopted legacy source's physical expiry/review cutoff.
- The result is an immutable `draft` plus a mandatory immutable event and **pending** outbox in one transaction. `canAuthorize` is always false. `adoptionIntentCount`/`quarantineIntentCount` count plans, not actual grants or revocations. The draft cannot be updated into an active namespace. Missing receiving/no-effects guards, missing evidence, late lifetime/actor drift and cleared scopes fail closed.
- Staging cannot carry live canonical identity/group/governance DML or truncation in the same transaction, including a foreign group-based app/license change outside the role inventory. The no-effects guard also recognizes the staged transaction's actual retained candidate, so clearing/restoring a GUC cannot hide an extra write. Normal unstaged role/PIM paths are deliberately **not** claimed to be fixed by this guard.
- The CLI validates the exact non-authorizing response and independently requires a real same-transaction candidate/event/outbox receipt before COMMIT; it prints a result only after deferred commit succeeds. A plausible server JSON response without receiving rows is not confirmation. No human token, internal bearer, default tenant or application database credential is synthesized.

### Implemented Owner-Bound Source Assessment (Inactive)

Migration `031_authority_source_plans.sql` adds `identity_authority_candidate_snapshot(candidate, tenant, issuer)`, an owner-only **read-only** source for assessing a committed draft. Exact known owner/issuer predicates precede inventory evaluation; an uncommitted staging result, absent/mismatched event or pending outbox, missing receiving guards, expired candidate, disabled/reincarnated tenant or changed inventory fails closed. The CLI independently checks the selected candidate, exact manifest/inventory, hashes, current inventory and receiving rows in the same repeatable-read transaction before returning a plan. A plausible JSON function response without the corresponding committed rows produces no successful stdout result.

`OwnedAuthorityPlan` consumes that SQL-confirmed snapshot, not arbitrary bearer claims or a mutable global role policy as positive governance. It resolves each proposed assignment through its complete retained same-user/same-role component and the candidate's complete tenant-bound policy proposal. Its plan namespace binds the candidate ID, explicit issuer, tenant version/generation, manifest and inventory hashes. Proposed role revisions additionally bind exact source/audit hashes, retained source IDs, applicable policy content, user/link authority generations and the proposed `(tenantId, role, version 1)` policy identity. Version 1 here describes a policy **proposal**, not a persisted active policy or a live source protocol.

- Quarantined, foreign, unresolved, disabled/unbound-recipient and unacknowledged offline sources cannot produce a positive proposed assignment. Unknown/malformed ownership or incomplete policy/graph data has no permissive global fallback.
- Privileged sources must remain finite and fit the proposed original grant-duration limit; the assessment does not truncate, extend or regrant them. A newly mandatory activation policy leaves an old direct assignment negative. Only an already-active complete PIM assignment/request/eligibility component with matching owner, role, original request/assignment policy incarnation and actual duration/expiry can satisfy that proposed source condition. Pending/approved decisions are never converted into portable activation authority. Legacy eligibilities have no policy-version column; their exact retained source/audit hash and matching request lineage are used rather than inventing one.
- A proposed recurring rule without a retained lease remains negative; no initial lease, independent approval or new grace period is fabricated. Retained review IDs, versions, cycles and physical cutoffs remain bound. A tighter window caps the original due time, and a tighter interval reduces the retained cycle schedule from its original anchor rather than restarting it at observation time. Unsafe arithmetic fails negative. Existing legacy lease grace is not proof of a newly approved owned publication.
- Required MFA/age is calculated from the applicable proposed owned policies, preserving the fixed privileged minima. The assessment proves **neither** a user's present MFA session **nor** named reviewers' live duties, full independence or a usable administrative recovery path. Those are still publication/receiving prerequisites; the plan is not permission to publish.
- Output always has `canAuthorize: false`, diagnostic `proposedRoles`/`proposedRolesRevision` and per-assignment `effectiveInPlan`/issue codes. It deliberately has no live `active`, `context` or user `roles` envelope. The actual issuer rejects it as an unavailable identity source. This assessment did not change `RoleState`, `AuthorizationState`, dependent license snapshots, issuer protocol 6 or public handlers. The subsequent separate migration-032 target fence below does change role/PIM target handlers, but neither step closes the live legacy-source/global-governance gap.

Migration 030's review functions and the new snapshot function now pin UTC as well as their schema search path. Exact inventory and receiving-scope hashes therefore survive actual Swiss, Hawaiian and Nepalese SQL session time zones; database timestamp serialization cannot silently rebase a reviewed source. A short-lived older draft created under non-UTC hashing may need a fresh explicit inventory/review rather than compatibility rebasing.

### Offline Operator Workflow

This is a privileged **development/migration preparation** tool. Supply `IDENTITY_AUTHORITY_REVIEW_DATABASE_URL` and `IDENTITY_AUTHORITY_REVIEW_SCHEMA` explicitly through appropriate secret handling; it never falls back to `DATABASE_URL`, auto-migrates a database or chooses `public`. Do not place database credentials in manifests, command output, commits or logs. Replace the placeholders in these commands with the intended already migrated schema/tenant/issuer/file:

```sh
cd services/core/identity-service
npm run authority:review -- inventory TENANT_UUID ISSUER
npm run authority:review -- stage MANIFEST_FILE
npm run authority:review -- plan CANDIDATE_UUID TENANT_UUID ISSUER
```

Review the redacted inventory and legacy constraints, then author the full JSON manifest; the CLI does not automatically copy permissive global settings or select sources for adoption. Its ten top-level fields are:

| Field | Required meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `schemaVersion` | Literal 1 for this offline manifest, not a higher live identity-authority protocol. |
| `tenantId`, `issuer` | Exact explicit canonical tenant UUID and configured issuer. |
| `expectedTenantVersion`, `expectedTenantGeneration` | Exact decimal strings from the reviewed inventory, including values above JavaScript integer precision. |
| `reviewAnchorUserId` | Known own enabled canonical user context; not authenticated approval evidence. |
| `inventorySha256` | The SQL JSONB content digest returned with the inventory; do not substitute a differently serialized client hash. |
| `policies` | Exactly one entry per current catalog role: `role`, `authentication` (`required`, `maxAgeSeconds`), `activation` (`required`, `maxDurationSeconds`, `reviewerUserIds`), and `review` (null or `intervalSeconds`, `windowSeconds`, `reviewerUserIds`). Existing/fixed constraints must not be weakened. |
| `sources` | Every inventory `(kind,id)` exactly once with `disposition`; only a specifically reviewed offline-bootstrap adoption may also carry `acceptOfflineOrigin: true`. |
| `reason` | Explicit 10–500-character control-free review explanation. |

Current limits are 1,000 owned users, links per user, total retained sources, relevant legacy review policies and audit records per source; a manifest file is limited to 2 MiB. Exceeding a graph boundary fails rather than truncating it. This is not a claim of enterprise-scale batch migration: partitioned review/publication with a complete namespace fence remains required before supporting larger histories.

After staging has actually committed, `plan` rechecks the exact committed draft and reports how its retained assignments fit the proposed owned policies. It is read-only, requires the same explicit review connection/schema plus exact candidate/tenant/issuer, and does not publish a namespace, select new sources or acquire foreign user locks. Its redacted result contains IDs, policy assurance requirements, hashes and issue codes, not source reasons, personal names/email or copied authority proof objects. A matching plan remains an assessment rather than authenticated human approval.

No activation/publishing command, positive authority-source change, new web protocol or **complete** authority receiver switch is included yet. Migration 032's separate target defense does not activate a draft. Recording a quarantine intention still does **not** revoke a retained imported foreign grant. The complete receiving implementation, owned resolver and coordinated migration remain required; the planned boundary is not marked implemented.

The preceding preparation gates applied the then-current **31** identity migrations to disposable native PostgreSQL; they do not replace the newer target-fence gates or the historical increment-51 full signed/browser acceptance:

| Identity native PostgreSQL suite | **350/350**, including 49 candidate/source-plan cases, actual PIM activation, physical committed-draft expiry, receiving failure/replay and cross-session UTC determinism | `/tmp/od-authority52-owned-final-native1.log` |
| Issuer unit suite | **444/444**, including refusal of the offline source-plan envelope | `/tmp/od-authority52-owned-final-issuer-unit1.log` |

These were **1,299 distinct cases within those four named gates**, not 1,299 additional regressions and not a full new live-authority acceptance. Focused/repeated executions are not added again.
