The prior 4,098-case increment-51 acceptance likewise belongs to its recorded source/migration set. No new active authority protocol, production migration, container deployment, live customer change or external vendor operation is inferred.

### Implemented Defensive Target Fence (Active, Partial)

Migration `032_authority_target_tenants.sql` and `AuthorityTenant` now protect the **actual existing** direct-role and PIM target paths rather than adding another preview. Before a write, all four exact receiving triggers must exist/enabled. The current actor's UUID/issuer/subject must resolve through the real enabled user/link, retained subject home and enabled tenant; supplied context user/link revisions must still match. The writable transaction holds that enabled tenant before locking only owned actor/recipient/reviewer users in UUID order. Known foreign target users, eligibilities and requests return 404 without taking or waiting on their foreign user locks. Direct role administration rechecks its actual role fingerprint, role-management permission and MFA after those locks, so a role physically expiring during an observed lock wait cannot create a stale grant.

The existing PIM self/eligibility/queue handlers now pass their canonical user rather than consulting a global queue. Queue ownership is in SQL **before** `LIMIT 101`, including a native case with 110 newer foreign pending requests. Eligibility/request joins require the same actual recipient; owner request creation filters its eligibility before `FOR UPDATE`. Activation includes only same-home saved reviewer locks. Cancel/revoke do not gratuitously lock a historical decision user who is not being re-admitted as positive activation authority.

SQL BEFORE triggers independently reject a canonical actor writing another user's foreign role assignment, eligibility, request or review lease. Positive saved PIM requester/decision identities must resolve to that same home, and canonical review capture refuses foreign named voters. They run ahead of the existing authority row triggers for inserts. A raw SQL UPDATE necessarily acquires its target row lock before a BEFORE-row trigger runs; **the application predicates**, not that trigger, supply the verified foreign no-lock behavior. New predicates are not a claim that every historical/cascade effect or globally keyed review-policy command is owned.

The legacy receiving SQL requires the actual table-owner/inheriting-owner session and no `SET ROLE`; a limited SQL login cannot invent a canonical GUC or call a raw write to launder an offline import. Actual owners retain their pre-existing offline/bootstrap/import powers for noncanonical historical actor records. This is **not** a new delegated offline API, proof that arbitrary owner SQL is safe, or the least-privilege application-database-role architecture planned for full root admission. A deployment with a separate limited app login will deliberately fail these legacy writes until its real receiving admission exists; do not grant ownership merely to silence the failure.

**Remaining boundary:** local canonical home/context checks are not the uncached original-bearer tenant checkpoint required before and after actual DML. Migration 032 adds no exact operation root/effect receipt, per-command pending outbox, deferred original token/MFA deadline evidence or negotiated scoped console protocol. Authentication/activation/review governance remains globally keyed; a global review operation can conservatively fail when it attempts foreign capture, rather than becoming a new tenant-owned policy. The live `RoleState`/issuer protocol remains unchanged, so earlier imported foreign sources can still be positive. The complete 52.1–52.5 source/policy/command/UI/license acceptance below remains required.

### Current Target-Fence Verification

| Gate | Confirmed result | Local receipt |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Identity unit suite | **508/508**, including 12 tenant-helper cases | `/tmp/od-authority52-target-unit3.log` |
| Identity native PostgreSQL suite | **365/365**, including 15 target cases, foreign user lock avoidance, pre-limit queues, coherent SQL lineage denial, physical operator expiry during a proven wait, exact owner-guard rejection and safe manual cleanup of imported foreign history | `/tmp/od-authority52-target-native4.log` |
| Issuer unit suite | **444/444** | `/tmp/od-authority52-target-issuer-unit1.log` |
| Complete signed issuer/receiving PostgreSQL suite | **251/251**, zero failures/cancellations/skips, including all 11 production-mode cases after the full preceding governance lifecycle | `/tmp/od-authority52-target-signed-full1.log` |
| Actual signed production-mode gateway | **11/11**, including foreign role/PIM denial under a current global administrator, unchanged evidence, legitimate own role grant/revoke, its live local license-management capability loss, and missing receiver denial | `/tmp/od-authority52-target-production1.log` |

The four independent complete gates cover **1,568 distinct cases**; the focused 11-case production run is included in the 251-case suite and is not counted again. Two further real-SQL cases keep cleanup distinct from new authority: a recipient can cancel, but cannot activate, an imported foreign approval while that foreign decider's row is locked; an own role administrator can withdraw an imported foreign-origin recipient grant without locking its old grantor or rewriting its original audit. Limited-login/SET ROLE tests now require the exact new receiving-owner error, not merely any SQL permission failure. These are controlled historical imports followed by normal guarded cleanup, not permission to create new foreign sources.

The complete expanded signed suite finishes successfully in **1,373,415.36 ms**, after its actual **302,455 ms** wait at 149 remaining identity requests. Its terminal process exits 0; no receiving limiter is weakened and the suite's zero-identity-429 assertion holds. The 11-case production subset is no longer substituted for this complete run. The signed role-to-license case observes actual local receiving capabilities and token invalidation; it does not prove a business license allocation, tenant-scoped licensing resources or vendor delivery. The live role producer still uses legacy governance/protocol 6, so this is receiving-target regression acceptance, **not** the complete owned-authority cutover.

The original run receipt is `/tmp/od-authority52-target-source-before.sha256`. During that run, only the isolated native target-test file received stronger assertions/new cleanup cases; it is not loaded by the signed runner. After the signed run completed, its separate browser harness received the timing fix described below. Unchanged runtime and signed-fixture files are checked against `/tmp/od-authority52-target-runtime-before.sha256`, and the final browser scope is bound by `/tmp/od-authority52-target-browser-source.sha256`. Neither change modifies production MFA verification or the signed receiver fixture. Separate console/regression checks do not imply a new scoped console protocol, production build or deployment.

The complete Chrome gate passes in **266,892.19 ms**, with zero failures/skips/cancellations. It exercises the real own-tenant role/PIM consoles, actual MFA, source withdrawal/reauthentication, administrative review and local license workflows. The five layout/driver cases use intercepted API/session fixtures; they are not extra proof of real OIDC or receiving authorization. Their isolated build/tsconfig and non-reused test server leave the application's original `tsconfig.json` unchanged. The receipt parser at `/tmp/od-authority52-target-named-gates.json` independently checks all thirteen terminal summaries and the non-overlapping total.

The initial Chrome run (`/tmp/od-authority52-target-browser1.log`) is **not accepted**: 48/49 pass and one administrative-review case times out waiting for enrollment confirmation. Its shared test helper generated the *previous* TOTP counter, the oldest accepted code; crossing the next real 30-second boundary between filling and confirming could make it stale. The helper now uses the current counter, records the exact successful enrollment response and only then remembers the consumed counter. It does not retry a failed enrollment, widen the production TOTP window, remove replay checks, bypass fresh MFA or increase receiver limits.

A new actual-browser case waits through that real counter boundary, requires **401 `invalid_otp`**, confirms only a fresh current code, checks that no application session cookie was issued and rejects reuse of the consumed enrollment challenge. The first focused reproduction correctly reached the stale-code denial but failed because an unqualified alert locator also matched Next's route announcer; its selector now identifies the actual error message rather than hiding either alert. The corrected two-case reproduction passes (`/tmp/od-authority52-target-browser-boundary2.log`, 51,993.82 ms), followed by the complete **50/50** gate above. No production MFA or browser-broker code changes were needed.

The frontend typecheck remains **non-green**: `/tmp/od-authority52-target-types1.log` reports the same **17** existing diagnostics as `/tmp/od-policy42-typecheck-final.log`; the only diagnostic-line delta is the already shifted sidebar location (472 → 473), not a new error/message. This work does not fix those unrelated prototype APIs/types. Thus the named regression set is green, **not** every repository check, a clean production build or a rollout approval. The complete owned policy/source/root/console cutover and other platform tenant/resource boundaries remain open. The final scoped source receipt is `/tmp/od-authority52-target-accepted-source.sha256`.

### Cutover Order, Not Partial Safety Claims

| Order | Concrete engineering deliverable | Gate before the next switch |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 52.1 | Define tenant/role policy identity and a reviewed adoption manifest for the exact legacy assignment, eligibility, request, policy and review graph; retain old rows as provenance, not fallback authority. | Two-owner native migration/replay tests; unknown/foreign origins quarantined; no expiry/cycle reset or positive multi-tenant cloning. A staged candidate is not an active grant. |
| 52.2 | Implement the owner-bound policy/source resolver and a stricter identity-authority envelope/fingerprint in `RoleState`, SQL helpers, `AuthorizationState`, issuer parsing and dependent snapshots. | Old/global-only positive source is refused; absent governance fails closed; mandatory MFA/PIM, fresh-token fencing, saved approval withdrawal and local license-authority consequences still hold. Do not publish a new positive protocol first and repair its meaning later. |
| 52.3 | Implement receiving root/effect/receipt guards and same-client original-actor admission for the complete role/PIM/review/policy command set. | Exact owned sources/versions, foreign no-lock/no-mutation, missing guard/outbox rollback, real expiry, scope tampering, original withdrawal during DML and observed concurrent revocation tests. |
| 52.4 | Switch all canonical public handlers, bounded lists/catalogs and their real role/PIM/review/assurance consoles to the same receiving boundary and scoped protocol. | No alternate global command path for adopted sources; actual signed and browser grant/review/activation/revocation workflows work without fabricated data or extra profile authority. |
| 52.5 | Re-run the full named regression set and explicit cross-tenant authority-to-local-license cases, then update the implemented-boundary receipt. | A complete source/command/UI/migration acceptance, not only a direct-grant patch, an inventory report or a mock capability. No live vendor or production rollout approval is inferred. |

This sequence permits isolated migration/source development, not exposing a second permanent permissive authority engine. Any staging work must remain explicitly inactive/unadvertised until its receiving/source guarantees are true; the known legacy gap remains reported until the complete switch is verified.

### Implemented Owned Policy Bases (Sealed, Not Active)

Migration `033_owned_authority_policy_storage.sql` implements the first **durable owned policy identity**, not another global-role setting or a permission grant. It normalizes one exact already committed candidate into a distinct namespace UUID/incarnation. The immutable candidate stays `draft`; the new namespace is constrained to **`sealed`**, never `active`. Its tenant, issuer, reviewed tenant version/generation, manifest/inventory/content hashes, actual sealing database role and original publication deadline are retained. Several alternative sealed plans may exist for a tenant; none is selected as its active authority namespace.

| Storage | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `identity_authority_namespaces` | New namespace ID, retained candidate lineage, explicit owner/issuer and separate namespace generation; no draft-state flip or default tenant. |
| `identity_owned_role_authentication` | Typed owned `(namespace, role, version)` MFA/age component, preserving fixed privileged minima. |
| `identity_owned_role_activation` | Independently keyed PIM requirement/duration/named-reviewer component, not the MFA component's version. |
| `identity_owned_role_reviews` | Independently keyed explicit recurring-review configuration; a disabled rule has null schedule and no reviewers, not a missing/permissive global row. |
| `identity_owned_role_policies` | Sealed base references to the three component versions, enforced by foreign keys and the namespace/tenant owner keys. |
| `identity_authority_namespace_events` / `identity_authority_namespace_outbox` | Exact immutable offline sealing event and mandatory pending evidence, committed atomically with every component. |

Only **initial version 1** is admitted by this sealing operation. These are immutable policy **bases**, not editable current heads or a live policy-management API. Later owned component changes require the complete verified receiving/history model; they cannot be appended through raw writes, a combined global counter, an implicit maximum-version lookup or a namespace reincarnation for each ordinary edit.

The SQL-only entry points `identity_authority_seal(candidateId, tenantId, issuer)` and `identity_authority_owned_policy(namespaceId, tenantId, issuer, role)` require the actual owner/inheriting-owner session without `SET ROLE`; execute permissions are revoked from PUBLIC and definer search paths/time zones are pinned. No CLI/HTTP sealing or publishing command is added. Sealing additionally requires the explicit `opendirectory.authority_seal_review = explicit-offline-owned-policy-seal` marker in a **fresh transaction with no assigned XID** before earlier writes or row locks; even an earlier explicit `txid_current()` means a new transaction is needed. Own enabled tenant and bounded own users are held in the established lock order. Foreign candidate selectors are refused before foreign user locks, and disabled/changed/reincarnated or expired reviewed input is not rebased.

One exact root, all four complete catalog component/reference sets and both evidence rows are required at receiving commit. Missing/altered receiving guards or outbox, late component changes, cleared scopes, changed source inventory and physical inherited deadline expiry roll back the entire seal. A no-effects guard covers ordinary canonical identity/governance rows and truncation; it also recognizes the real same-transaction namespace after a caller clears its GUC. The fresh-XID precondition rejects earlier unrelated writes/truncation, before an admission GUC or namespace row existed. Policy inspection refuses an uncommitted namespace before its deferred validation has run.

After commit, private inspection verifies both retained candidate and namespace receipts plus the exact normalized content. It reads the **owned** component keys and never fills a missing policy from a global role row. Two real tenants can retain different settings for the same catalog role; later legacy global authentication/activation changes do not rewrite these owned bases. Lost evidence or tampered component content is unavailable, not an empty default. UTC serialization and string-returned generations preserve exact evidence across SQL time zones and values above JavaScript's safe integer range.

Every inspection/sealing result explicitly has `canAuthorize: false` and state `sealed`. A retained review anchor is context metadata, not a signed human decision or fresh-MFA proof; named reviewer duties/full independence and a usable bootstrap/recovery handover are **not certified** by these bases. No assignments, eligibilities, requests, review leases or business licenses are created, copied or revoked. Historical sealed settings remain evidence, not authorization after a deadline. `RoleState`, `AuthorizationState`, global policy handlers, issuer schema 6 and the consoles do not consume these bases yet. The actual owned source/adoption resolver, publication/activation record, exact original-bearer command receipts and coordinated protocol/UI switch remain required; the known global-governance gap is still open.

### Implemented Sealed Source Kernel (Not Live Authority)

Migration `034_owned_authority_source_resolution.sql` implements an actual PostgreSQL source-resolution kernel for the immutable sealed bases. `identity_authority_owned_role_evidence(namespaceId, tenantId, issuer, userId)` selects the explicit committed namespace, its exact retained candidate and the known owned recipient; no namespace is inferred from a beneficiary or role name. The candidate's immutable manifest/inventory, already bound by the namespace receipt, are the durable **sealed adoption binding** `(namespace, kind, original UUID)`. This does not create a second set of assignments or a mutable live adoption table.

The kernel reads the typed owned authentication, activation and recurring-review components through their independently versioned base references. It does not attach a mutable global policy to an otherwise owned recipient. Current assignment/eligibility/request/review rows and their bounded retained audit hashes must still match the exact reviewed bindings. The entire original same-user/same-role graph must be adopted, live at review and origin-coherent; offline origins retain their specific acknowledgement, while foreign, unresolved, quarantined, subsequently created and changed sources cannot enter through a fallback. Added review attachments or changed dependency edges also invalidate the affected component, even when the original assignment row has not changed.

- **Direct and activated sources:** no permanent privileged grant, excessive duration or direct grant under a new mandatory-PIM rule is relabelled compliant. A PIM assignment needs the same original active request and nonrevoked eligibility, coherent versions, actual recipient, duration and physical expiry. A pending or approved request is not a projected grant. Revocation does not refresh a hash or silently re-adopt the source; unrelated exact components can remain eligible.
- **Both review planes:** assignment and eligibility leases participate in the component. A new mandatory review cannot invent an approval/lease. Original IDs, cycles, due times and deadlines remain untouched. A tighter interval is applied to the retained cycle, and a tighter window never resets its original due time; numeric arithmetic remains negative rather than overflowing on a maximum-bigint cycle. Existing unanswered cutoffs are retained even when assessing policy changes.
- **Global provenance, not global authority:** the old review interval needed to interpret an inherited cycle comes from the retained candidate, not the current global rule. Lease-specific audit events always remain part of the exact source hash. Later global-policy-only review events are excluded using the candidate's original review cutoff; otherwise a later global retirement would silently become owned policy authority. The original reviewed policy-origin evidence remains hash-bound. Tests independently change actual global MFA, PIM and review policy while requiring identical sealed owned results.
- **Current recipient and finite evidence:** disabled or unbound recipients cannot obtain projected roles; tenant lifecycle version/generation and the recipient home incarnation are checked. Current user/link revisions bind the evidence fingerprint. At most 1,000 source nodes/audit rows/links and 100 positive assignments are accepted, without silently trimming a privileged subset. Returned validity is bounded by the original publication deadline and applicable source/review cutoffs. Replay, UTC and bigint-string fences are retained.

Both new SQL functions are owner-only security definers with pinned schema/UTC and no `PUBLIC` execution. Granting execution to a real limited login or using `SET ROLE` does not launder ownership. Reads work in an actual read-only transaction and do not acquire foreign user row locks. Missing namespace evidence or receiving guards is an error, not permissive empty governance.

On migrations 001–034 the private result uses `schemaVersion: 1`, **`canAuthorize: false`**, `state: sealed`, `proposedRoles` and `proposedRolesRevision`; there are no live `roles` or bearer `context` claims. Migration 036 replaces its source input and uses private assessment version 2, still without authorization. No public endpoint, CLI activation, role/PIM/review command or license side effect is introduced. The publication deadline bounds this **sealed assessment**, not the lifetime model for a future published namespace. Publication still needs current complete candidate/adoption validation and a real independently usable bootstrap/recovery handover; this kernel does not certify current named reviewer duties or human MFA. Later legitimate source changes require owned receiving/history receipts, not automatic hash refresh or reinterpretation of legacy commands.

`RoleState`, the raw deferred role snapshot, `AuthorizationState`, issuer schema 6, dependent duty-specific license snapshots and all public governance handlers still use the old source. They must be switched together to a single active owned source under the stricter protocol. This kernel is a tested part of that cutover, **not closure of the legacy global-governance gap** or a second supported positive engine.

### Implemented Original-Bearer Command Checkpoints (Live, Partial)

`DirectoryRoles`, `PrivilegedActivation` and `RoleAuthentication` now protect their **actual public write paths** with `AuthorityCommand`, not another inactive preview. Previously these receivers checked canonical local roles before DML, but did not independently recheck the original token after their writes. `PrivilegedAccessPolicy` and `RoleReviews` already had their own original-access-state revalidation; those checks are retained, not replaced by a new permissive path.

The public role grant/revoke, eligibility grant/revoke, PIM request/approve/deny/activate/cancel/revoke and role-MFA update routes forward the exact original `Authorization` header. The shared command boundary clones the original verified claims and checks that token at the configured issuer **before starting a transaction**, then again **after the complete operation and its SQL audit effects, before the transaction callback returns**. Every return path, including idempotent/no-op PIM replies and already revoked role/eligibility replies, goes through the final check. Missing original headers, rejected tokens and unavailable/mismatched state are errors; no client-credentials token, internal bearer, cached positive state, retry or replacement post-change fingerprint is available.

Mandatory role-policy fresh MFA is checked again after the final asynchronous issuer response. Activation and protected PIM decisions similarly retain their action-specific fresh assurance at the final checkpoint. This does not extend `auth_time` through refresh or an operation's local result. Existing pre-DML permissions, current canonical role/MFA checks, target-tenant guards, finite durations, versions, independent saved approval and reviewer checks remain in place.

Legitimate self-activation and self-deactivation still use the **same original bearer**. The issuer independently reads committed canonical authority while the local admitted transaction is uncommitted; the operation does not invent a bearer with its expected after-state or skip revocation checking because its own role is changing. A separately revoked original token must still roll back that exact self-change. After a successful commit the old source-bound token is stale and a fresh ordinary session is needed; successful local role withdrawal is not an automatic removal of existing business licenses.

### Implemented Deferred Authority Checkpoints (Migration 035)

Migration `035_authority_command_checkpoints.sql` extends the actual role/PIM/authentication/activation-policy/review **write paths** with a mandatory final SQL checkpoint. This addresses physical expiry and extra local changes after the last asynchronous application check; it does **not** select or publish the sealed owned namespace.

`AuthorityCheckpoint` retains a private copy of the original claims, reads the existing canonical `RoleState` and requires its original fingerprint/held-role assurance. SQL derives the actual enabled retained tenant and exact subject/link mapping, holds that tenant row through the transaction and returns tenant/user/link incarnation metadata as bigint strings. No request-selected tenant or global-role owner is invented. The normal original-bearer issuer checks remain in place. All five management classes participate: `DirectoryRoles`, `PrivilegedActivation`, `RoleAuthentication`, `PrivilegedAccessPolicy` and the create/retire/decide paths in `RoleReviews`.

After the operation's SQL and its ordinary final issuer check, the adapter records the **earliest** original access-token expiry, initially required held-role MFA cutoff and any final action-specific MFA cutoff. Refresh issue time, a weaker final policy or a mutated caller object cannot extend that deadline. The transaction uses UTC for content hashing; the SQL routines also pin schema and UTC. Original context is retained separately from the actual local post-operation user revision/source snapshot, so legitimate PIM self-change does not require a fabricated post-change bearer or skipping the original issuer check.

| New receiving storage | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `identity_authority_command_checkpoints` | One final checkpoint per transaction, real tenant/link/user incarnations, original context, hashed token identifier, actual recorded database role, local after-snapshot hash and finite deadline. This is not an authority grant or a predeclared logical command root. |
| `identity_authority_command_checkpoint_events` | Exact immutable `final-application-checkpoint` actor/context/deadline evidence, bound to the retained row hash. |
| `identity_authority_command_checkpoint_outbox` | Matching mandatory **pending** evidence in the same transaction; no delivery or external license effect is implied. |

- SQL receiving validates exact enabled row/deferred/no-effects/truncate guards before command mutation. Limited logins and `SET ROLE` cannot impersonate the real receiving owner through granted security-definer execution. Existing application database-owner dependence is **not** a newly solved least-privilege database-role model; do not grant ownership as an operational workaround.
- The adapter independently requires actual same-transaction checkpoint/event/pending-outbox rows rather than trusting a plausible JSON function response. Missing evidence, an omitted outbox, a late event-content change or changed scope rolls back the business mutation and every checkpoint row.
- Once sealed, no further canonical identity/group/governance DML or truncation may ride the transaction. The guard also finds its actual retained transaction checkpoint after a GUC is cleared. Ordinary unstaged transactions and explicit offline bootstrap remain separate paths; this is not their positive authorization. Unmodified legacy SQL writers are not yet forced to produce this checkpoint before their effects: it is mandatory in the upgraded five management classes, not a negotiated safe downgrade path or the complete adopted-source receiving boundary.
- Deferred validation rechecks exact evidence, tenant/link/user state and the observed local after-source snapshot, with physical deadline checks before and after that validation. Real token/MFA expiry during the pre-COMMIT wait rolls back. Original authentication expiry remains HTTP 401; the established fresh-MFA challenge remains 428 rather than a generic context-rebase error. Actual role expiry can also invalidate the local source snapshot without a revocation worker.

**Boundary not claimed:** this is a final local consistency/time fence, not proof that every effect **before** that checkpoint matched a pre-admitted command. In particular the local after-snapshot is not an authorization rebase or a proof of the exact permitted self-reduction delta. It currently reuses the legacy raw role snapshot and its global-policy/history coupling; a bounded owned SQL evidence reader remains part of the required single-source cutover. Exact root/target/before/expected-after admission, source/policy history and receiving actor derivation remain required. An independently withdrawn issuer token after the last RPC is not distributed-commit serialized by these SQL rows. Global governance/source remains legacy schema 6, the owned bases remain sealed, and no new public capability, human approval, publisher, complete command journal, outbox delivery or vendor fulfillment is advertised.

### Implemented Owned Source Bases (Migration 036, Not Active)

Migration `036_owned_authority_source_bindings.sql` materializes the reviewed source graph into **typed, immutable, namespace-owned initial versions**. The sealed SQL kernel now reads those rows and edges, not candidate JSON as an alternative source engine. A committed policy namespace without its separately committed source set is unavailable; it cannot fall back to the candidate inventory. The candidate and policy receipts remain mandatory retained provenance, and the initial materialization still checks the exact current reviewed inventory.

| Storage | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `identity_authority_source_sets` | One initial set per explicit namespace/tenant/issuer: frozen recipient-account evidence, original review time/deadline, source count, policy/manifest/inventory/content hashes, actual database role and transaction. |
| `identity_owned_authority_source_versions` | Exact original `(namespace, kind, source UUID, version)` plus recipient, reviewed origin, disposition, source state/hash and independent owned MFA/PIM/review component references. Only initial version **1** is currently admitted. |
| `identity_owned_authority_source_edges` | Original ordered dependency edges with same-namespace source-version foreign keys. An out-of-set reference remains explicitly `unresolved` with a null related version, never an inferred owner or later automatic binding. |
| `identity_authority_source_set_events` / `identity_authority_source_set_outbox` | Exact immutable `sources-materialized` content/actor evidence and mandatory pending outbox, atomic with every source and edge. |

Unsupported historical roles can be retained as **quarantine** with no invented owned-policy key. A familiar role or enabled beneficiary cannot make foreign/unresolved provenance positive. Complete PIM request/eligibility/assignment components and both review planes preserve their original IDs, source states, review lineage, order, cycles and physical cutoffs. Adoption remains a reviewed intention, not a grant.

The SQL-only `identity_authority_bind_sources(namespaceId, tenantId, issuer)` requires the actual owner/inheriting-owner session without `SET ROLE`, a **fresh transaction without an assigned XID**, the explicit `opendirectory.authority_source_review = explicit-offline-owned-source-review` marker and a previously committed sealed policy namespace. Known owner/issuer selection precedes bounded own-user locks. Missing guards/evidence, post-seal inventory drift or physical inherited expiry rolls everything back; there is no automatic backfill or rebasing. Earlier canonical writes cannot ride the operation. Later canonical DML/truncation is prohibited even after clearing its local scope, and deferred validation requires the entire unchanged initial scope, actual content and original deadline. Scope tampering cannot admit a second version or redirect rows into another namespace. All new tables also retain the earlier staging, sealing and authority-checkpoint no-effects guards.

`identity_authority_owned_source_data` refuses uncommitted materialization and verifies actual typed rows, edges and retained receipts. Private inspection, including the low-level row reader, remains owner-only despite an explicit definer-execute grant to a limited login. Schema and UTC are pinned, and public execution is revoked. Migration replay preserves source identities and evidence; a second materialization fails rather than overwriting the first.

`identity_authority_owned_role_evidence` now returns private **assessment schema 2**, `sourceSetSha256` and version-string source tuples. Its fingerprint binds that content hash and the exact source/MFA/PIM/review versions. It still returns **`canAuthorize: false`**, `state: sealed` and `proposedRoles`, without live `roles` or `context`. Legacy global MFA/PIM/review edits do not become owned policy authority. Current raw source withdrawal or changed graph/audits remains negative; a new unreviewed source cannot enter the initial set. The original publication deadline still bounds this sealed assessment, not future live source lifetime.

**Still required for the full objective:** explicit current source/policy heads and immutable admitted history; exact receiving command root/effect receipts for their transitions; finite independently usable bootstrap/recovery and namespace publication; and the coupled `RoleState`/SQL snapshot/issuer/source-protocol/console cutover. These initial rows are **not mutable current heads**, `MAX(version)` selection, a selected active namespace, an activation endpoint, a human approval/MFA proof, an outbox delivery mechanism or a complete license workflow. The existing live schema-6 global source is unchanged; neither its global-governance gap nor the complete 52.1–52.5 acceptance is closed.

Every listed process is terminal with exit 0, without failed/skipped/cancelled/todo cases. The native gate runs the complete ten-file identity suite against actual disposable PostgreSQL. New cases prove missing-source-set refusal without candidate fallback, exact typed active-PIM/both-review-plane graphs, unsupported-role quarantine, unresolved foreign edges, foreign-selector/no-foreign-lock behavior, immutable initial versions, second-version and cross-namespace scope-tampering rejection, complete deferred admission, missing guards/event/outbox rollback, late source-content changes, cleared-scope effects/truncation, prior-effect and uncommitted-seal rejection, post-seal drift, real physical expiry, owner-only limited/assumed-role reads and replay/UTC stability. Existing sealed-source tests also pass through the new typed bases, including changed global governance, maximum-bigint review cycles and bounded source overflow.

The production-mode gate uses the actual signed issuer/gateway and newly migrated identity storage. Its existing real PIM workflow denies allocation before activation, allows actual local allocation after independent approval/fresh MFA and a fresh ordinary token, and removes allocation authority after self-deactivation while preserving the existing business license row. This is regression evidence for the **unchanged live source**, not active owned-source or external vendor acceptance. No new browser/console, frontend typecheck, full 267-case signed run or complete thirteen-gate regression is claimed for migration 036. The historical 4,365-case migration-035 receipt is not extended or added to this targeted total; the 63-case focused preparation run (`/tmp/od-authority57-native2.log`) is included in the full 445, not counted again.

The first focused run retained its one replay-fixture failure (`/tmp/od-authority57-native1.log`, 42/43): replaying migration 034 alone intentionally restored the earlier private schema-1 reader. The fixture now replays 034 then 036 in actual migration order; it does not preserve a candidate fallback or relax the source-set requirement. The 63-case preparation run passed, followed by the complete native gate on the final owner-checked row-reader version. Runtime stayed frozen at `/tmp/od-authority57-runtime-frozen.sha256`, verified by `/tmp/od-authority57-runtime-check-final.log`; the scoped source/documentation receipt is `/tmp/od-authority57-source-final.sha256`. Owned test servers/clusters have stopped; no deployment, external license fulfillment or live namespace publication occurred.

The next code step is **separate explicit current policy/source references with append-only admitted version history**, preserving these initial receipts rather than rewriting them. Its receiving command must prove exact owned before/after transitions and independently versioned MFA/PIM/review semantics before any active publisher or source-protocol switch is exposed. The complete coordinated 52.2–52.5 implementation and acceptance remains required, not optional follow-up hardening.

### Storage And Resolver Contract For The Next Cutover

The following remain implementation constraints for the complete 52.1–52.3 cutover. Migrations 033/034/036 implement immutable owned policy/source bases and their sealed source kernel, **not the active resolver, mutable policy/source commands, a publish endpoint or an activated protocol**:

1. **Separate live namespace from draft identity.** An owned authority namespace must have a new retained UUID/incarnation, one explicit tenant and issuer, and exact reviewed publication/adoption evidence. A candidate ID remains a plan identity; do not turn its immutable `draft` row into live authority. Tenant lifecycle version/generation and authority-namespace incarnation remain distinct fences.
2. **Separate policy component versions.** Authentication, activation and recurring review share the owned `(namespace, tenant, role)` identity but not one mutable version counter. Today's authentication change invalidates a role fingerprint without revoking its underlying assignment; assignment/request activation versions intentionally do invalidate the grant. A single combined version compared to `assignment.policy_version` would accidentally turn every MFA-policy edit into role withdrawal. Preserve these semantics with independently versioned owned components and retained policy history. Ordinary unrelated policy edits must not reincarnate the whole namespace or revive/invalidate an unrelated duty-specific license lease.
3. **Bind the exact source, never a beneficiary-derived default.** An owned source binding must retain `(namespace, kind, original source UUID)`, recipient home, reviewed origin/component hashes and original expiry/review incarnations. Quarantine is negative; no old positive assignment may be duplicated for another tenant or silently imported because its recipient has a home. Later receiving changes must update only their admitted source/version effects with retained before/after evidence; they cannot re-adopt a new lineage through a hash refresh.
4. **One owned source for SQL and JavaScript.** The replacement SQL role-evidence reader, `RoleState`, raw deferred role snapshot and PIM/review guard helpers must resolve the same owned authentication/activation/review universe. Replace the legacy global attachment/version-setting triggers together: otherwise an old `WHERE role=$1` path could overwrite an owned request's version or attach another tenant's review. Keep legacy global rows as provenance, not a second positive engine or a fallback when owned governance is missing. Update delegate, scope-reviewer and publication snapshots from that same resolver.
5. **Coordinate strict source negotiation.** A future active source must expose its verified namespace/incarnation and require the new authority schema at the issuer; relabelling today's global source with a higher number is forbidden. The four existing context fields may remain structurally stable only if their role fingerprint cryptographically binds the namespace, owned component versions and actual sources. Old/global-only active responses and unadopted positive sources must fail closed; complete issuer/source/receiving migration is a breaking rollout gate, not a permissive runtime fallback flag.
6. **Prove the initial handover and subsequent human commands separately.** The first owned administrator/recovery path needs explicit finite reviewed bootstrap/adoption evidence and a usable same-tenant independent path. Record an actual offline database-owner action as such; `reviewAnchorUserId` is not a human signature or MFA proof. Do not synthesize an internal bearer to bridge the bootstrap, skip original-issuer post-DML checks for a planned self-change, or expose a publisher before these admission/receipt guarantees and the exact owned resolver exist.

### Original Acceptance Checklist

1. Inventory every direct grant/revoke, eligibility, request, approval, activation, deactivation, review, role-authentication and mandatory-activation policy receiver and its console. Bind recipient, original grantor/configurer, named approver/reviewer, known target and every bounded queue to the same uncached original-bearer tenant. Shared role names/catalog metadata must not become shared policy authority.
2. Define explicit tenant-owned policy keys and migration/adoption for existing global policy and grant/review records. Do not infer an owner from a role string, caller-supplied tenant or default company, duplicate an old positive grant into every tenant, or silently turn missing scoped governance into a permissive global fallback. Preserve historical cutoffs, source incarnations and revocation evidence.
3. Preserve protected duties, fresh original MFA, independent review, exact versions, deterministic owned-user-before-target lock order and valid self-reduction without bypassing an independently withdrawn bearer. Require actual SQL root/content/actor/tenant/deadline evidence and atomic pending outbox at receiving commit, not only application prechecks or a second permissive endpoint.
4. Keep the role/PIM/review consoles useful with explicit scoped receiving protocols, actual metadata and known UUIDs instead of unrelated profile permission. Verify complete grant-to-approved-activation-to-expiry/revocation and independent recertification in the visible workflow; do not call a pending eligibility an effective privilege.
5. Gate with real two-tenant SQL, foreign coherent references even under global administration, pre-limit queue isolation, missing/mismatched evidence, migration replay, observed concurrency and post-DML original withdrawal. Use actual signed issuer/gateway and browser lifecycles, including the effect of scoped role loss on real local license allocation. No external license fulfillment claim follows from a role mutation receipt.

This plan does not remove the outstanding OU, application, device, policy-resource, licensing-resource and guest boundaries, or the universal least-privilege/default-governance work. They remain required for the original Entra-like platform objective.

## Remaining Gaps

- The gateway has dedicated actual production-mode transport evidence and the complete 249-case signed regression, including originless requests and authoritative proxy CORS. Actual production Next/container ingress, deployment secrets and off-host operation are not verified by the native gate; do not treat it as rollout approval.
- The legacy runtime is consolidated into the canonical adapter. Existing prototype data/schema migration and the retired SAM/analytics/asset/realtime features remain unimplemented; do not advertise them or expose historical controller modules as an alternate service. Canonical notification delivery still requires an authorized feed/outbox.
- Canonical policy configuration and issuer policy-command admission now have exact content/target/publication checks, original-actor checkpoints, mandatory SQL command/origin/audit/outbox and generation-bound device offers/reports. Fresh original deployers can explicitly cancel own accepted commands with retained receiving evidence and recover bounded own history. Tenant/resource scopes, complete enrollment/lifecycle governance, other command families/certificate/package/internal receivers, durable publisher workflow planning/recovery, automatic source-triggered/cross-owner cancellation and supersession, separate effective-versus-draft runtime, safe agent execution/deduplication, off-host evidence and remaining prototype policy-as-code/agent views are still required. Approval withdrawal stops new admission rather than automatically cancelling accepted queues or undoing device effects. Cross-service checkpoints are not distributed commit serialization, and neither queue acceptance nor a device's own report proves observed endpoint enforcement.
- Flat canonical membership has opt-in independent first approval/reviews, mandatory bounded group-license projections and optional dedicated-client token renewal with expiring per-group source consent. Nested/dynamic groups, equivalent outage leases for other access planes/direct-only grants, bulk performance guarantees, automatic secret rotation/consent renewal and global identity/lifecycle mapping remain outstanding.
- Canonical principal binding and authoritative direct-user eligibility now protect the policy-service license workflow. Other services, historical noncanonical assignment identifiers, automatic identity linking, and external/lifecycle identifiers still need equivalent reconciliation; this is not yet a universal identity model.
- The explicit Microsoft fulfillment worker connects effective local entitlement projections to bounded Graph assignment/removal and observation. A separate read-only connector-bound collector supplies inventory; new additions additionally need a finite reviewed burn-down budget, atomic retained charge and one-time pre-POST admission. Both profiles remain off by default and have owned HTTP-contract verification only. Live Microsoft test-tenant acceptance, manufacturer-exclusive reservations and the SQL-to-HTTP effect gap, canonical independently authenticated capacity-review governance, automatically reviewed purchased-pool and billing/cost reconciliation, service-level activation, national clouds, external account lifecycle, credential automation and additional vendors remain open. No external license is purchased or provisioned on the user's behalf by this development run.
- Provider reconciliation beyond the bounded Microsoft direct-license plane, authoritative cross-service user IDs, lifecycle-event ordering, broker authorization, and transactional outbox delivery are still required. User/group source versions fence confirmed directory snapshots, but a delayed legacy offboarding event can still conflict with later local reactivation; those broker events are not yet version-enforced.
- Audit retention, off-host tamper-resistant evidence, complete catalog-change auditing, and an administrative audit viewer remain outstanding. Database triggers alone are not an immutable compliance archive.
- Tenant isolation and general resource boundaries beyond the explicit licensing pool plane, custom roles, default enforcement across all privileged roles/apps, risk/device/location conditions, phishing-resistant methods, independently reviewed lost-all-factors recovery, recovery notification/outbox delivery and complete receiving-service enforcement remain outstanding. Canonical per-application and held-role MFA/age have live enforcement and purpose-limited first binding; saved-code recovery replaces only the factor and requires normal login. Canonical eligible/approved/active grants use the same live role source and verified fresh TOTP at activation, and versioned role policies can require PIM instead of direct grants. Pool-scoped PIM and opt-in mandatory pool/duty policy are implemented, including protected global/legacy mutation paths, but unconfigured duties and global catalog roles remain separately permissive. Recurring canonical-role, human license-source, pool grant/eligibility and flat-group membership reviews enforce independent recertification and unanswered cutoffs; mandatory defaults, general cross-service delegation, application ownership/consent, group ownership and device-recipient reviews remain outstanding. Existing broad `admin` access should still be reduced as delegated roles are adopted.
- Individual-token, persisted family revocation and canonical human-user/link/role/client generations are enforced through the issuer at core gateway, identity-service, policy-service and delegated quick-actions, including its per-step checks. Main user/role/PIM/OAuth consoles and human OIDC role activation are canonical; independent credentials/passwords, legacy PIM/group/recording/emergency and provisioning backends are not yet consolidated or retired. Current role and OAuth-client snapshots are fenced against actual live authority, but legacy non-OIDC and signature-only receiving paths still need consolidation. Federated logout/browser session administration and enforcement at remaining receivers are incomplete.
- Core gateway, policy-service, identity-service and quick-actions enforce a shared API audience and explicit access purpose. OAuth signing now supports protected persistent files and public overlap; secret provisioning/backup, KMS/HSM, remaining deployment manifests, separate device/SAML signing lifecycle, per-resource/tenant audiences and equivalent validation at remaining services still need work. This does not establish complete OAuth production hardening.
- Delegated HTTP orchestration has no durable operation journal/idempotent recovery. Already accepted remote effects, legacy Redis metadata and receiver-specific application/user/credential stores still require canonical reconciliation; an operation UUID or partial-response warning is not that implementation.
- OAuth registration and mandatory direct/flat-group human sign-in assignment are canonical, but app/service-principal ownership and app roles, independent consent/assignment approval and access reviews, nested/dynamic groups, tenant scopes, canonical general group claims, complete inventory pagination, expiring/overlapping/asymmetric application credentials, and consolidation of AD/Kerberos/credential-provider/SAML application stores remain required. This is not complete Entra application governance or vendor fulfillment.

## Verification of the First Increment

- Policy-service: `npm test -- --runInBand` passes, including authorization/ownership checks, production fallback protection, and JWT verification against a local JWKS server using genuinely signed tokens.
- Frontend: `npm test -- --runInBand` passes, including capability-based controls, request payloads, real response envelopes, and rewrite routing contracts.
- Both Compose files pass `docker compose ... config --quiet` with disposable validation values for required secrets; no deployment is started.
- The frontend-wide TypeScript check still reports 18 existing diagnostics. Comparing the original kiosk source with the updated source produces the same diagnostics and no new errors.
- The first increment's Jest API tests mock SQL. The PostgreSQL suite now additionally runs the actual license HTTP app with signed tokens and an independent persistent identity source; a deployed identity provider's role issuance and external vendor provisioning are not verified.

The issuer's existing API suite plus new security and persistence-contract suites exercise actual RSA-signed tokens, client grants, MFA challenge rejection, failed authentication, public S256 PKCE, token purpose, client-bound refresh/device flows, stored grant loading, and license-role consumption. Tests use persistent ephemeral HTTP listeners rather than creating a new listener for each request; repeated full issuer runs pass after removing that source of intermittent connection failures. PostgreSQL and authentication-service are mocked at their boundaries. The local Docker daemon is unavailable, so this increment does not claim a container build or deployment against a real database/authentication service.
